# Microduck Official Update Tracking

**English** · [简体中文](官方更新跟踪.md)

[Back to home](../README.md) · [Docs navigation](README.md) · [Software sources and our changes](软件来源与我们的改动.md)

This entry was filed on **2026-11-14, JST (Asia/Tokyo)**. This page records updates to the official runtime, the training project, the HAT and the emotion assets, and how they affect the Feetech replica. Stable releases, development branches, PRs pending merge and official showcases are labeled separately.

**What is synced here is information and adaptation advice.** An item being recorded here does not mean it has been merged into our source code or system image; once a port is finished, the commit in this repo, test results, image version and the scope of real-hardware verification are added separately. The machine-readable comparison baseline is in [upstream-status.json](upstream-status.json).

## What matters most this time

- **The latest official stable release is `daemon-v0.21.1 / API61`.** Released Nov 13, 19:53:07 JST (UTC `2026-11-13T10:53:07Z`), pinned at `094d4bb4af31ec181156e1ea772437fdd7a55978`. It includes NFC text compatibility, a panel button that controls time-limited Wi-Fi SSH, and restored defaults for head smoothing. The official team reports that it passed the update engine CI; the full stable bundle has not yet been tested on the robot. [Stable release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.21.1) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd...094d4bb4af31ec181156e1ea772437fdd7a55978)
- **Official OS 1.4.2 ships with daemon 0.21.1 and policy set v10.** The Beta and Face Board v1 images enable `autod` and `mapd`. After a system slot update, maps must be rebuilt. Main branch dev1800 comes from the same source as the stable daemon release. [Official OS release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/releases/tag/microduck-os-v1.4.2)
- **The replica repo's Zero 3W RC4 is public.** The Studio home page adds servo and IMU diagnostics, and `robotd` is raised to local control.51/API44. The physical card and phone audio recording still await the acceptance check. [Replica RC4](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/tag/microduck-replica-image-zero3w-20261112-rc4)
- **Laser dot following** has been merged into the main branch and is off by default. When enabled, it searches at 1 Hz and follows at 15 Hz by default. Upstream observed it mistakenly following bright red objects on FaceBoardV1. For the Feetech build, check camera calibration, false detections, action takeover and stopping on signal loss first. [Main branch increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/7a5e193abd3464ea2d8f6ba184ad5d7fac3dd460...9259844672fcb92477509a6e3fe240f53109db17)
- **This project's GitHub Latest currently provides the 20261027 image**. 20261104 is still a stable image, keeping 0.15.1/control.48/API43, and includes IMU recalibration verification, hotspot recovery and the previously preinstalled features. The companion 2026.11.4 software is still a test release. For the Nov 4 image, first boot from a new card, real network switching with the hotspot, walking and getting up after a fall await hardware acceptance checks. [Default download](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/latest) · [20261104 stable image](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/tag/microduck-replica-image-20261104) · [Companion test software](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/tag/microduck-software-v2026.11.4)
- **This project's latest stable software is [2026.10.3101](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/tag/microduck-software-v2026.10.3101)**, i.e. the Oct 31 revision 01: it fixes selection, leader lines and stale cache for servos 32/34 in the 3D servo view, and keeps the mapping, camera zoom and SSH guidance from the 31 release. The native program bytes are still 0.15.1/API42, and the Oct 27 image was not rebuilt; this is separate from the standalone 18.2 migration, and the new gamepad driver still awaits a hardware acceptance check.
- **Camera**: 18.8 limits the MPP encoder's pending frames to 2 by default, preventing a particular Rockchip plugin from exhausting the capture buffers, and shows the stopped state; the third encoding stream is still unverified. The report from the author's board came from 18.7 plus an environment variable, not from the final 18.8 or from this project's acceptance check. Feetech camera calibration and the existing preview state were not confirmed in this round.
- **Actions and sounds**: the recorded pad prototype entered 18.8 via [#542](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/542): 13 expression/sound sets, LB/RB/Y mappings, and holding Select for 2 seconds to rest / 4 seconds to request shutdown; the functional added/removed lines across 47 files and the 26 asset blobs match the old dd3. Feetech amplitude, action mutual exclusion and PCM usage still need review before adoption; this project has not ported it yet.
- **Model management**: there is a new proposal for layered loading of the community policy switch; this project's standalone 18.2 already has a layered implementation based on Params validation, and the semantics for handling a bad user layer need to be kept. Model import dry runs and phase skills are still proposals pending merge.
- **ToF**: #580 proposes documenting that the value is the horizontal distance from the sensor origin, per the current implementation, while #546 proposes changing the value to distance from the torso axis; both are pending merge. Feetech external navigation needs to check mounting geometry, coordinate frames and stop thresholds. [#580](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/580) · [#546](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/546)

This comparison gives no count or duration. The 40 stops and 10 minutes recorded previously still refer to the 10 cm simulated platform. The proposal's pinned source is 816ef17cd3fe36599051a866e3efd392ce2c3dbd, API61, still pending merge. Real stopping at table edges with no echo, on reflective floors and with Feetech HD-1910 still needs a priority acceptance check. The changes in this round concern only simulation evidence; camera, model management, training and sound are unchanged. Upstream replaced the simulation attachment at 20:39:25 JST (UTC 2026-11-14T11:39:25Z), and the old link no longer works. ### Nov 14, 19:22 check: ToF proposal to stop at drop-offs that return an echo

#600 was opened at 18:29:50 JST (UTC 2026-11-14T09:29:50Z) and updated at 19:01:52 (UTC 2026-11-14T10:01:52Z). Pinned source `816ef17cd3fe36599051a866e3efd392ce2c3dbd`, committed UTC 2026-11-14T09:40:39Z. Compared with daemon 0.21.1 it adds 2 commits and 6 files, API61. It is still pending merge, and no corresponding development package appears in the current tags or release list. [Proposal](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/600) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/094d4bb4af31ec181156e1ea772437fdd7a55978...816ef17cd3fe36599051a866e3efd392ce2c3dbd)

ToF reprojection marks valid echoes that point far enough downward and fall below the estimated ground as a drop-off. Autonomous obstacle avoidance requires drop-off evidence in two consecutive frames, and prefers ground observations from the same column or from earlier to locate the edge. A stop is triggered when a confirmed drop-off lies in the forward path and cannot be located. robotctl marks drop-offs in red. No-echo readings with status 255 still use the original clear-space handling. The author reports running for 10 minutes on a 10 cm high simulated platform and stopping at the edge 40 times; table edges with no echo and reflective floors still await verification on real hardware. [Pinned design](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/816ef17cd3fe36599051a866e3efd392ce2c3dbd/docs/design/autonomous.md) · [Pinned obstacle avoidance implementation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/816ef17cd3fe36599051a866e3efd392ce2c3dbd/autod/src/obstacle.rs)

Before Feetech HD-1910 enables autonomous actions, high-priority verification covers the actual ToF mounting geometry, stopping distance, manual takeover and no-echo scenarios. The proposal keeps the existing servo driver, camera, model management, training and sound. The official stable daemon 0.21.1, OS 1.4.2, the other tracked repos and the official blog all remain as previously recorded. The replica candidate does not yet include this logic. [Stable release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.21.1) · [Official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/)

### Nov 14, 01:05 check: replica 0.21.1 candidate and spare-card image recorded after the fact

This round was verified at UTC 2026-11-13T16:05:00Z. The four official default branches, stable daemon 0.21.1/API61, OS 1.4.2, the tracked development packages and PR states all remain at the previous baseline. The official blog index has no new posts. [Stable source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/094d4bb4af31ec181156e1ea772437fdd7a55978) · [Main branch development package](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-main) · [Official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/)

**The local Feetech runtime candidate** was committed at 00:56:29 JST (UTC `2026-11-13T15:56:29Z`) as `70bc940cf0fe3b3cede0ccc81c1d962418540713`, canonical Git tree `4ae57ba30e31d284e0f1ded8f44ff2e158ebcf0d`. The port is based on the official `daemon-v0.21.1` and brings in HD‑1910 control, gamepad, model loading and raw diagnostics from the standalone 0.18.2 production commit `d870d68fa5fe16a349eaacc906a9a748608445d5`. The local API62 adds replica extensions, whereas the API62 in official proposal #449 defines phase skills. Existing Linux logs record 1849 regular regression tests passing and 69 ignored by default, of which 59 pass when run explicitly with ORT 1.28.0. Media and Bluetooth pass build checks. The ARM64 `robotd`, `padd-feetech` and `updaterd` pass version queries under QEMU, and Unix IPC with a simulated `robotd` verified the commit identity, health and status stream. The candidate stays on a separate local branch; the signed companion bundle, image integration and hardware acceptance check are still to be done. [Official 0.21.1](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.21.1) · [Phase skills proposal](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/449)

**The Zero 3W spare-card image** is built from local commit `7d3a279af30275436d5aefe98d96131a4014bbaa`, committed UTC `2026-11-13T06:44:03Z` (15:44:03 JST). On top of RC4's control.51/API44 it adds camera probing at boot and ToF service startup. The existing offline records have rechecked the archive, the camera startup conditions, and that `tofd` still runs and reports itself unavailable when the bus is missing. The spare-card image is not yet public; booting from a physical card, camera picture, ToF distance and motion all await verification. High priority: bring RC4's bus read-order saving and readback and IMU mounting orientation verification and stability hold into the new version, and integrate Studio, the gamepad, companion signing and rollback. The new Feetech candidate disables laser following, autonomous soccer and autonomous mode; before enabling them, run acceptance checks on camera mounting and ToF stopping first. For model management, verify identity validation and loading first; actions and sounds await bench and hardware acceptance checks. There was no training or weight replacement in this round.

### Nov 13, 20:41 check: stable daemon 0.21.1 and OS 1.4.2 released

daemon 0.21.1 was released at 19:53:07 JST (UTC `2026-11-13T10:53:07Z`); the stable tag, source `094d4bb4af31ec181156e1ea772437fdd7a55978`, main branch dev1800 and both manifests point to the same commit (committed UTC `2026-11-13T10:41:15Z`). Compared with stable 0.21.0 it adds 8 commits and 16 files, and is still API61. The 8 attachments include the stable channel manifest, the version manifest and the bootloader signature. The byte digests of both manifests and the package metadata have been verified. The official team published directly from CI, skipping the staging and promotion jobs; there is no hardware verification record yet for the full stable bundle. [Stable release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.21.1) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd...094d4bb4af31ec181156e1ea772437fdd7a55978) · [Release job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/43411018805/job/131837213182)

The earlier main branch changes #590, #591 and #593 now ship with the stable release. NFC MIME text written by a phone can trigger the existing pairing or mode functions, a button on compatible panels can control time-limited Wi-Fi SSH, and the `head_alpha` default shared by head and torso is restored to 0.2. #592's alternative patch remains closed without merging. Before Feetech HD-1910 adopts this, medium priority to check NFC card reading, gamepad takeover and head response. Camera capture and training have no direct changes, and sound keeps the existing prompts. [#590](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/590) · [#591](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/591) · [#593](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/593)

Yocto #134 was merged at 20:07:24 (UTC `2026-11-13T11:07:24Z`), adding 4 commits and 5 files over 1.4.1. The recipe is pinned to daemon 0.21.1, assigns dedicated users to `autod` and `mapd` and enables their services, and makes the build fail if a known service unit is missing from the install. The official notes say map files are stored inside the system slot, so maps must be rebuilt after an OS upgrade. ToF mapping, stopping, takeover and map persistence are high-priority verification items before the replica adopts this. [Official OS release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/releases/tag/microduck-os-v1.4.2) · [Pinned OS diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/compare/5451d13f768c8ca974777fca011c2cdfeadc4924...635c41113cf129504c7e8ae1a7a896e0e4f94153) · [Daemon recipe](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/blob/635c41113cf129504c7e8ae1a7a896e0e4f94153/meta-microduck/recipes-microduck/microduck-daemon/microduck-daemon_0.21.1.bb)

### Nov 13, 19:40 check: NFC compatibility and panel button merged

#590 was merged at 19:24:56 JST (UTC `2026-11-13T10:24:56Z`). NDEF MIME `text/plain` is now read as text in the parser shared by Type 2 and Type 5, tolerating case differences and a charset parameter. Pairing cards and mode cards written by some phones can therefore be recognized, and on-demand card reading still only displays the content. #592 was closed without merging at 19:21:21 (UTC `2026-11-13T10:21:21Z`); its hardware test records belong to that proposal's own patch. The #590 author reports 53 NFC unit tests passing; physical-card verification of the merged patch is still to be confirmed. ### Nov 13, 18:40 check: head smoothing and panel button development packages

Main branch dev1795 was released at 18:41:13 JST (UTC `2026-11-13T09:41:13Z`), pinned at `4cede10a95483bf0b40598531725424631b23c61`/API61; stable 0.21.0 still points to `d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd`. This main branch update adds 1 commit and 2 files: the `head_alpha` default read at startup is restored from 0.1 to 0.2, which affects smoothing of head and torso posture. The author explains that 0.1 felt too soft on real hardware; the joystick curve and the 10 rad/s target speed limit are kept. Before the Feetech build adopts this, medium priority to check head travel, manual response, camera aiming and torso posture. Five kinds of NFC cards can switch between walking, roller skating, laser tracking, soccer and autonomous modes. API61's `nfc.read` can wait in `robotctl` or `duckctl` and read a card's UID and NDEF text or URI; reading a card does not trigger pairing or a mode switch. Card writing supports NTAG and compatible Type 5; MIFARE Classic is recognized and refused for reading and writing. The official team advises updating the whole set of daemons together, since an old API59 `robotd` rejects the newly added `hello.client` and `robot.head.alpha`. The stable package includes `autod` and `mapd`, which both stay idle while autonomy is off. [Pinned card read/write notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd/docs/robot/nfc-tags.md) · [Pinned protocol](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) · [Pinned package requirements](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd/deploy/updater.toml)

Actions include retrying getting up after a fall, head joystick speed control, autonomous soccer and gamepad takeover. Entering autonomous soccer plays a fixed prompt; on exit, the same prompt plays followed about 350 ms later by another sound, both from the existing sound library. The camera is used to recognize the laser dot and the purple ball, and ToF plus foot odometry are used for obstacle avoidance and on-demand mapping. Roller skating is officially pinned to the v10 swizzle, and the training repo has no new commits. Before Feetech HD-1910 adopts this, verify servo posture and retries, gamepad takeover, card-triggered actions, camera recognition and ToF stopping first; model mapping and prompt tone timing are checked separately. The replica has not integrated this yet. [Pinned actions and sounds](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd/robotd/src/main.rs) · [Pinned mapping design](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd/docs/design/mapping-design.md) · [Pinned model sources](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2d8eab1d90f880ab7469c8edd205e42576667bd/docs/design/policy-channel-design.md)

### Nov 13, 14:45 check: retry for getting up after a fall merged into the main branch

**Main branch development build.** [#587](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/587) merged `a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925` at 14:03:37 JST (UTC `2026-11-13T05:03:37Z`). The proposal's final source is `837f1d67310d27e02a7e05f361459a355a2a3108` (committed UTC `2026-11-13T11:52:21Z`); the proposal's commit time is later than the merge time GitHub records, so both original values are kept; the main branch commit is UTC `2026-11-13T05:03:36Z`. dev1783 was released at 14:06:49 (UTC `2026-11-13T05:06:49Z`); the tag and the verified manifests point to the same SHA, with 16 attachments, and the protocol is still API57. Relative to dev1780 there are 3 commits and 5 files in total, and the PR patch matches the main branch increment. The stable daemon is still 0.20.1/API55, and the official OS is still 1.4.1. The training, HAT and expression repos and the official blog have no substantive changes. [Main branch pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/886b62dd792a3e9e958507cd5d9dd5a984f1aa20...a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925) · [dev1783 release job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/43369496285/job/131702753716)

**Actions and configuration.** `limp_fall`, enabled by default, monitors getting up after the policy takes over. If the policy has actually been driving for a full 3 seconds and the existing upright criterion (within about 26° for 200 ms continuously) is still not met, control is handed back to the standing policy through the existing posture transition. By default it retries at most 3 times; setting it to 0 disables the monitoring. After the retries are exhausted, it exits the policy and holds the initial posture and servo torque; the policy can resume automatically once fresh IMU data shows the robot upright and still for a full 1 second, and pressing Start twice also exits this state. Disabling the policy, a skill taking over, sitting down or shutting down all end the monitoring. The 5 changed files are the [runtime logic](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925/robotd/src/main.rs), [default parameters](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925/robotd-params/src/lib.rs), [parameter registry](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925/robotd-params/src/registry.rs), [deployment config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925/deploy/robotd.toml) and [design notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5239df21fdf212fbad6b3b8b5582c4e42506925/docs/design/robotd-design.md).

**Impact on the replica.** This is handled by `robotd`, unlike the earlier `autod` prototype, which retried once, then finally released torque and played a call-for-help prompt. The author reports get-up, hold and recovery checks on the official robot. The replica has not integrated this yet; before Feetech HD-1910 adopts it, high priority to check the IMU, posture travel, holding stiffness and gamepad takeover. Camera, ToF, model weights and management, training and sound have no direct changes.

**Two API59 development packages.** `autonomous-map` dev1752 was released at 19:12:50 JST (UTC `2026-11-12T10:12:50Z`), pinned at `79ed15ec1ebd395c57b4230c3b100293c7d373fa`, 3 commits and 3 files ahead of dev1748. `autonomous-revive` dev1754 was released at 19:18:08 (UTC `2026-11-12T10:18:08Z`), pinned at `b40ebd9994ee72ebbadf8e1429e7f668929eda4b`, 7 commits and 8 files ahead of dev1749. Each package has 16 attachments. The current revive already contains the current map, plus 1 extra commit and 3 files. The source commit times GitHub returns, UTC `2026-11-12T17:08:00Z` and `2026-11-12T17:13:23Z` respectively, are both later than the corresponding release fields. [map increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/e46245940a16130dc71139ecbcf04ef89c98bac4...79ed15ec1ebd395c57b4230c3b100293c7d373fa) · [revive increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/7ab727737663463f0156ab18e6e21c4df45d1924...b40ebd9994ee72ebbadf8e1429e7f668929eda4b) · [Pinned diff between the two packages](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/79ed15ec1ebd395c57b4230c3b100293c7d373fa...b40ebd9994ee72ebbadf8e1429e7f668929eda4b)

**Actions and sounds.** In both packages, `autod` confirms getting up by 1 second of continuous upright posture. If the robot is still down after 5 seconds, it cuts the policy, waits 2 seconds to return to the initial posture, and tries once more. If that times out again, it releases torque and plays the existing call-for-help prompt tone. Gamepad takeover or the policy being turned off externally ends the monitoring. Before Feetech HD-1910 adopts this, check retries, torque release, posture limits and takeover first. Low priority for the current delivery. [Pinned get-up state machine](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/79ed15ec1ebd395c57b4230c3b100293c7d373fa/autod/src/getup.rs) · [Pinned action wiring](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/79ed15ec1ebd395c57b4230c3b100293c7d373fa/autod/src/main.rs)

**ToF and localization.** revive detects backing up through odometry and uses the map to infer walls behind the robot. When backing up near a wall it stops, and only moves forward if the front ToF is fresh and clear; without a valid map it only logs the backing up. `sat` and `bumped` trigger one nearby scan, avoiding the lost-localization flow. Before adoption, check ToF mounting and map error first. There are no camera capture, model management or training updates this round, and the official stable release and the replica repo's RC4 remain as already recorded. [Pinned autonomy logic](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b40ebd9994ee72ebbadf8e1429e7f668929eda4b/autod/src/brain.rs) · [Pinned design notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b40ebd9994ee72ebbadf8e1429e7f668929eda4b/docs/design/autonomous.md)

### Nov 12: turning on Wi-Fi SSH over BLE withdrawn, NFC and roller skating development packages updated

**Track 0.20.1 first.** #574 was created at 16:49:04 JST (UTC `2026-11-12T07:49:04Z`), and dev1727 was released at 16:52:24 (UTC `2026-11-12T07:52:24Z`), pinned at `1516a488ec0502d2c66530745dc238ec8d5ea499`. #574 was merged at 16:57:02 (UTC `2026-11-12T07:57:02Z`); the main branch is pinned at `7fba12ed048d0d3a1c306ba8a2787a1a407b9974`, 3 commits and 11 files relative to 0.20.0. It removes `system.openWifiSsh` and `duckctl ssh-wifi`, and keeps the authenticated console bridge, gateway logs and the old pairing cache filtering. Upstream's reason for the withdrawal is that SSH on the shipping image uses a shared high-privilege account. The API rises to 55, and the old method is rejected. The dev1727 tag and release API both return 404, and the last source has been kept. 0.20.1 staging was released at 16:57:20 (UTC `2026-11-12T07:57:20Z`), and dev1728 from the same source at 17:00:58 (UTC `2026-11-12T08:00:58Z`), with 16 attachments. At the time of checking, staging had 0 attachments and the stable release was still 0.20.0. If the Feetech build follows this access path, authorization and recovery boundaries are listed as high priority. [#570](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/570) · [Pinned merge diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/e0c7369d5ac60f3548f957861c355d140e735e21...e7c1cab77bf6bb9d13fc575b6552aa5c3fc4eaf1) · [dev1703 last source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/52947b900207d46e9d7572d2807bec9a7eeebd15) · [dev1712 release job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/43158891768/job/130986957257)

**#572 pending merge and dev1714.** The PR was created at 14:25:48 (UTC `2026-11-12T05:25:48Z`), and the development package was released at 14:44:28 (UTC `2026-11-12T05:44:28Z`) with 16 attachments. Both are pinned at `4fb1faa99117fad038cbe4efeb9252f46ed21481`, 4 commits and 14 files ahead of the main branch. The gateway raises the log level for authentication failures, unreachable media and full sessions. `duckctl open` bridges to the authenticated gateway through local loopback, and the WebSocket checks the current session's token and the page Origin. Scanning filters out cached devices whose advertisements were not received in the current round. The new `system.openWifiSsh` requests, over authenticated BLE, that Wi-Fi SSH be opened until the next reboot; it requires the image to have the corresponding switch, and the remote media route refuses the request. This API54 extension is independent of soccer, flashlight brightness and phase skills. The author checked scanning on real hardware; the full console and opening SSH over BLE await on-board verification. Feetech camera and control access are listed as medium priority; if Wi-Fi SSH is adopted, authorization, exposure scope and the recovery path are high priority to check. ToF, model management, training, action skills, servos and sound assets have no direct changes. ### Nov 12, 13:49: NFC photo posture and brightness adjustments

The photo tag dev1711 was released at 13:49:13 JST (UTC `2026-11-12T04:49:13Z`), with 16 attachments, pinned at `5112e87999b8c94ffd7cafffb30a561eae4f104c`. Relative to dev1704 it is 5 commits and 11 files, keeping 0.19.0/flashlight brightness API54. It brings in dev1705's withdrawal of Classic NDEF reading, keeping Type 2, Type 5 and ISO 14443-4 e-paper triggers. The photo prototype's changes are still on a development branch. [Pinned increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/ff5d073066d5315407454cf5661113286305d726...5112e87999b8c94ffd7cafffb30a561eae4f104c) · [Development package](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-nfc-photo-tag) · [Upstream release job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/43156039056/job/130979916525)

A robot that is already sitting takes the photo in its sitting posture. After a standing shot it waits 1 second before requesting to sit down, then opens its mouth. If sitting down is refused, it can still wait standing. In the torso coordinate frame, the head-raise target is set 1 m ahead, at a height of 0.75 m when standing and 0.95 m when sitting. Countdown brightness changes to 3%, the shutter flash to 20%, and the wait to release the e-paper is shortened to 1.2 seconds. Both the normal completion path and the path where no e-paper arrives actively return to center. JPEGs are still saved locally, the prompt tones are kept, and writing to the e-paper is not yet implemented. Low priority for the current Feetech delivery; before adoption, check head and mouth limits, framing and exposure, sitting/standing stability, gamepad takeover and stopping first. This round has no direct ToF, model management, training or sound asset changes. The three upstream CI jobs pass on the pinned head; time synchronization of the Feetech camera and ToF is listed as medium priority, and the receiver plugins, FEC and browser behavior still need to be checked. [Pinned increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/f66ed71637d879288d8a23dbf066ab732be49019...831fc107f0536cfa78cbe3c679116ce4268fef5d) · [Last known Classic source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808) · [dev1705 release job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/43149422358/job/130956279058)

**NFC gamepad pairing.** #571 was created at 12:43:59 (UTC `2026-11-12T03:43:59Z`) and is still pending merge. dev1707 was released at 12:43:50 (UTC `2026-11-12T03:43:50Z`), also at `ae343360ee9210d10cd41a456ca7ecca3294f3ee`/0.19.0/API53, 3 commits and 5 files relative to the main branch. A tag can carry the gamepad's MAC; it first does a targeted search for 8 seconds and falls back to generic pairing only on receiving `NotFound`. If the gamepad is found but bonding fails, the failure is reported directly, and an already connected gamepad still takes precedence. An invalid address is handled as generic pairing. It reuses the existing `pad.pair` and confirmation tone. The author reports 42 NFC tests passing; physical cards and gamepads await on-board verification. If the Feetech build adopts NFC pairing, it is listed as medium priority, focusing on multi-gamepad fallback and protection of the connected gamepad. ### Nov 11, 16:34: laser following local scan proposal

#568 was created at 16:34:24 JST (UTC `2026-11-11T07:34:24Z`), pinned at `6afda03e74e96bdbbef130165117cc83eee88bf2`, 1 commit and 3 files relative to the main branch. The source is still 0.19.0/API53; the proposal is pending merge, and no matching dev build was found among the releases checked. [PR](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/568) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/ef666aada8a7e945cbbeefbf1577a091b8a8294b...6afda03e74e96bdbbef130165117cc83eee88bf2)

When tracking has a previous spot, the candidate search first looks within four times the configured radius around it, about 192×192 pixels by default. A result is accepted only if it passes the filters and its center is no more than twice the radius from the previous spot; otherwise the same frame falls back to a full-frame search. The next scan after a full second has elapsed uses the full frame, so a higher-scoring target outside the window may have to wait until that scan to take over. For a local scan, `peak` takes whichever scores higher: the current local value or the most recent cached full-frame value. Whole-frame copying and the default search/follow rates keep the existing path. [Scan implementation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6afda03e74e96bdbbef130165117cc83eee88bf2/mediad/src/laser.rs) · [Parameter notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6afda03e74e96bdbbef130165117cc83eee88bf2/deploy/robotd.toml)

In a 720p scan benchmark on a Mac, the author reports 19.5 µs for the local scan and 474 µs for the full frame. The FaceBoardV1 CPU data comes from the old implementation. The author estimates this change's gain from it; it still needs to be measured on the board. The upstream check, coverage, and board jobs for the pinned commit succeeded. Camera performance optimization is medium priority; before enabling following on Feetech, check first false detections, target switching, and stopping. Motion control keeps using the existing vision input; no direct changes to ToF, model management, training, or sound. [Author's benchmark](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/568) · [Upstream jobs](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/42980481017/job/130389476446)

[Dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-ball-mode) · [Relative to dev1692](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/f66ed71637d879288d8a23dbf066ab732be49019...36ed22c0424d2e2b060ea81baaa54fefcc995ca8) · [Soccer protocol](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/36ed22c0424d2e2b060ea81baaa54fefcc995ca8/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) · [Phase-skill diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/9259844672fcb92477509a6e3fe240f53109db17...225d887389cde26b1be918eea0b05dfa1179c794)

The soccer tag requests switching to walking and enables manual soccer. In this mode LB/RB become left- and right-foot kicks, and D-pad down starts autonomous play. During autonomous play, a new button request exits to manual and that press is consumed; holding a stick temporarily takes over motion. Soccer and laser following turn each other off through `robotd`. Autonomous control uses the camera to detect a purple ball, combines it with odometry projection and short-term target memory, and after searching, approaching, and aligning, requests the existing kick skill. The vision thresholds are still initial values; the acknowledgment of a mode or skill request and the actual motion should be verified separately. dev1692 was published at 13:51:19 JST (UTC `2026-11-11T04:51:19Z`), with 16 assets, actually at `f66ed71637d879288d8a23dbf066ab732be49019`/0.19.0/API53. [Dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-nfc-mode-tags) · [Relative to main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/ef666aada8a7e945cbbeefbf1577a091b8a8294b...f66ed71637d879288d8a23dbf066ab732be49019) · [Relative to dev1690](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808...f66ed71637d879288d8a23dbf066ab732be49019)

The laser tag first requests `walk`, and only after that is granted does it request turning on following. If the second step fails, the already switched walking mode is kept. The walking and skating tags first request turning off following; if that request fails it is logged and the mode switch still proceeds, so a log line saying the mode switch succeeded does not confirm that following is off. The laser acknowledgment from `robotd` means the request was submitted; camera detection, policy loading, and actual motion still need to be confirmed separately. Pairing records take precedence; the soccer and autonomous tags still only give a prompt and write a log. dev1690 was published at 13:30:17 JST (UTC `2026-11-11T04:30:17Z`), with 16 assets; the tag actually points to `59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808`. Relative to the main branch `ef666aada8a7e945cbbeefbf1577a091b8a8294b` it adds 2 commits and 9 files; the source is 0.19.0/API53. It is currently classified as a standalone development prototype; no matching proposal was found among the PRs checked. The official stable release is still 0.19.0/`590e07a`. [Dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-nfc-mifare-classic) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/ef666aada8a7e945cbbeefbf1577a091b8a8294b...59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808)

The ST25R100 identifies MIFARE Classic from the card's select response and reads formatted NDEF according to the card directory, covering 1K cards and the first 15 data sectors of 4K cards. The current implementation is read-only. The CLRC663 USB reader reports Classic cards as unreadable. When the directory is unreadable or the reader does not support the card, this is reported only once per tap. The second commit fixes the byte order of the NDEF application identifier in the directory and adds a regression case based on a real card directory sample. [Card-reading implementation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808/nfc/src/classic.rs) · [Tap handling](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808/nfc/src/main.rs) · [Upstream pairing notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808/docs/robot/pair-a-gamepad.md)

The new card type reuses the existing gamepad pairing entry, the connected-gamepad check, and sound feedback. If the Feetech design adopts the ST25R100, this can serve as a medium-priority reference for extending card types; current delivery priority is low. No direct changes to the camera, ToF, model management, training, action policies, or sound assets. The upstream build and release jobs for the pinned commit succeeded; the author notes it has not yet been verified with the on-board reader. The regression case based on the card directory sample covers parsing only; real-card reading, dual antennas, and full pairing still await an acceptance check. [Release job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/42959127567/job/130322843874) · [Pairing logic](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/59e6e31bf771d94cd34274fdf88f5cc493092808/nfc/src/pairing.rs)

A `Stuck` version that is still running is kept, and a later successful record can clear the old result. ### Nov 10, 20:28: EEPROM setup code dev build

#563 was created at 19:59:01 JST (UTC `2026-11-10T10:59:01Z`), and dev1677 was published at 20:28:03 (UTC `2026-11-10T11:28:03Z`) with 16 assets, actually at `ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099`. Relative to the #556 draft head `d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09` it is 3 commits and 13 files, still pending merge. The source keeps the draft's 0.18.9/API51. The main branch dev build dev1674 is still API53, and the stable release is still 0.18.8. [#563](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/563) · [Dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-setup-code-from-eeprom) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09...ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099)

The proposal adds an `md1` setup code to the ID EEPROM of the beta and FaceBoardV1. The original 18-character label serial number is kept, byte 18 is a separator, followed by the board's initial name and an independently generated 32-byte key. The factory QR code includes the label serial number. When exporting before startup, the EEPROM is preferred, and the setup ID is checked against the initial name derived from the local SoC. Only when there is no chip or no setup code does it try the development-board key file; a corrupted or mismatched code refuses that fallback. The install tool also refuses an EEPROM that already has a setup code or cannot be read. [Layout and parsing](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099/robotd-params/src/label.rs) · [Credential selection and installation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099/robotctl/src/setup_code.rs)

Before `configd` starts, the valid key is written to `/run/credstore`. When there is no valid code, the export tries to clean up old credentials; errors are logged and the configuration service still starts. When the default service has no key imported, BLE takes the original PIN path and the media gateway exits. While the label serial number is still valid, the label PIN can still be used. The gateway gains an install section that the daemon install flow can enable; the Yocto image still needs to be checked separately. The existing explicit key-file parameter continues to work. [Configuration service](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099/configd/systemd/configd.service) · [BLE entry](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099/btd/src/main.rs) · [Gateway unit](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099/duck-gateway/systemd/duck-gateway.service)

The author reports the relevant crate tests and clippy, noting that it has not yet been tried on a board. The upstream build and release records have been checked. For the Feetech design, check first the EEPROM and board identity, client compatibility with QR codes that carry the serial number, the PIN fallback, the camera and control entry points, and upgrade rollback. No direct changes to servo drivers, camera capture, ToF, model management, training, action policies, or sound; this project has not integrated it yet. [Upstream job](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/actions/runs/42807031937/job/129800890893) · [Factory plan](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/ac535e98e88cf3195227a0f8b9cabf9ab398a099/docs/design/factory-provisioning.md)

### Nov 10, 17:40: training repo updates the v7 turning recipe and simulation benchmark

This round observed, at `2026-11-10 17:40:11` JST (UTC `2026-11-10T08:40:11.875976+00:00`), that `develop` had moved to `0daab160d3fb1c2cbaba16a4a19052f36709dd9e`, 4 commits and 5 files relative to the old head. The raw Git commit time `2026-11-10T14:58:01Z` is later than the observation time and is kept as in the original metadata. The training repo has not published a GitHub release. [Pinned changes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/compare/bae39f5900b318f7e87fc485c30a31213cfa14eb...0daab160d3fb1c2cbaba16a4a19052f36709dd9e)

The current recipe keeps the anchor yaw remap, the fine-grained turning reward, and in-place turning sampling starting from 0.2 rad/s, and turns off v8's joint-mask teacher and wide initial poses. When the SO(3) initial-pose probability is zero, the corresponding random draw is skipped. A new headless simulation benchmark uses flat ground plus backlash, fixed commands, and a batched policy graph to cover velocity and attitude, getting up, and walking under push disturbances. Mean velocity is computed only over environments that have not fallen. [Training config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/0daab160d3fb1c2cbaba16a4a19052f36709dd9e/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/microduck_velstand_env_cfg.py) · [Random initial pose](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/0daab160d3fb1c2cbaba16a4a19052f36709dd9e/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/mdp.py) · [Benchmark source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/0daab160d3fb1c2cbaba16a4a19052f36709dd9e/scripts/velstand_bench.py)

The official Hub v9 `velstand.onnx` has the same LFS digest and size as v7 and still corresponds to `j4i6yoq2@1250`. The author separately records `n3fg9pgq@2000`, trained further from it: in simulation, the response to in-place ±1 rad/s commands improves from v7's +0.48/−0.41 to +0.88/−0.94 rad/s, and the end-of-episode upright rate in the strongest push group is 91% versus 98%. The author says turning on real hardware has improved. The public metadata request for its candidate Hub repo returns 401; the weight source and digest await independent confirmation. Training and model lineage are rated a high-priority reference; the observations, actions, normalization, backlash, and recovery for the Feetech HD-1910 need separate verification. No corresponding changes to the camera, ToF, or sound. [Upstream evaluation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/0daab160d3fb1c2cbaba16a4a19052f36709dd9e/docs/velstand_policy.md) · [Official v7 metadata](https://huggingface.co/api/models/pollen-robotics/microduck-policies/tree/f745f1089051c64f9fd1fc2a3b1ef67feec14703) · [Official v9 metadata](https://huggingface.co/api/models/pollen-robotics/microduck-policies/tree/8dc9feca069ca4e89f0e307d405cf9e1f0bb1ee9)

The stable release is still 0.18.8. [#556 draft](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/556) · [Dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-codex/secure-media-and-ble-defaults) · [Pinned increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/c820cace8de390010d06602f92c5b368d037be7f...d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09)

#559 writes BlueZ's `JustWorksRepairing = always` into the board-side configuration, so that after a phone forgets the device it can re-establish link encryption; this takes effect the next time the Bluetooth service starts. On FaceBoardV1 with an iPhone, the author first reproduced the old pairing failure, then verified re-pairing with the new configuration and a reboot. The application layer still uses the original PIN or secure-mode credentials. The corresponding Yocto #109 was merged into the #108 security draft at 23:22:40 on Nov 9 (UTC `2026-11-09T14:22:40Z`). It sets the same policy in the image recipe and makes the build fail when the expected configuration is missing. #108 is still based on the stable 0.18.8; the secure daemon, the offline camera entry, and upgrade rollback need separate verification. [#559](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/559) · [Board-side script](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09/scripts/setup-board.sh) · [Yocto #109](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/pull/109) · [Yocto #108](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/pull/108)

#560 makes `btd`, the gateway, and `robotctl` use the same `duck-xxxx` initial name derived from the SoC serial number; changing the display name does not change the setup ID. Without a valid serial number the secure entry point refuses to start. The install tool reads the hex representation of a 32-byte key from standard input, checks the declared data mount, refuses by default to overwrite an old key, and stages the key with 0600 permissions before replacing it atomically. A display command can print the setup code and a terminal QR code. An old `--secure-robot-id` configuration makes `btd` fail to start; if the old ID saved on the phone does not match, it needs to be rescanned. [#560](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/560) · [Installation and QR code](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09/robotctl/src/setup_code.rs) · [Initial name](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09/robotd-params/src/serial.rs) · [Gateway unit](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d2e8d7b880f94ee4cc718e707bf0917225fafb09/duck-gateway/systemd/duck-gateway.service)

The generic `microduck_gamepad_pairing` tag still goes through the existing discovery flow and refuses if the result is still ambiguous after filtering. An NDEF text or URI record can also append, after the marker, whitespace and a hexadecimal address in six colon-separated groups; lowercase addresses are converted to uppercase. Old cards that contain only a MAC still do not trigger, and a record with a malformed address is not downgraded to a generic request. For multi-record tags, the first valid record in order is used. [Tag parsing](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/70d781ed074e299d6a3baaae631505c5d26f9add/nfc/src/ndef.rs) · [Upstream pairing notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/70d781ed074e299d6a3baaae631505c5d26f9add/docs/robot/pair-a-gamepad.md)

`nfcd` passes the specified address through the existing `pad.pair` parameter and reuses `configd`'s address-matching path. If the target is not found, it does not pick a different gamepad. If the state already shows a connected gamepad it skips; one tap makes one attempt, and the chirp and greet before and after pairing reuse the existing sounds. IPC, BlueZ selection, and the card-reader driver are the same as on the main branch. [Request passing](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/70d781ed074e299d6a3baaae631505c5d26f9add/nfc/src/main.rs) · [Tap flow](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/70d781ed074e299d6a3baaae631505c5d26f9add/nfc/src/pairing.rs) · [BlueZ matching](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/70d781ed074e299d6a3baaae631505c5d26f9add/configd/src/bluez.rs)

RF card reading, multi-gamepad pairing, and HID control still await verification on real hardware. Medium priority if a compatible card reader is available; low for the current Feetech image. ### Nov 9, 18:19: impact protection PR adds a ROBOTIS V4 single-servo bench test

#551 updated its description at 18:19:10 JST (UTC `2026-11-09T09:19:10Z`); the head is still `730710b99d909a46770293d296413a4397d78289`, pending merge. The author reports a V4 single-servo session with continuous power and no restart. After register 67 is enabled, writing the brake time, holding current, or current-limit EEPROM leaves the switch on. With torque on and the servo at rest, writing 1→0→1 to 67 showed no change in current, PWM, position, or status, and rewriting 1 while holding changed the position by no more than one encoder count. When hitting the end stop, register 66 reads 0→1→2, and it also reads 0 after 67 is turned off, so a telemetry value of 0 alone cannot prove that protection is enabled. The full-robot `robotd` V4 bus path, writing 67 from 0 to 1 while holding, gear durability, and the Feetech HD-1910 adaptation still await verification. The source is API52; the API51 left over in the PR description is earlier text. Evaluating action protection for ROBOTIS is high priority; a direct port to Feetech is low priority. ### Nov 9, 17:27: laser spot following dev build

`daemon-dev-laser-track` was published at 17:27:52 JST (UTC `2026-11-09T08:27:52Z`); dev1657 has 16 assets, and the tag actually points to `5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d`. The source is 0.18.9/API52, with 4 commits and 29 files relative to `da34a22`. This dev build, impact protection, and phase skills each have their own API52 contract. The stable release is still 0.18.8. [Dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-laser-track) · [Pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/da34a2212ae12eddfd73d0dfbf924f291aaf1dc9...5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d) · [Protocol](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs)

`mediad` picks bright red spots out of the existing UYVY frames, filters them by blob size, and then computes the camera ray using the board-specific intrinsics and mounting orientation. Default search is 3 Hz and following is 15 Hz. Zero3W keeps the quarter-turn mounting orientation, while the GC2093 on the beta and FaceBoardV1 is mounted upright. Detection results go through the local `media.laser`, and the WebRTC console uses `media.laserDot` to show accepted spots as a red circle and size-rejected ones as an amber dashed circle. The existing capture exposure and encoding path stays in use; this project has not yet measured the added frame-processing overhead. [Detection source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/mediad/src/laser.rs) · [Board settings](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/robotd-params/src/board.rs) · [Console source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/mediad/webclient/index.html)

`robotd` uses the measured head joints, IMU attitude, and odometry to project the ray onto an assumed ground plane, reusing the head inverse kinematics and walking intent. The master switch is off by default; once enabled, the head and walking both take part by default, and each can be turned off separately. Non-zero manual input that is still within its validity period takes precedence; once the stick returns to center, laser following can take over again. When the spot is lost, the ray is invalid, or the video service disconnects, the previous target can still be used until 1 second (default) after the last valid observation. The default projection limit is 2.5 m, the target stopping distance is 0.25 m, and the speed limits are 0.25 m/s and 0.8 rad/s. WebRTC is allowed to call `robot.laserTrack`; BLE rejects this command. [Projection and target holding](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/robotd/src/laser.rs) · [Motion takeover](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/robotd/src/main.rs) · [Default parameters](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) · [WebRTC routing](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/mediad/src/route.rs) · [BLE routing](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5064ac4b2e602f909ed64eed928f37b060ad086d/btd/src/route.rs)

For the Feetech HD-1910, camera intrinsics, head mounting and limits, false detections, manual takeover, and stopping on signal loss are all high-priority review items. 0.25 m is the stopping parameter for the follow target; ToF obstacle avoidance and the real stopping distance on hardware need separate verification. The prototype reuses the existing gait model and delivers no changes to model management, training, or sound. This project has not integrated it yet; this round checked the release metadata and the pinned source.

### Nov 9, 09:02: media and BLE security draft

#556 was created at 01:02:20 JST on Nov 9 (UTC `2026-11-08T16:02:20Z`) and is still a draft. The dev build dev1653 was published at 09:02:20 (UTC `2026-11-09T00:02:20Z`) with 16 assets; the tag is actually `c820cace8de390010d06602f92c5b368d037be7f`, matching the draft head. The source is 0.18.9/API51, and access conditions change with the draft. The official stable release is still 0.18.8. [Draft](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/556) · [Pinned changes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/da34a2212ae12eddfd73d0dfbf924f291aaf1dc9...c820cace8de390010d06602f92c5b368d037be7f)

`mediad` binds to 127.0.0.1 by default, so signaling, the console, and snapshots listen only locally. BLE requires paired encryption by default, the BlueZ agent declares `NoInputNoOutput`, and installation still uses the old PIN by default. An optional development mode uses a per-robot 32-byte pre-shared key and Noise-encrypted BLE RPC; an incomplete handshake expires after 15 seconds, and after authentication the session is cleared if there is no inbound request for 60 seconds. The optional gateway listens on 9443 by default, holds up to 8 sessions, has a 10-second handshake limit, and connects to local 8443 after authentication. The gateway ships with the dev build, but the service must be started explicitly. [Media entry](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/c820cace8de390010d06602f92c5b368d037be7f/mediad/src/main.rs) · [BLE session](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/c820cace8de390010d06602f92c5b368d037be7f/btd/src/secure.rs) · [Gateway](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/c820cace8de390010d06602f92c5b368d037be7f/duck-gateway/src/main.rs)

The Android and iPhone setup, camera, and reconnection results recorded by the author come from a beta board and their respective pinned builds. iPhone BLE used the ARM build of `8d999f5`; the diff from that commit to dev1653 is four Markdown files. The data showing deadman triggering at about 0.61 s and the app's forward intent dropping below 0.001 m/s at about 0.92 s came from a holding or fallen state. Walking and braking distance on real hardware, adversarial testing of unpaired GATT, iPhone network loss, and the production image acceptance check still await review. ### Nov 7, 02:57 dev build: robot microphone one-way audio track and per-session video pause

The new [`daemon-dev-mediad-mic-track`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-mediad-mic-track) was initially, at UTC `2026-11-06T15:47:07Z`, dev1618/b8c39/source 18.8/API50, 1 commit and 16 files; currently, at UTC `2026-11-06T17:57:02Z`, it is dev1629/[`36366dc26b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/36366dc26bc741886c6e2459b34d798e7368b8a2)/source 18.8/API51, each with 16 assets. [A follow-up 1 commit and 6 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b8c39b5067a2f3a1099245648066fd56c05cc270...36366dc26bc741886c6e2459b34d798e7368b8a2) adds video voting; overall it is 2 commits and 18 files, not merged into the main branch.

[The robotd microphone tap](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/36366dc26bc741886c6e2459b34d798e7368b8a2/robotd/src/mic.rs) reuses the petting worker's single arecord capture and shares the raw 16 kHz/mono/S16LE bytes through /run/robotd/mic.sock with permissions 0660; listeners that read slowly or write short are dropped. When petting is off, it captures only if there is listener demand; when audio is off, the tap is not served. [mediad](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/36366dc26bc741886c6e2459b34d798e7368b8a2/mediad/src/mic.rs) sends 640 bytes every 20 ms, sends silence when capture is missing, prebuffers 200 ms, drops old data beyond 500 ms, and reconnects after a disconnect. The target Opus voice/24 kbps/complexity 5 is set only if the plugin accepts the properties, so on-board latency or CPU cannot be guaranteed from the constants.

**media.mic is on by default, and connected WebRTC sessions can receive robot-side sound; the page's listen only toggles local audio mute and does not stop capture or transmission.** It is not an intercom, browser microphone input, ASR, or TTS; the meanings of the audio and media switches must be checked separately. The follow-up [media.setVideo](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/36366dc26bc741886c6e2459b34d798e7368b8a2/mediad/src/session.rs) only counts the votes of the WebRTC control sessions: if any of them wants video, it continues; new sessions want it by default; disconnecting withdraws the vote; with no votes it is on to allow negotiation. The rendezvous control lane has no vote and returns accepted=false. accepted only means the vote was counted; on is the global result. The pause valve sits before webrtcsink and requests a keyframe on resume; the camera, raw/detector, audio, and indicator lights keep running. It does not stop the camera or stop room sound from being sent out.

A total of 22 test definitions were added (15 tap/feed/pump, 2 protocol, 4 session, 1 keyframe); without an H264 encoder, the keyframe test can simply print SKIPPED and return. The author's workspace/ARM build and laptop fake-tap test-tone reports for the initial b8 cannot be relabeled as a final full rerun on 363, much less as a robot acceptance check; the high CPU/temperature mentioned later is problem background, not an optimization measured by this project. The sound capture contract and media resources are rated a high-priority review before adoption; this round only read the source and did not listen to audio, open the page, restart, or connect to a robot. Both being API 51 does not prove it matches the main branch's candidate-list contract.

### Nov 7, 00:12 dev build: squat-stand action still lacks a production model

The new [`daemon-dev-crouchstand`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-crouchstand) published dev1616/[`1d193c79fc`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/1d193c79fcd7073f607fbeb492e7648ca14de474) at UTC `2026-11-06T15:12:30Z`, 16 assets, source 18.8/API50, 1 commit and 15 files. It adds an 8th optional crouchstand slot with default X=crouch_toggle; the vx posture flag is 1 for crouch and 0 for rise. It first pauses for 0.5 s with zero twist, crouches in about 1 s, holds until toggled again, then rises in about 1 s; head/body commands remain available. Progress is time-based and does not measure physical stillness; the whole cycle, including the hold, is busy, which affects other actions, automatic reflexes, and the restart gate, but does not amount to removing all Safety protections. Stopping with Start and the sit at shutdown clear the state.

The slot defaults to none, and the [controller](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/1d193c79fcd7073f607fbeb492e7648ca14de474/robotd/src/control.rs) rejects the request when there is no model; v9 does not provide crouchstand either. v9's roller_crouch is a different, phase-encoded skating action and must not be loaded into this posture_flag slot just because the names are similar. Of the 4 new tests, the 3 controller ORT tests are ignored by default and use a feedforward fixture, and only 1 mapping test is normally enabled; there is no corresponding squat-stand verification on real hardware. Holding X no longer re-triggers the daemon's own toggle/ground_pick, while other chained skills still resend; MAX_SKILLS goes from 30 to 29. Although the API is still 50, it also adds Rust field/enum/config and default-mapping contracts. Feetech models, motion amplitude, busy, and fall recovery are rated a high-priority review before adoption; no new weights or training deliverables.

### Nov 6, 22:21 dev build: impact protection adds ROBOTIS V4, not synced with stable 18.8

[`daemon-dev-impact-guard-v3`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-impact-guard-v3) published dev1615 at UTC `2026-11-06T13:21:53Z` with 16 assets, actually at [`e2d3ecedfd`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/e2d3ecedfd22fbfc6817c34a8e94e5cc55557886). [Old d081 → new e2](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/d081d8005394c604e40da09fcbc78d0cd6fb72fe...e2d3ecedfd22fbfc6817c34a8e94e5cc55557886) is only 1 commit and 10 files, adding support for the ROBOTIS custom V4; the source is still **0.18.6/API51**. [Relative to the current 188/4a](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a...e2d3ecedfd22fbfc6817c34a8e94e5cc55557886) it forks from stable 186/98, with 9 historical commits and 12 files on the development side and 23 main branch commits missing; it must not be described as synced with 187/188, or as 9 new features stacked on top of 188. Stable 188 and this project's image do not include it.

The [model constants](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e2d3ecedfd22fbfc6817c34a8e94e5cc55557886/duck-control/src/model.rs) extend the applicable firmware from version 3 to exactly [3,4]; V4 adds RAM register 67 as Impact Guard Enable. The [bus register check](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e2d3ecedfd22fbfc6817c34a8e94e5cc55557886/duck-control/src/bus.rs) caches V4 IDs after reading the firmware; rechecking the same ID first removes the old record and then adds it again according to the new firmware. Both V3 and V4 use the original two settings blocks 40–43 and 56–59, and EEPROM differences are still postponed while torque is on; the R defaults 6/20/400/32767/7/40 and the 1000 mA current limit/Goal Current path are unchanged.

Each `set_torque(true)` first tries to write 67=1 for the cached V4s, then tries to enable torque on all joints one by one; false does not write 67, and daemon startup by itself does not automatically turn on torque. **A failed write to 67 is only recorded in the error list; torque enable is still attempted afterwards, and the Bus errors are aggregated at the end; there is no readback of 67 or atomicity guarantee.** So this only confirms the write order and does not treat "protection is on from the first Start", recovery after brownout/reboot/latched errors, or braking performance as verified conclusions. The code also explicitly does not know whether a reboot, turning torque off, or a latched error clears the switch; the author's description that it defaults to off at power-up/firmware boot cannot replace these acceptance checks.

`bus.impact_guard=false` still writes the old six OFF parameters so that the protection does not trigger; **V4's 67 is still written as 1, rather than using 67=0 to turn it off**. The [original impact_guard telemetry](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e2d3ecedfd22fbfc6817c34a8e94e5cc55557886/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) now also accepts V4 status, while other firmware stays null; the original path that reads register 66 after the target write, and only when there are subscribers, is kept. The API is still 51 with no new fields, but the firmware applicability semantics have changed; the public Rust constant changes from the singular IMPACT_GUARD_FIRMWARE to the plural FIRMWARES and adds ENABLE_FIRMWARE, so external source consumers must check as well.

This increment **has no new test functions**; the existing FakeIo telemetry fixture was changed to one V4, the rest V3, and the mouth 53, which cannot cover real serial writes to 67, torque order/failure, readback, EEPROM postponement, or reset. No new execution log for the final e2 was found, and this round did not run tests. The commit/source comments say that on Nov 6 a V4 bench without protection enabled lost two gear teeth after about 17 impacts; this is the author's old failure observation, **not gear life or a full-robot acceptance check after enabling the new e2**; the earlier lavandiere R walking/shoving results are not relabeled either.

Earlier combined verification or active-tripping descriptions must not be restated as new V4 results; this project has not enabled, ported, or run either branch.

ROBOTIS V4 enable failure/reset/lifetime research is rated a high-priority review before adoption; current Feetech delivery is low priority. Firmware number 4 does not mean other servos have the same capability; the 40–43/56–59/66/67 addresses, 1 A, and the R parameters cannot be used directly on the HD-1910, and there are no conclusions about Feetech drivers or protection performance. ### Nov 6, 20:37 proposal: finiteness of gaze coordinates and the rejection contract

New pending-merge [#549](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/549) is pinned at [`515e0e725a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/515e0e725a45b0da04b6f37ba7f262604e7d0aae), opened at UTC `2026-11-06T11:37:22Z`; branched from official 188/4a, 1 commit, 2 files, source 0.18.8/API50. As of the final check at UTC `2026-11-06T12:04:08Z` it was still unmerged and no corresponding dev release was observed; neither official 188, the main branch nor this project's image contains this fix.

[HeadFk::look_at](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/515e0e725a45b0da04b6f37ba7f262604e7d0aae/kinematics/src/head.rs) first replaces a non-finite neck_pitch with 0; only when the target coordinates contain NaN or infinity does it immediately return a finite [neck,0,0,0] with clamped=true. **When only the neck angle is invalid and the target is finite, it still proceeds with solving and does not guarantee clamped=true**, which differs from the proposal description, which says both cases clamp immediately. The iteration adds finiteness checks on the determinant, the step norm and the candidate angles, and also exits when the step becomes too small; if the final residual is non-finite it sets clamped. The original 30-iteration cap, the damped solver and the model's joint ranges are kept. There is no new calibration and no guarantee about gaze accuracy on real hardware.

The [robot.look entry point](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/515e0e725a45b0da04b6f37ba7f262604e7d0aae/robotd/src/main.rs) returns the existing `INVALID_PARAMS=-32602` before writing the intent if any of x/y/z/neck_pitch is non-finite; finite but unreachable targets still solve for the nearest reachable pose. **API staying at 50 does not mean the rejection contract is unchanged**; the old "Never refused" comment cannot override the newly added rejection path either. The library's finite-pose fallback and the RPC parameter rejection are two layers of behavior and must not be conflated into "the request executed successfully".

2 new test definitions are added: the library test checks clamped and finite joints for three non-finite coordinate examples, while the neck-NaN example only checks for finite angles and has no clamped assertion; the RPC test builds the Rust call directly, dispatches x=NaN, and checks the error code/message and that the default head intent remains finite, without pre-seeding a non-zero sentinel for value-by-value comparison. **It does not go through JSON serialization/parsing, sockets, BLE/WebRTC or the full control loop**, so it cannot be used to claim that a literal NaN is accepted over the wire, nor does it exhaust every field/input combination. The author reports that 31 kinematics and 260 robotd unit tests plus fmt/targeted clippy passed; this round only checked the source and the statements, without re-checking the full logs/environment, re-running, or testing on the board.

The linked [issue548](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/issues/548) and the PR description say a NaN intent would make the whole robot hold continuously via Safety::NotFinite. This round's check of [intent storage](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/robotd/src/intents.rs) confirms it does store values as-is, but the slew actually called by head_ema already filters non-finite targets; the new tests do not reach that loop/Safety. Therefore only the numeric solver and request entry guard are confirmed here, and **the persistent whole-robot freeze and its fix are not presented as independently verified results**.

The input numeric/RPC contract is listed for high-priority pre-adoption review, the head-pointing feature as medium priority, and current image delivery as low priority. Camera impact is limited to IK for pointing the camera axis; there is no new image recognition, video pipeline, lens calibration or preview fix, and no ToF projection/ranging, model management/weights/training, action/skill or sound changes. Feetech HD-1910 drivers, neck geometry/range and tracking on real hardware still need separate acceptance checks.

Previously retired tags keep their history and are not re-reported as failures. This project's Nov 4 0.15.1/control.48/API43 test delivery, the stable 31-r1 / 27th image, and the separate 18.2/API47 production/test identities and original verification boundaries are all kept as they were; the native/companion directory and the 15→18 online migration remain disabled. ### Nov 6, 19:43 / 19:59 proposals: ToF distance reference and zeroing after stop

New pending-merge [#546](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/546) is pinned at [`a42be8dbd5`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a42be8dbd5923cbc509e2539f6c263f3fd01d031), opened at UTC `2026-11-06T10:43:48Z`; [#547](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/547) is pinned at [`264a71f125`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/264a71f125fbc8bfd2983015bddfae6b185dd329), opened at UTC `2026-11-06T10:59:01Z`. Both branch from official 188/4a, each with 1 commit and 1 file, source 0.18.8/API50. At the final check at UTC `2026-11-06T11:09:27Z` both were still unmerged and no corresponding dev release was observed; **neither official 188, the main branch nor this project's delivery contains these two changes**.

546's [actual projection](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a42be8dbd5923cbc509e2539f6c263f3fd01d031/kinematics/src/tof.rs) keeps the sensor-relative horizontal distance for the `MIN_RANGE_M=0.10` near-range noise filter; only `Zone::Hit.range` changes to the gravity-leveled `hypot(r×dir.x+sensor.x, r×dir.y+sensor.y)`, matching the original documentation, which measures horizontal distance from the vertical axis through the torso origin. point is still in torso coordinates, and the floor/too-close classification order, raw readings and driver are unchanged. **The linked [issue430](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/issues/430) states that there is currently no consumer in the repo that reads Hit.range**; the [official monitor](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/robotctl/src/monitor.rs) checked this round does plot using point and displays the raw distance, so this library field fix cannot be described as improving the built-in obstacle avoidance or the current web pages. The issue author's external navigation / MuJoCo 32k-zone results come from the old 0.17.6, not a new run of 546 or a hardware acceptance check.

1 new synthetic-echo test is added for alpha / level pose / default height / near-center, and the original one-meter echo assertion is strengthened; there are no new range values for tilted poses, and no beta or Feetech hardware cases. The PR description gives no execution results, and this round did not run the tests either. **Adaptation inference**: the Hit distance for a front-mounted sensor will increase, so an external consumer that keeps the same stopping threshold may let objects come closer to the sensor; before adoption, the physical outline, mounting pose, FK/IMU and thresholds must be re-checked at high priority, and alpha's offset of about 6–8 cm must not be copied as a general Feetech constant. It is not new SLAM, localization, an obstacle-avoidance controller or a ToF driver; current delivery is low priority.

547's [actual control loop](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/264a71f125fbc8bfd2983015bddfae6b185dd329/robotd/src/main.rs) extracts the original three-axis ramp into `update_twist_ema`, keeping the EMA and the optional per-axis acceleration limit; after the update, the applied twist is set to zero as a whole only if **the effective target on all three axes is exactly zero, all filtered values are finite, and each absolute value is strictly less than 1e-3**. A non-zero target (0.0005 in the test) is not zeroed, and non-finite filtered values do not satisfy the condition either; this is convergence of the decayed tail value, not an immediate stop, torque-off or a change to the deadman timeout. 1e-3 is compared per axis against translational velocity / yaw rate, not measured joint velocity or a combined norm.

The motion flag is still `step.busy || command.twist_magnitude()>0`, and the forced-busy, moving and not-fallen checks of `safeToRestart` have not been removed; scripted actions, returning home, rest at shutdown, limp-fall or pick-up transitions can still report busy. When other conditions allow, this fix can eliminate false walking reports caused by residual commands, but **it does not measure that the robot has physically come to rest, nor does it provide a lock that blocks new commands during restart**. It is listed for high-priority control/update gate review before adoption, and it cannot be used to lift the disablement of the 15→18 online migration or to conclude that Feetech braking is safe.

2 new tests are added that cover **only the helper function**: with initial [0.5,0.2,0.1], per-axis acceleration 1, dt 0.02 and alpha 0.2, it checks that zeroing takes between 0.5 and under 1 second and that the loop takes fewer than 100 ticks; another checks that a small non-zero target is not truncated. The assertion message's "about 35 ticks" is not an exact 35-tick assertion, nor a measured guarantee of about 0.8 seconds; it does not cover the full control_loop, deadman, RPC, updater or the robot, and the description contains no executed results. The PR description/comments explain the roughly 33 seconds as the EMA components underflowing to zero; the linked [issue351](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/issues/351) actually distinguishes **underflow of the sum of squared magnitudes** from subnormal components that are still non-zero, and the [existing twist_magnitude](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/duck-control/src/obs.rs) does indeed sum the squares and then take the square root. Therefore only the numeric derivation for the author's specific parameters is kept here, and the 33 seconds is not described as a measured physical stopping time or a general delay; this round did not reproduce the numbers or run the code.
