### Nov 6, 18:24 merged: camera encoder buffers and stopped state

New [#541](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/541), final [`3372424f7b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/3372424f7b0f4465f6e038f2245e32235322c4e0), PR itself still 0.18.7/API50, 3 commits and 5 files, was merged as 4a at UTC `2026-11-06T09:24:07Z` and went into 18.8. [mediad](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/mediad/src/pipeline.rs) sets `GST_MPP_ENC_MAX_PENDING=2` before gst::init, only when the environment variable is missing; a value the user has already set is kept as is. The plugin class reads it at first initialization, so it also covers the encoder that webrtcsink creates on its own. The problem comes from a specific Rockchip plugin holding the actual capture buffers via DMABuf: the default pending of 16 can exhaust all 8 buffers. It is not a new sensor fault or a calibration change. After the limit, each encoder holds at most 3, so the discovery stream plus one viewing stream uses 6/8; **a third encoder for viewing plus media.stream push was explicitly not tested**, so arbitrary concurrency cannot be guaranteed on this basis.

The [protocol helper](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) computes the age from camera.json's mtime; beyond 5 seconds, [health](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/robotctl/src/main.rs) and [monitor](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/robotctl/src/monitor.rs) show stopped instead of showing the old fps as currently running, and the health JSON gains an optional `camera_stopped_secs`. A future mtime counts as zero age, and missing metadata returns None; WebRTC session reading reuses the age helper, and the pass-through boundary for stale frames / bad files still exists. **API staying at 50 does not mean the CLI JSON extension contract is unchanged**.

The author's on-board results come from beta OS1.1.0, BUILD_ID20261106073050, with **the same variable set via a systemd drop-in on 0.18.7**: capture 30 fps, 1280×720 frames, 30 seconds of single-viewer WebRTC at 30 fps / bufcnt 7–8, and the 7 lost frames were all at startup. This is not the same as the final 18.8 full tree, this project's image or recovery of the existing preview. 2 new test definitions for age / health stopped rendering are added, and the author reports several host checks; platform and concurrency limits still remain. This round only checked the source and statements, without running tests, visiting the web pages or changing services. The camera dependency upgrade is listed for high-priority review before adoption; image quality and Feetech calibration are to be verified separately.

[BlueZ scanning](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/4a763dd893f3982c3a11c136468ccf5d26bcc28a/configd/src/bluez.rs) treats the presence of RSSI as "heard this time", and removes the stale bond for a classified gamepad or explicit MAC that is paired, not connected and currently heard; if removal fails it is still excluded from this round's candidates, and later scans can retry. Candidates are taken only from devices currently heard or connected, cached offline gamepads are no longer falsely reported as Paired, and ambiguity rejections get extra logging. **This is a BlueZ RSSI lifecycle heuristic, not a hardware pairing-mode flag**, and compatibility with LE gamepads that re-advertise is still to be checked; an explicit MAC can skip classification. 4 new Linux pure-logic tests and 1 FakePads offline case are added; the author reports macOS checks / ARM clippy, not yet tested on the board, so the old failure logs must not be treated as acceptance of the fix. Bluetooth re-pairing is listed for high-priority regression review; this round does not remove any local pairings.

### Nov 6, 11:36: recording proposal now tests the real read function and distinguishes end from interruption

Pending-merge [#532](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/532) was updated from 3ac to [`e5c3552893`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/e5c355289352cc9ea42624d4d70bba42c2662c8b), [adding 1 commit and 1 file](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/3ac2cf1f3263b1da74d753ea0ec4ae1b58e3d583...e5c355289352cc9ea42624d4d70bba42c2662c8b); relative to the original a64 it is 2 commits and 2 files, source still 0.18.5/API49, not synced with official 18.6/API50. [worker](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e5c355289352cc9ea42624d4d70bba42c2662c8b/pet-detect/src/worker.rs) extracts read_block from pump, returning real samples or EOF, and makes the recording log distinguish "Eof, read to the end" from "Shutdown, stopped midway", fixing the earlier false report that also wrote "played to the end" on interruption.

Of the 4 recording-related test definitions (9 tests in the worker module in total), the original EOF and loud-sound tests now call **the real read_block**, 1 new test checks that an odd-byte read errors, and the missing-path test is kept. The EOF loop has a cap of 64 iterations and checks total sample count, termination and a repeated EOF; the loud-sound test uses 16 loud frames / 3 quiet frames, fed into SoundSentry through the same conversion. The author says that changing EOF to empty samples or deleting the odd-byte check makes the relevant tests fail, and says local build/test was checked; this round did not re-check the run logs or re-run. **It still does not call worker_loop, read_recording, the full pump or PetHandle, and does not load ONNX**; the missing-file test still only checks File::open, so it cannot be said to cover the real warning / no-retry path. The Shutdown log fix is a source review finding, not a full-thread hardware test.

Input is still only raw 16-bit signed little-endian, mono 16 kHz PCM, with no WAV/MP3 decoding, automatic sample-rate detection or resampling; an odd number of bytes in a single read errors, and leftover bytes are not stitched across reads. Production spawn still constructs the ONNX detector first; the Rust source compatibility boundary where a public PetConfig field is replaced by AudioSource also remains, and the API49 number being unchanged does not mean callers are unaffected. Offline recording verification is listed as a medium-priority reference, and current image delivery as low priority; there is no new self-sound filtering, memory, sound event subscription/state, ASR/TTS, model or training, and this project has not ported or executed this recording path.

### Nov 6, 02:36 merged: final rejection code and own-video recognition for WebRTC updates

Already-recorded [#535](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/535) has final source [`8c058c7c1b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/8c058c7c1be51e8663998c551731904e86d0ae6f), merged as 0fb10f2 at 02:36:19 and then included in 18.6. [Compared with the recorded 830 it adds 2 more commits and 9 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/830fa5e398e92ed988a67b9a2147d6f0381ce858...8c058c7c1be51e8663998c551731904e86d0ae6f); API is still 50, but the contract has changed: a new [`SESSIONS_CONNECTED=16`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/98ce17e4868ba9370713c4d11f3d934e5a1b7687/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) is added. 16 is returned only when the session check fails; earlier failures such as clock, disk or robot state checks still return the original preflight rejection. robotctl maps 16 to REFUSED; clients can use the code to decide whether to suggest retrying with `interrupt_sessions=true`, and should not match only on the error message.

[VideoHolder](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/98ce17e4868ba9370713c4d11f3d934e5a1b7687/mediad/src/relay.rs) lets the rendezvous control lane subtract its own video user **when the same sessionId holds the bridged video**; the lane is no longer treated across the board as a bystander that occupies no media. The guard that replaces/releases video only clears the corresponding holder, so that releasing an old session does not clear a new one. Other users still require explicit permission to interrupt; consumers that are still negotiating ICE may also be counted. The session source is still a snapshot updated roughly every second while the camera has frames; missing/bad files or ones older than 5 seconds still pass, so it is not an atomic connection lease or permission authentication. `os.apply` checks the network clock, and `os.switch` ignores that clock failure; network reconfiguration is still restricted to BLE, and the daemon's apply/rollback/select still do not go over WebRTC. The old private `robot.remoteSessionActive` has been removed, so mixed installations of old components must be checked.

Relative to 830, 1 in-memory VideoHolder test is added and the original Seat/preflight tests are expanded; the full 535 has 6 new test definitions, not new hardware cases. The author's final report is 1,828 passing on the macOS workspace excluding nfc, keeping the platform limits for nfc/robotd clippy, and explicitly states it was not run on the robot; the old 830's 1,827 cannot be relabeled as final source test results. This round did not run tests or visit web pages. The author also says the os-update-anyway branch of microduck-app will provide a "continue after disconnecting" prompt; with the current credentials, the repo, the branch and the PR list all return 404, so **we can only record the author's statement and cannot yet independently confirm that the companion client is usable or merged**. The official release itself does not fill in this acceptance check.

The original daemon-dev-os-update-over-webrtc tag and release both return 404 and are archived as retired after merge, keeping the last actually observed dev1589/830/API50 and the earlier a642 source; we do not guess that it ever published a final 8c package. The final version's error 16 and own-video recognition also differ from the old dev 50 and the other 50 contracts of impact-guard/stress. The update interface and recovery flow are listed for high-priority pre-adoption review; the camera only takes part in the gate via counting, with no new image-quality calibration, HD-1910, ToF, model, training, action or sound implementation.

### Nov 6, 01:17: proposal to fix Spaces control replies split across HTTP chunks

New pending-merge [#537](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/537) is pinned at [`de74b2de6f`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/de74b2de6fcd405298a63a96cdcac414177eb3cf), [1 commit and 3 files relative to c15](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/c15c2377d8a80662405bb5e773351c984ad6cffc...de74b2de6fcd405298a63a96cdcac414177eb3cf): `spaces/shared/wire.py`, a new test file and CI. There are no Rust/protocol changes, and it inherits source 0.18.5/API49; it is not a new daemon release, nor #535's API50 session contract.

Although the [actual client](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/de74b2de6fcd405298a63a96cdcac414177eb3cf/spaces/shared/wire.py) keeps the WsConsumer name, it uses rendezvous's HTTP `POST /send` and SSE `GET /events`; it does not go through WebRTC and does not carry video or continuous remote control. The old implementation decoded UTF-8 per network chunk and replaced CRLF; if a character or `\r\n` happened to straddle chunks, it could produce garbled text, leave a complete reply in the buffer, or merge it with the next event and drop it. The new implementation keeps the incremental decoder state across chunks, concatenates the text first and then replaces CRLF in one pass, and still only dispatches complete double-newline events. An incomplete trailing event is still not dispatched; nothing new is added for authentication, sending requests, disconnect handling or confirmation that the robot executed the command.

[7 test methods](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/de74b2de6fcd405298a63a96cdcac414177eb3cf/spaces/shared/test_wire.py) check complete LF/CRLF, newlines/Unicode split across chunks, every two-chunk split for both line endings, multiple replies plus an incomplete trailing event, and a real requests response iterator reading single-byte HTTP chunks. The downstream of the real reply handler is still a Mock RPC, and the last test only starts a local fake HTTP server; no real rendezvous, account, browser or robot is involved. The [CI step](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/de74b2de6fcd405298a63a96cdcac414177eb3cf/.github/workflows/ci.yml) creates a Python venv before the Rust checks, installs the existing requests dependency and runs this unittest, without pinning the requests version; adding it to the workflow does not confirm that the CI run succeeded.

The author reports 7 passing on macOS/Python 3.12.15/requests 2.34.2, 5 of which failed on the original main, and reports the same CI discovery command plus YAML/Shell and diff checks; the author explicitly did not run the full Rust suite locally, leaving Linux and whole-repo checks to CI. **This tracking only read the source and the result statements; it did not run these tests or download executables.**

Control reply reliability is listed as a medium-priority pre-adoption reference, and current image delivery as low priority. The impact on action control is limited to the reply parsing/display path, and it cannot be used to claim that commands were executed by the robot; there are no changes to HD-1910 drivers, camera image quality/calibration, ToF, model management, training, weights, motion execution or sound implementation. Default-branch commits of the four repos, official 18.5/54 and its assets, existing PRs/dev tags, model metadata and the [official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) had no other substantive changes; this project's Nov 4 test image/software, the stable 31-r1 / 27th image and the separate 18.2 records are all kept, and this round did not visit or change the existing web pages.

### Nov 6, 00:32 archived: WebRTC update proposal adds session checks, dev build bumped to API50

New pending-merge [#535](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/535) was first opened on Nov 5, 23:58 JST; this round's first read was a642/API49, 2 commits and 3 files, only opening up `os.apply`/`os.switch`. The follow-up commit after 00:26 changed it to [`830fa5e398`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/830fa5e398e92ed988a67b9a2147d6f0381ce858), [3 commits and 19 files relative to c15](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/c15c2377d8a80662405bb5e773351c984ad6cffc...830fa5e398e92ed988a67b9a2147d6f0381ce858), source still 0.18.5, **API bumped to 50 and `robot.remoteSessionActive` removed**. The new [`daemon-dev-os-update-over-webrtc`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-os-update-over-webrtc) also changed during this round from the 00:20 dev1588/a642/API49 to the 00:32 dev1589/830/API50, each with 16 assets; the initial source is kept in the JSON history, and the contracts of the two packages must not be conflated. It has not yet been merged into the main branch or included in official 18.5.

[Session reading](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/830fa5e398e92ed988a67b9a2147d6f0381ce858/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) now uses the consumers count in the existing `/run/mediad/camera.json`; it is published roughly every second while the camera has frames, and writing was changed to a side file followed by rename. The [shared preflight](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/830fa5e398e92ed988a67b9a2147d6f0381ce858/updater/src/preflight.rs) rejects updates when there are WebRTC users, and an explicit `interrupt_sessions=true` lets it continue; BLE/duckctl's daemon apply and periodic updates are also affected. [Seat handling](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/830fa5e398e92ed988a67b9a2147d6f0381ce858/mediad/src/session.rs) subtracts the data channel that sent the request itself; the rendezvous control lane is not counted as a media user. When the snapshot shows no other users, mediad automatically adds that flag only for the two OS requests, to avoid rejecting itself; if there are other users, the original request is kept.

**This is a snapshot check that can let things through, not a connection lease or authentication.** A missing, unreadable, bad-JSON or older-than-5-seconds file passes as unknown/nobody; there is no atomic interlock against the camera stalling or sessions added after the reading. The LAN permission boundary is unchanged, so it cannot be described as "guaranteed not to interrupt others". updaterd still holds the engine lock and checks that the robot can be restarted; the networked `os.apply` checks the clock, and only the slot-switching `os.switch` deliberately ignores clock failures. It still calls the existing image script / RAUC, and a missing script still yields NOT_SUPPORTED; no system update capability is added for this project. `net.connect/forget` is still restricted to BLE, and daemon `update.apply/rollback/select` is still refused over WebRTC; reconnection and `os.status/last_result` handling are still the client's responsibility, and no reconnection acceptance check is added.

A read-only review found 5 new test definitions: stats-file freshness, alone/multi-user judgment, OS request rewriting and forwarding to a fake updaterd; the original route/preflight tests were also adjusted, and these are not hardware disconnect/restart tests. For the final 830 the author reports 1,827 passing on the macOS workspace excluding nfc, clippy on the four specified crates and fmt, notes that nfc cannot be built on macOS and that robotd clippy has dead-code issues in unchanged Linux code, and that it was not run on the robot; the initial a642's mediad 97 passing is not relabeled as the final full-tree result. This project did not re-run anything. This item is listed for **high-priority interface/recovery contract review**: the new API50 also differs from the other API50 contracts of impact-guard/stress; this project's 15 series, the separate 18.2/API47 and the existing web pages have not been upgraded because of this. The camera only takes part in the update gate via the stats file; no image quality/calibration, HD-1910, ToF, model, training, action or sound features are added.

### Nov 6, 00:17: roller-skating height reward corrected, author's hardware report listed separately

New pending-merge [#91](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/pull/91) in the training repo is pinned at [`9bc1ae7afe`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/commit/9bc1ae7afe332b12b839030221d501d3f07ff8f0), 1 commit and 2 files relative to bae. The [roller-skating recipe](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/9bc1ae7afe332b12b839030221d501d3f07ff8f0/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/microduck_velocity_rollers_env_cfg.py) only changes the `com_height_target` band from `[0.0935,0.1235]` to `[0.125,0.145]` meters, with the weight still 2. The comment says the roller-skating model's HOME upright torso z is about 0.1386 m and the spawn z is 0.1335–0.1435; the upper limit of the old legged recipe is about 15 mm lower, which actually rewards crouching. These measurements are the author's report; this round did not re-measure them in simulation.

The actual [reward function](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/9bc1ae7afe332b12b839030221d501d3f07ff8f0/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/mdp.py) reads the root link height minus the terrain origin z; **it is not a mass-weighted CoM measurement or a servo position / safety limit**. Inside the band it adds 1, outside the band it is the negative squared distance, then multiplied by weight 2, so at this magnitude it mainly acts as a reward gate. Swizzle inherits this factory; other models still need to be measured separately, and the numbers cannot be copied directly to the Feetech robot.

The [new test file](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/9bc1ae7afe332b12b839030221d501d3f07ff8f0/tests/test_com_height_band.py) has 3 parameterized test functions that explicitly list only rollers and ice_skate, at most 6 cases, and skip if a module/scene is missing; although the file header claims auto-discovery, **it does not actually enumerate swizzle and does not automatically cover all derived tasks**. Using HOME PD in CPU MuJoCo, they take the median torso height over an upright window of 25–100 steps, and check that tilt is under 5°, that the upright and spawn ranges are inside the band, and that the crouch margin is 5–30 mm; they do not directly test the reward output or a training policy doing closed-loop roller skating.

The author reports 277 passed / 4 skipped, and says W&B run 598l1d3r, task Mjlab-Velocity-Swizzle-MicroDuck, model_6000 used this code and has done roller skating, head control and recovery to standing on real hardware. **This is an upstream author's hardware report**: this round did not obtain the original run / checkpoint hashes or the full training provenance, so it cannot be written up as an acceptance check of this project's Feetech build or the separate 18.2. No new weights were published, downloaded, trained or swapped in. Pre-training review for roller skating / swizzle is listed as medium-high priority, and current walking image delivery as low priority; camera, ToF, model management, sound and runtime action code have no changes from this item.

[`daemon-dev-pad-mapping`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-pad-mapping) moved to dev1586/[`dd3ea7643b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/dd3ea7643b3fc5314dae08bed34564e1fc30061f), source still 0.18.5/API49, 16 assets. [mapper](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/dd3ea7643b3fc5314dae08bed34564e1fc30061f/pad-map/src/mapper.rs) changes Select's RobotRest from "on release between two and four seconds" to **firing immediately once held for two seconds**, and fires RobotShutdown at four seconds; both times it sets the local up to false, and nothing is sent on release anymore. The existing robotd loop keeps a shutdown request received during rest pending and shuts down after rest finishes; this is the request order in the source, and does not mean the robot has sat down within two seconds or that power is physically cut at four seconds. The general Hold algorithm is unchanged, and only comments in its file were changed; 1 new mapper test checks the calls fired at the thresholds and no repeat on release, with no servo/daemon/HD-1910 timing acceptance check.

This control timing is listed for medium-high-priority re-review before adoption; it is separate from the old ground-grasp-x personal "release to restart servos / three seconds to shut down" mapping, and this project's buttons have not changed along with it. Camera, ToF, model management, training and new audio implementation have nothing new from this change.

The 4 parameterized test functions of the [existing regression file](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/2f46ee991466d39a473143211027f3af1e6e9ee1/tests/test_rough_terrain_cfg.py) expand to 26 cases, covering registration, train/play creation order, nested configuration isolation and the Backlash task; this time they are merely kept along with the original patch, not 26 tests newly added after the sync. ### Nov 5, 20:09: pad-mapping prototype adds expression actions and sounds

The new [`daemon-dev-pad-mapping`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-pad-mapping) is 0.18.5-dev.1584, actual [`30a0e3bb60`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/30a0e3bb601e46913c42358a056104ee27f36500), source 0.18.5/API49; [relative to c15 it is 4 commits and 45 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/c15c2377d8a80662405bb5e773351c984ad6cffc...30a0e3bb601e46913c42358a056104ee27f36500), including one merge, not 45 features. It has not been merged into the main branch or officially released; being API49 as well does not imply that official 54 or this project already has these skills.

The [player](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/30a0e3bb601e46913c42358a056104ee27f36500/robotd/src/emotion.rs) reads 13 JSON/WAV pairs from the release package's emotions directory; `[emotions] dir`, defaulting to `/var/lib/robot/emotions`, overrides items with the same name and appends new ones, and is read at startup. Without a WAV an item still loads as a silent action, and bad JSON is skipped per item; it only checks that keyframe values are finite and that times are strictly increasing, mouth is clamped to 0–1, and there is no dedicated head/torso hardware range validation. It only executes head, body pose, mouth and a one-time `skill=sit`; twist/soften/relax are ignored, so not all control semantics of the original assets are implemented. The 13 keyframe files were read; devastated contains sit; `play_dead` is excluded by the generation script, and curious additionally has a neck/pitch motion removed. The generation script uses an asset clone supplied by the caller and does not pin an upstream revision; this round only pins this branch's commit, and the unchanged default commit of the asset repo is not written up as a "new version" of the assets. The 13 WAVs only had their Git file identity checked; they were not downloaded, listened to or decoded.

The [default mapping](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/30a0e3bb601e46913c42358a056104ee27f36500/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) changes to LB=yes, RB=no, Y=excited, with A/B still stand-up/sit and pick-up, and X left empty; existing explicit configuration still takes precedence. `robot.do` and the subscribe skill list include the expression names, with a policy skill of the same name taking precedence; queued accepted does not mean it has played, and it may be dropped when busy. During playback it overrides that tick's head/torso/mouth and sets zero velocity, and after it finishes it returns to the client's latest command; this cannot be called restoring the pose from before it started, or continuously locking the gamepad. A sit-down keyframe may leave the robot in a sitting pose.

Control ownership and sound still have boundaries to check: the [loop](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/30a0e3bb601e46913c42358a056104ee27f36500/robotd/src/main.rs) only checks controller.busy at the start, and later ordinary skill requests can still enter the original handling path, so it cannot be summarized as all actions being mutually exclusive throughout; when the natural timeline ends it only clears playing, with no corresponding stop-audio call. `play_file` skips when ride occupies the PCM, other one-shot sounds may also preempt it, and the animation may still continue; the source does not establish audio/visual arrival/completion confirmation. 9 new test definitions cover interpolation, loading, the sit marker, the queue and the packaging entry point, etc.; they were only read, with no full control loop / sound card / Feetech action acceptance check. The branch workflow adding directory packaging does not mean this project's ARM full package or image already carries the assets.

Action and sound research is listed as a medium-high-priority reference; before merging, Feetech joint directions, pose amplitude, policy observations and control ownership must be adapted, and the asset provenance and actual packaging must be checked. There are no new model weights, training, ASR/TTS, camera or ToF features; this round did not change the button mapping, swap models or install this package.

### Nov 5, 19:46: face-board-v1 name and compatibility proposal

Pending-merge [#533](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/533) is pinned at [`f12becac5a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/f12becac5aaca58f7ec42cb518c146ee54bd2883); the companion [`daemon-dev-board-face-board-v1`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-board-face-board-v1) is dev1583 at the same actual SHA, source 0.18.5/API49. The GitHub target base is now c15, but the actual fork point is a64, [with only 1 commit and 10 files of new functionality](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/a64ee692eebfce46a35c50b77705249752df9273...f12becac5aaca58f7ec42cb518c146ee54bd2883); relative to c15 it is 1 ahead and 5 behind, not including the subsequent sysroot/roller-skating merges.

[Board::FaceBoardV1](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/f12becac5aaca58f7ec42cb518c146ee54bd2883/robotd-params/src/board.rs) adds the label face-board-v1, hw_rev=3, identified by the first match in the compatible list; a normal device tree puts the dedicated string before the generic beta string. It reuses the beta board: GC2093/0°, body IMU mounting, robotd reading the head IMU, SPI for ToF and NFC, and the CPU serial number; explicit ToF link parameters still take precedence. This is the name/routing for the official beta board paired with the V1.0 face panel; **it adds no LED driver or button logic, and is not a new hardware adaptation for the HD-1910 HAT or a new camera calibration**. hw_rev=3 is not an upgrade pass that any replica board can fill in.

An old daemon that does not recognize the new enum will reject the configuration and fail to start; just renaming the board has compatibility consequences. The companion pending-merge [Yocto #95](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/pull/95), pinned at [`e396144ecd`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/commit/e396144ecd86d9e894c5e0547e688c8c5dda880f), 1 commit and 4 files, checks whether the seeded robotd binary contains the name when a [board] template exists; if not, it falls back to beta, errors if there is no binary, and still skips the declaration if there is no board table. This is a build-time heuristic based on string presence, not a runtime handshake or full capability verification; the RAUC machine compatibility identifiers of the face panel and beta are also still different, so images cannot be cross-flashed on this basis. The author reports three fake-rootfs cases and oelint, and explicitly did not run bitbake; this project did not run these checks. Neither proposal has been released, and this round did not build or flash an image.

Official board support is tracked at medium priority, and current Feetech delivery has low applicability priority; camera/ToF only reuse the existing beta selection, and models, training, actions and sound have nothing new from this item. The daemon's existing configuration/routing tests were extended, with no new test functions or hardware verification by this project.

### Nov 5, 19:01: impact guard adds an upstream hardware report, defaults unchanged

`daemon-dev-impact-guard-v3` was updated to 0.18.5-dev.1579, actual [`dc48e98b0a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/dc48e98b0a8d6ddd81b620be8dda1283f64214c0), still source 0.18.5/API50. [The 2 commits relative to c619 only change 1 document](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/c619ac934cbce29e2f7d2063ec78a3d1dc858ddf...dc48e98b0a8d6ddd81b620be8dda1283f64214c0); **no protection logic or tests were added, the code default is still the original factory configuration, and the R configuration was not adopted automatically.**

The [updated document](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/dc48e98b0a8d6ddd81b620be8dda1283f64214c0/docs/robot/cheatsheet.md) reports that with the same ONNX, 0 can walk while 5/5a/5b still fall; braking triggers about 6 times per second while walking, and short-circuit braking of the load-bearing knee followed by soft hold weakens support. The author says that in comparisons on lavandiere, 6/7 had no falls in 20 push disturbances combined, while the combinations that kept hold current or 50 ms braking fell 6 times in 31; the new R is v_min=6rad/s, brake=20ms, decel=400rad/s², hold=32767mA (hold disabled), v_fast=7rad/s, PWM=40%, and it is reported to have walked for more than 13 minutes and withstood 32 push disturbances.

These are **upstream hardware reports** in a pinned document; this round has no raw run logs / complete trial provenance list to re-check independently, so they cannot count as tests by this project. R is the author's recommendation for their ROBOTIS V3 robot, and 32767 is that firmware parameter's usage, not a Feetech allowable current; it does not cover HD-1910 registers, units, braking/load-bearing characteristics or gear-life acceptance. Applicability priority for current Feetech delivery is still low, and servo protection research needs high-priority hardware review; a "documentation-only dev build" must not be written up as a new safety protection having taken effect.

### Nov 5, 18:57: sound detection recording input proposal, limited test scope

Newly opened pending-merge [#532](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/532), pinned at [`3ac2cf1f32`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/3ac2cf1f3263b1da74d753ea0ec4ae1b58e3d583), is 1 commit and 2 files relative to base a64, source 0.18.5/API49, with no separate dev release or main-branch merge yet. It replaces the public Rust field `PetConfig.alsa_device` with `source: AudioSource::{Alsa,File}`, so library consumers need to adjust; **the IPC contract and API number being unchanged does not mean the Rust public interface is unchanged.** robotd's only call site still passes Alsa, and no TOML/CLI recording input is added.

The [worker implementation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/3ac2cf1f3263b1da74d753ea0ec4ae1b58e3d583/pet-detect/src/worker.rs) has File use 16-bit little-endian, mono, 16 kHz raw PCM, with `pump(impl Read)` reusing the inference and SoundSentry path; a file ends at EOF and does not enter the microphone backoff restart, while a microphone EOF is still handled as stream failure. There is no WAV/MP3 decoding, sample-rate detection or resampling; reading an odd number of bytes still errors. The full `PetHandle::spawn` still loads the model/ORT first, so it cannot be summarized as the real recording worker not needing a model. The end log also records Shutdown as playback finished, and the log itself does not prove that all bytes were processed.

The 3 new test definitions respectively only do a BufReader read-through, manually feed recording samples into SoundSentry, and assert that File::open fails; **none of them call the real worker_loop/read_recording/pump/PetHandle**, so they do not directly cover the file not restarting, the number of warnings or the full model chain. The author reports local build/test and that modifying the volume fixture makes them fail; this round only read it, did not re-run it, and did not conclude from it that recordings are equivalent to the full live-microphone chain.

This item is listed as a medium-priority reference for offline audio verification; it does not include self-played sound masking, memory, sound event notifications / state fields, ASR/TTS or new models/training data. The existing way of consuming ambient sound events is unchanged; it does not affect HD-1910, camera, ToF or the motion policy, and has not gone into this project's image. This round, default-branch commits of the other three repos, model metadata, other existing PRs/dev tags and the 3 official blog posts had no substantive changes; this project's Nov 4 test software/image assets, the stable 31-r1 / 27th image and the separate 18.2 records remain as they were. Only documentation was maintained, without running tests, building, installing or operating web pages / services / the robot.

### Nov 5 check: the impact guard is a ROBOTIS V3 firmware experiment

The new dev tag [`daemon-dev-impact-guard-v3`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-impact-guard-v3) is 0.18.5-dev.1572, actual [`c619ac934c`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/c619ac934cbce29e2f7d2063ec78a3d1dc858ddf), source 0.18.5/API50, [4 commits and 12 files relative to official 54](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/54e0789635ae28120be9ea0dd51750a494ab50ac...c619ac934cbce29e2f7d2063ec78a3d1dc858ddf). It has not been merged into the main branch / official package, and no firmware was flashed to the robot on this basis.

The [Dynamixel implementation](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/c619ac934cbce29e2f7d2063ec78a3d1dc858ddf/duck-control/src/bus.rs) sets Current Limit to 1000 mA during the startup check and also writes Goal Current when it changes; it writes two dedicated register blocks only for firmware_version=3, and if torque is on, defers this to the next torque-off startup. `bus.impact_guard` defaults to true, and the 6 parameters default to 1 rad/s, 50 ms, 400 rad/s², 500 mA, 9 rad/s, 50% PWM; turning it off simulates "current limit only" through thresholds that cannot trigger, rather than switching back to the old firmware. The current units/registers and protection capabilities in the code all target the XL330 / custom V3, and **are not 1A protection for the HD-1910 or automatic Feetech adaptation**; the position mode of the stock firmware cannot be guaranteed to give the same current-limiting effect on this basis either.

[State collection](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/c619ac934cbce29e2f7d2063ec78a3d1dc858ddf/robotd/src/main.rs), when there is any state subscriber and a sensor sample, additionally does a synchronous read of register 66 after the target is written; non-V3 joints are filled with null, and the whole group is omitted on a read failure, so null values cannot be counted as not triggered. The extra bus read still takes loop time; the source estimates about 1 ms, which is not a measured HD-1910 budget. `RobotIo` defaults to None, so our own Feetech backend does not get this feature.

The [experiment notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/c619ac934cbce29e2f7d2063ec78a3d1dc858ddf/docs/robot/cheatsheet.md) specifically point out that stepping on the ground may trigger the protection and a sustained soft hold, making the knees/hips go soft while bearing load; 5b "keep braking / PWM ramp-down, remove hold" is a configuration to be compared, not a default recommended by this project or a walking acceptance check. The 6 new software test definitions were only read. Current Feetech delivery is listed as low applicability priority, and servo protection research has a high hardware adaptation bar; there are no camera, ToF, model/training or sound asset updates.

### Nov 5 check: stress record preservation and network time sync prototype

The original [`daemon-dev-stress-test`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test) advanced to 0.18.5-dev.1577, actual [`a5b69ed072`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a5b69ed07230517cf5fe5802f9302d84488227ac), API50; **the separate stress-test-v2 is still 619 / map API50 and has not been replaced**. Old 854→a5 is 20 commits and 55 files, of which 17 commits come from the already-checked main branch, 1 merge and 2 log/clock feature commits; there is no new map or rear continuous obstacle avoidance scheme this time, and the original ToF and motion limits still apply.

The [record layer](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5b69ed07230517cf5fe5802f9302d84488227ac/robotctl/src/runlog.rs) changes the stress default directory to `/var/lib/robot/stress`, targeting the official Yocto data partition; the old `/var/lib/stress` is not migrated, and this project's partitions cannot be assumed to be the same. meta is replaced via a temporary file, fsync and rename; reopening the CSV trims the leftover tail after the last newline and reuses the old header; this cannot recover lost data or prove that rows are never lost on brownout. Each segment adds a revision/release/slot/OS/policy summary, configuration and notes, and tries its best to save old boot logs still in the journal into the corresponding run; this is not the same as automatically backing up all history or a single continuous endurance test.

The new [`stress verify`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5b69ed07230517cf5fe5802f9302d84488227ac/robotctl/src/stress/verify.rs) checks meta, missing/empty files, text, CSV column counts, time going backwards and timer resets that segments can explain; it allows cross-thread backward steps within 1 second, and missing fields / non-numeric values are not comprehensively rejected. The plotter warns about and drops rows with mismatched column counts and keeps plotting; **a warning does not mean a pass, and no warning is not proof of cryptographic integrity or action success**. The append repair only trims the tail at the last newline and cannot be summarized as a full CSV semantic repair.

Before starting stress it first waits indefinitely for time sync; the boot unit's 180-second timeout is changed to infinity, and robotd's wait budget is counted separately afterwards. After 20 seconds it also reads the **plaintext HTTP Date** from Baidu/Tencent/Google/HF in turn; if any two differ by ≤5 seconds it calls clock_settime with the later value and leaves the source in `/run`. This changes the system time and depends on the network, permissions and the trustworthiness of the sources; two sites agreeing is not authenticated time, and an existing marker is not continuously re-checked. Offline it may never start, so it cannot be described as offline automatic testing having been fixed. This round did not access these time sync endpoints or execute this logic; the logging approach is listed as a medium-priority reference, and automatic time sync needs an independent review before hooking it up is considered.

This round consisted entirely of source/metadata reading and document verification, with no running of upstream / this project's tests, builds, installs, image publishing, or robot or web page / service operations. Default-branch commits of RL, HAT and emotions, the related PRs and model metadata and the [3 official blog posts](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) are unchanged; the identity and assets of this repo's Nov 4 public test software/image are also unchanged, and the stable 31-r1 / 27th image and the separate 18.2 production/test records are kept. Isolated verification of each piece of software, the ARM full package, image and hardware acceptance continue to be recorded separately.

### Nov 4, 20:38 check: new proposal with automatic rollback off by default

Pending-merge [#526](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/526) is currently pinned at [`aff2ce4cc0`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/aff2ce4cc02a7e23077c215b6ff7af70700d9f99), currently 3 PR commits / 18 files relative to main-branch 9db; the [1 commit and 2 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b64def7b73708ae0edc1cb2456925a2ad0643706...3362dbe3f8d0e599460b987ce67318c1004fbf14) appended at 20:46 only make the real robotd gate test fixture explicitly turn on auto_rollback and restore the rollback log that the board-test script matches; they add no test definitions and do not change the default policy, nor do they prove CI has passed. dev.1560, released first at 20:42, was b64; the 20:51 [auto-rollback-opt-in dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-auto-rollback-opt-in) was updated to 0.18.4-dev.1561, actual `3362dbe3f8d0e599460b987ce67318c1004fbf14`, filling in this CI fix; the old b64 snapshot has been kept. At 20:57 it also merged the already-reviewed main-branch 9db; dev.1563/aff already includes #525, source 0.18.4/API48; this time the 8 final blobs relative to 3362 are all identical to the main branch, with no additional automatic rollback logic. The earlier b64 and 3362/API47 snapshots have been kept; the current API48 contains both the missing-parts diagnostics and the rollback switch, and must not be conflated with main-branch 48, which only has the missing-parts diagnostics, or with trip 48. **The official main branch and official 0.18.4 have not yet included the automatic rollback switch, and the same API number does not mean the same recovery contract.**

[update_gate.auto_rollback](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b64def7b73708ae0edc1cb2456925a2ad0643706/robotd-params/src/lib.rs#L2657) defaults to false, and the editor has a toggle; updaterd reads the robotd.toml pointed to by robotd_config (default /etc/robot/robotd.toml) at decision time, so no restart is needed. When off, a failed target activated by apply/select/manual rollback stays on the current version, reports Stuck and prompts for manual recovery; the trial-run marker is cleared and the log records Aborted rather than RolledBack, so it is not added to known_bad. **Staying on a failed version is not a health pass, nor is it a recovery.** The [apply branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b64def7b73708ae0edc1cb2456925a2ad0643706/updater/src/engine.rs#L1169) wraps the whole post_swap, so in practice it also affects post-install hooks, service actions and new updater self-check failures; the scope goes beyond robot.health.

When an unconfirmed trial run reaches the boot count threshold, turning it off also only records a single Stuck and clears the trial run, without automatically falling back to previous/golden. [boot-check](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b64def7b73708ae0edc1cb2456925a2ad0643706/scripts/robot-boot-check#L220), after determining a failed unit, when off and not in dry-run only prompts for robot-rescue --reboot and exits 2; dry-run still returns the original failure verdict / exit 3 first for the OS slot gate to read, so it cannot be equated with a master switch for recovery at all layers. The manual rollback code and the signature/digest/compatibility checks are still kept.

There are also parsing boundaries to check: the [Rust layering](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b64def7b73708ae0edc1cb2456925a2ad0643706/robotd-params/src/lib.rs#L120) discards a user layer with syntax errors, so a base-layer true may remain in effect, and it cannot be summarized as "a broken user configuration always turns it off"; boot-check takes a literal true line by line with awk, which is not the same parser as the TOML merge, so the two may diverge with a bad user layer. This is a static source review; no reproduction was executed. The 7 new test definitions were only read; the original rollback fixtures explicitly turn on the switch, and the author explicitly states that two Docker-dependent scripts were not run locally, so they cannot be recorded as passed by CI / this project. **It is listed for high-priority contract review before hooking up the upstream updater; this round does not change this project's update or recovery defaults.** This item has no HD-1910 driver, camera, ToF, model/training, motion policy or sound asset updates.

| Full beta image | microduck-replica-20261104.img.xz, 755,670,732 bytes; SHA256 `f9970ec551bcddbeeae9748b0ce38f36450fa1497618c07920723849f975aa78` | Preinstalls the matching software and the native / gamepad services, and includes the hotspot recovery script fix; fresh-card boot, real network switching and motion still need verification. |
| Companion beta software | 2026.11.4.software-update.tar, 9,676,800 bytes; SHA256 `b6e8d90896fd1aaeb4a6288e226ba7228a1fe7d738a79c9dcde7407dfadad211` | Updates the IMU / web console etc. and separately confirms the companion driver bundle; uploading the software package does not replace the system network-setup scripts on an old card, and the bootstrap archive is not a web-console upload package. |

The [release source notes](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/blob/f215b658a4f24b669cf664b6381a559c61472009/docs/更新说明-20261104.md) focus on resampling the IMU in the HOME stance, checking the upright / forward-lean direction, and showing the correction amount; also preinstalled are the gamepad mapping, camera zoom, SSH, music / voice / action and 32/34 diagram fixes made after the 27th. The default is the legacy quaternion; it does not flash the MCU automatically and carries no fixed mounting orientation / personal calibration. This does not justify claiming that the new ToF, upstream models / training, or walking / getting-up improvements have been integrated. Handling of the old 31 driver transaction is described in the [native update notes](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/blob/f215b658a4f24b669cf664b6381a559c61472009/docs/native-update-20261104.md).

This check only read and verified the small assets' SHA256, the Git tags / commits and the provenance relationships: the software manifest, provenance and image component records match each other, and the asset metadata / same-name digests agree; **the software archive / image bodies were not downloaded, signatures were not verified, and nothing was installed, flashed or re-tested**. The [release validation report](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/download/microduck-software-v2026.11.4/validation-report.json) records Studio 1525 total (97 skipped), image 157, updater 35, voice 173, publisher 17, and 24 recovery interruption points for each of two kinds; the Studio / updater tests actually ran from f70afc3 and voice from 3478589. This check confirmed that the corresponding Git subtrees are identical to f215, but this cannot be restated as a final full-tree re-run. The native report also has 3 reproducible failures against the public baseline, which were not counted as passes; the offline re-check of the final compressed image is also not the same as a fresh-card / on-robot motion acceptance check.

The public beta package and the full ARM image now exist; **hardware acceptance checks are still pending, and the production / test provenance and delivery status of the standalone 18.2 have not moved forward**. This time we read this repo's new release records; it was not a tracking task that built or published an image. Apart from the main branch / PR / dev tag changes above, the default commits of the other three repos, the 8 official assets, other existing dev tags / PRs, model metadata and the 3 blog posts are all unchanged.

### Nov 4 check: runtime missing-servo diagnosis PR and two standalone dev builds

At 19:31 JST, the pending-merge [#525](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/525) was opened, pinned at [`909478c8bb`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/909478c8bbfeee09a289ce0b9d4157ac38bf687f), 1 commit and 8 files relative to main branch d13. At 19:35 a standalone [missing-servo degraded dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-robotd-runtime-missing-servos-degraded) was released at the same time, 0.18.4-dev.1559, whose actual SHA is the same as the PR, source 0.18.4 / API48; **neither the main branch nor the official 0.18.4 / API47 has changed, and the PR is still not merged.** This API48 is a semantic extension of existing health fields and must not be equated with other API48 contracts such as the gamepad / trip ones.

When consecutive read failures reach max_consecutive_errors, the [control loop](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/909478c8bbfeee09a289ce0b9d4157ac38bf687f/robotd/src/main.rs#L2538) first runs a census check and then publishes the count; while failures persist it rechecks every 5 seconds, and a single successful read clears the count and the list. The [Dynamixel backend](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/909478c8bbfeee09a289ce0b9d4157ac38bf687f/duck-control/src/bus.rs#L247) pings the 15 servos and the ID200 small board one by one. When missing parts are found, [robot.health](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/909478c8bbfeee09a289ce0b9d4157ac38bf687f/robotd/src/main.rs#L964) reports the list using the startup-time wording, with healthy=false / degraded=true; when everything answers, the backend has no answer, or the check itself errors, it does not invent missing parts and still marks the robot unhealthy based on consecutive read failures. Earlier branches such as policy errors may still take precedence.

This is a diagnostic proposal for telling update failures apart from missing parts; **it is not a fix for lost connections or proof that motion is safe, and it adds no torque-release / stop / automatic part-replacement flow**. The census runs synchronously on the control thread, and the source estimates about half a second when everything is silent; the Feetech bus timeout, control / deadman latency and health sampling window need verification. Before the first census returns, the threshold count has not been published yet, and the downstream health function still checks for tick staleness, so "census first, then publish" alone cannot guarantee that the update gate never sees a transient stall. [RobotIo returns None by default](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/909478c8bbfeee09a289ce0b9d4157ac38bf687f/duck-control/src/io.rs#L206); a read-only check of this project's pinned d870 found no census in RobotIo, HardwareIo or FeetechIo, so simply keeping the default implementation will not give Feetech missing-servo diagnosis. This item is rated **medium-high priority as a diagnostic reference**, but the HD-1910 protocol / health criteria and timing must be adapted separately.

3 new state-decision tests and 1 test definition pairing a real control_loop with a fake bus were added; they were only read, not run. The author reports that the robotd / duck-control / robotctl tests pass on macOS with no new clippy warnings, excluding nfc, and explicitly says it has not been run on a board yet; this cannot be recorded as a Linux full-workspace, Feetech hardware or this-project acceptance check. There are no changes to the RGB camera, ToF, policy weights, model management, training or sound assets.

At 19:28 a separate [nfc-mode-tags dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-nfc-mode-tags) was also created, 0.18.3-dev.1558, actually [`42afb9dbac`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/42afb9dbac17bd9a0422711c7f00407f8373d99f), source 0.18.3 / API47. On top of the existing pairing commit 7ae it [adds only 1 commit and 4 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55...42afb9dbac17bd9a0422711c7f00407f8373d99f), keeping the generic pairing card, candidate priority and SPI Type 5 support; **the original nfc-pairing-tag is still 7ae and has not been replaced.** Relative to the current main branch it is 5 commits ahead and 6 behind, and still lacks the later 517 / 518 / 519 changes.

The [mode table and requests](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/42afb9dbac17bd9a0422711c7f00407f8373d99f/nfc/src/modes.rs#L58) only implement walk_mode→walk and roller_skating_mode→roller; soccer_mode, laser_mode and autonomous_mode only chirp and log NotYet without sending a mode request, so soccer, laser or autonomous skills cannot be called supported. Text / URI values are trimmed of leading/trailing whitespace and then matched exactly and case-sensitively; [a multi-record card pairs first, otherwise takes the first matching mode](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/42afb9dbac17bd9a0422711c7f00407f8373d99f/nfc/src/main.rs#L246), keeping the per-tap suppression. The mode path lacks the pairing flow's "skip if a gamepad is already connected" check, so the control handover needs review.

Mode cards reuse the chirp, and a failed sound call still proceeds with the request; Switched only means IntentResult.accepted, **it does not wait for state confirmation that the mode switched or that the policy loaded**, and rejections / connection failures are logged. The 4 fake-Driver test definitions were only read; no card reading, mode transition or on-robot check was done. With full NFC hardware this is medium priority; with no current NFC deliverable it is low. There are no new models, training, HD-1910 adaptation, camera / ToF pipeline or sound material. Neither package was downloaded, run, installed or brought into this project.

This round fast-forwarded and kept the new content on this repo's remote. Although the main repo already has the [Nov 4 candidate notes](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/blob/cb2345867a61e92976c8218e5aa3a82042389d16/docs/更新说明-20261104.md), the releases API showed both the 20261104 image and the 2026.11.4 software as draft=true, prerelease=true, published_at=null, and fetching the public release by tag still returned 404; **at this point they cannot be recorded as a newly published image / software**. The existing 27th image, the 31-r1 released software and the standalone 18.2 production / test checkpoints keep their own identities; the new main-repo document also cannot replace the validation records of the standalone candidate. The other official default commits, the 8 official assets, existing dev tags / PRs, model metadata and the 3 blog posts are all unchanged; this round only committed the tracking document and did not run tests, build, install, connect to the robot, or operate the web console / services.

### Nov 4 check: 5 boundary-validation PRs, not yet in the main branch or a release package

Between 13:48 and 13:50 JST, #520–#524 were opened, all **pending-merge, non-draft proposals**, 1 commit each, 7 files in total; all are based on main branch `d13be2c1a2f4b1e0b0e499b08b92f870518b1384`. The following was checked against the actual pinned source; PR titles and the authors' test reports are not treated as released features.

| PR / pinned commit | Actual change and scope | Suggested priority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [#520](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/520) · [`a38a49e52a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a38a49e52a6f2501effd93b65948dd4c467efc8f/robotctl/src/endurance.rs#L118) | The endurance walk / stand / sit durations share a span; a `Duration::try_from_secs_f64(hi)` check is added, rejecting `inf` and oversized values such as `1:2e19` before the background unit starts. NaN was already rejected by the ordering check, and the new test only preserves that behavior; it does not validate all float parameters or all Instant deadlines. | Medium: reference before using the endurance-test CLI; not a new motion policy. |
| [#521](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/521) · [`26083c5e45`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/26083c5e45ec31c609cbb260d53b9d084c586823/updater/src/config.rs#L440) | Rejects `max_uncompressed_bytes=0` or `max_archive_entries=0` when loading the config, moving an error that previously only appeared at unpacking time earlier. When unset, the default limits still apply, and 0 does not mean unlimited; the default limits, signature verification, installation and rollback flows are unchanged. | Medium: reference for native updater config pre-checks; does not mean the 15→18 migration or the full delivery package is done. |
| [#522](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/522) · [`a9f2e94edc`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a9f2e94edc96ced47f84eab1f4fa031275476437/duck-detect/src/lib.rs#L105) | RGB detection decode filters out non-finite scores before thresholding / sorting / overlapping-box suppression, so bad scores no longer suppress valid candidates at the same location. The new test covers NaN / positive infinity, and the condition also excludes negative infinity; no new validation was added for box coordinates, threshold or iou_limit, so this cannot be called full tensor validation. | Medium: watch when enabling detection consumers; does not change camera capture, transport, calibration or model weights. |
| [#523](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/523) · [`88779b1114`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/88779b1114c1b9e3e9a7ed22de8b1563e072ac07/duck-ble/src/framing.rs#L181) | chunks changes from only a 20-byte lower bound to a 20–512-byte clamp, protecting callers that pass 517 or larger values directly, and verifies fragment reassembly. The existing main-branch [btd backend](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d13be2c1a2f4b1e0b0e499b08b92f870518b1384/btd/src/bluez.rs#L440) already limits the length with notification_payload before passing it through Link to the session; this is extra protection at the function level, not a fix for a new failure seen in phone testing. chunks itself still does not subtract the ATT header, so small-MTU callers must still pass the correct payload limit. | Low: preventive BLE hardening; raise if new callers bypass the helper. |
| [#524](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/524) · [`fce32d114d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/fce32d114d4747f1bc469cc67cf3b1c975592623/scripts/publish-console.sh#L32) | Both publishers report a usage error and exit 2 for a missing / empty / hyphen-prefixed --space value, so --dry-run is no longer swallowed as the target; [publish-space](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/fce32d114d4747f1bc469cc67cf3b1c975592623/scripts/publish-space.sh#L19) also rejects options in the NAME position. The check happens before staging / clone / push; the error message says org/name, but that format is not fully validated. | Low: reference when manually publishing the official HF Space; does not involve this project's web console or image releases. |

The 4 native modules each add 1 test definition and the publishers [add 3](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/fce32d114d4747f1bc469cc67cf3b1c975592623/xtask/tests/publish_scripts.rs#L1), 7 in total, all only read. The authors each report that the target crate tests, fmt and clippy pass, and say a normal dry-run of both scripts still works; **this project did not run these checks, scripts or any hardware test**. A read-only check of the local pinned production commit `d870d68fa5fe16a349eaacc906a9a748608445d5` shows the span, archive budget, decode and chunks still keep the old conditions, so none of this can be recorded as integrated.

This set of proposals adds no Feetech HD-1910 driver / gain / current adaptation, ToF, model management / weights, training, motion policy or sound assets; the detection item affects the robustness of RGB inference results and the endurance item affects test parameters, and neither proves hardware behavior has passed acceptance. The default commits of the four repos, official 0.18.4 / API47 and its 8 assets, dev tags, merge status of existing PRs, model metadata and the 3 official blog posts are all unchanged. RL #77 only has the thank-you comment that was already reviewed. This project's pinned 18.2 candidate, existing software validation records, released software and the 27th image remain as they were; this round only maintained documentation.

### Nov 4: new stress-test-v2, a frozen-map stress-test prototype

At 00:55 JST, the [stress-test-v2 dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test-v2) was created, 0.18.4-dev.1557, actually [`6193e4eaf5`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce), [source 0.18.4](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/Cargo.toml) / [API50](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs). **It is separate from the original stress-test, whose old tag is still 854 / trip API48; official 0.18.4 / API47 and its 8 assets are unchanged.** The [25 commits and 56 files relative to 854](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/854adb810d9d9fa2188ebace6d28543d888235a6...6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce) consist of 23 historical commits from the mapping branch, one merge and the current 16-file feature commit, so they cannot be counted as 25 new features. Relative to main branch d13 it is 53 commits ahead and 2 behind, 70 files, and still does not include the #519 editor row.

The mapping service mapd and the algorithm maploc use robot.state and the head's 8×8 ToF to accumulate / match views while the robot is parked, bridge them with odometry and save the map; **mapd itself sends no motion commands**. The [map config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) defaults to enabled=false; when off it only reports itself unavailable and does not subscribe to sensors. The defaults of 25 Hz state, 60 ms ToF latency and 60-second autosave cannot be taken as Feetech calibration or a verified A55 load. Including mapd / its unit and routes in the package template does not mean this project has built and delivered it. map.status / stream / grid / wipe can be forwarded over WebRTC, while map.mode is explicitly not forwarded over WebRTC / BLE and uses the local map socket.

The [stress driver](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/robotctl/src/stress/mod.rs) requests a localize_only frozen map on every lap and reads the 5 cm grid. The [room geometry](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/robotctl/src/stress/room.rs) treats non-free cells as wall / unknown, computes clearance, and picks a relatively open position once as home; among 24 headings it looks for a direction that can fit the whole walk, prefers heading back when more than 0.5 m from home, and if no full-length path exists it picks the longest and logs map_short. While walking it looks 5 cm ahead, and if blocked or if localization is lost on the way it stands until the end of that step; in-place turns have no map guard. Map state is limited to 1 second of freshness and it tries to bridge with nearby odometry; **this is not a proven localization error bound or body / foot / fall clearance**. The clearance threshold is the smaller of wall and the current clearance, so when already against a wall it is not a forced 30 cm; wall / unknown / off-map boundaries with zero clearance at the start still need verification, and the claim from the description that it never hits walls cannot be repeated as fact. A frozen snapshot also does not guarantee that newly moved-in obstacles will be detected in real time along this walking path.

With no map, the service unavailable, or relocalization failing before starting, **the step falls back to the old odometry box, forward ToF and the backing-up look_back, rather than always stopping**; the old path can still continue without ToF. The default relocalization is nominally 15 seconds, pausing 3 seconds each time before turning 90°, and turning / RPC / getting up can lengthen the actual time. --no-map keeps the old guards; --map-learns only skips the freeze request. [map.mode and the mapper](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/maploc/src/mapper.rs) keep learning when there is no map, and a failed freeze is only logged before continuing; freezing clears islands not connected to the main map, rather than being a read-only subscription. The mode is global and not persistent: when mapd restarts it goes back to learning first, and the freeze is only re-sent on the next lap; a normal finish requests a return to learning, which is not the same as restoring whatever mode someone else set before, and abnormal exits / RPC failures cannot guarantee the handback. Therefore "all robots always test against the same frozen map" is still an experimental condition that needs verification.

[map load](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/robotctl/src/map.rs) importing another robot's map requires root, does a basic JSON shape check, clears the last_pose hint, stops mapd, sets the old map aside, writes a temporary file / permissions and replaces it, then tries to start the service; **this is not a plain read-only copy, a failure does not automatically restore the set-aside old map**, and it will not turn on a disabled config. This round did not import or run this command.

The [latest commit](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce) explicitly says it has not been on a robot yet and has not run the maploc_truth ground-truth regression gate. The 33 cm to 8 cm improvement in the [design notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6193e4eaf5a5090f7cb22a8e6d4b810080ff49ce/docs/design/mapping-design.md) is the author's simulation result, and the 15 new geometry / file / freeze simulation test definitions were only read; neither this nor the old official mapping blog post is treated as a real-hardware acceptance check for this project / the final source. **Standalone ToF mapping / stress-test research is medium priority, and low priority for the current 18.2 delivery; first review the API50 consumers and delivery, ToF / Feetech odometry calibration, map permissions / lifecycle, and the actual stop boundaries.** No servo driver, gain / current adaptation, RGB camera pipeline, policy weights, training or sound assets were added. The extra parking / turning changes the measurement conditions, so the map source, mode and events need to be recorded. The other official default branches, PRs, model metadata, blog posts and this project's pinned 18.2 production / test, released software and 27th image records are unchanged; this round did not run source / tests / replays, build, install, import maps, connect to the robot or operate the web console / services.

### Nov 4 check: NFC dev build takes in Type 5 and gamepad candidate priority

At 23:34 JST on Nov 3, the [nfc-pairing-tag dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-nfc-pairing-tag) was updated to 0.18.3-dev.1556, actually [`7ae5735695`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55), and was checked and recorded on Nov 4. The [3 commits and 9 files relative to 5d](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/5d6066b297fda58af6189788202033a666b373b0...7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55) include gamepad candidate priority, the intake of the existing #475 Type 5 approach, and fixes following its review. The source is still 0.18.3 / API47, 4 commits ahead and 6 behind main branch d13, 14 files. **It is still a moving dev prototype; official 0.18.4 and its 8 assets, the default main branch and this project's source / image are unchanged, and #475 is still not merged.**

[BlueZ selection](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55/configd/src/bluez.rs), when no address is given and there are multiple candidates, first keeps unbonded gamepads, folds dual-sided ones by the existing last-three-octets address rule, then prefers candidates with icon / class / appearance classification evidence, dropping name-only matches. Only if two equally strong unbonded candidates remain after folding does it report ambiguity; when all are bonded it still picks one by address under the old rule. **When a real name-only gamepad and another classified gamepad are present at the same time, it may now pick the latter directly instead of refusing.** This is a heuristic trade-off, not proof that the two belong to the same gamepad. The [generic card marker](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55/nfc/src/ndef.rs) must still equal microduck_gamepad_pairing exactly and case-sensitively after trimming, and old MAC-only cards still do not trigger; skipping pairing when a gamepad is already connected, one attempt per tap, and the chirp / greet requests are kept, and #477's pad.connect / status API48 is not added.

**Type 5 is only wired into the ST25R100 SPI branch; the CLRC663 USB serial branch still only reads Type 2.** [nfcd polling](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55/nfc/src/main.rs) asks ISO14443A and ISO15693 in turn on both antennas, and whichever finds a tag first wins; the single-slot inventory assumes one tag in the field and does not enumerate multiple tags. The tag needs a readable NDEF with the marker above; "Type 5" does not mean every card / phone has been verified. [Protocol switching](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55/nfc/src/st25r100.rs) keeps the original A initialization and saves 7 register groups, enables settings such as the AM regulator / 12% modulation only in V mode, and restores them when switching back to A; nfcd resets the RF field after entering V. The 14 new test definitions cover register round-trips, candidate selection and parse / read fake devices; they were only read statically and are not an RF regression measurement.

[Type 5 reading](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55/nfc/src/vtag.rs) reads blocks addressed by UID, uses the length declared by the CC's MLEN, capped at 32 blocks; a nonzero MLEN uses the 4-byte container, and MLEN zero with RFU bytes 4–5 zero uses the 8-byte container, keeping the old bring-up fallback of MLEN0 / 4 bytes. When the first item is not an NDEF TLV it reads the declared / capped range so that a leading Lock Control TLV can be skipped by the shared parser; no CC is treated as blank, and a message exceeding the read bound reports an explicit error rather than treating truncation as success. The source also changes the description of 0x26 to standard single-slot inventory, with 0x06 as 16-slot, and removes the old explanation that "ST additionally requires bit5". These bounds cannot be taken to mean arbitrary large-capacity / extended-address tags are supported. The default 5 Hz is kept; the roughly 110 ms empty-field poll is only a beta estimate in a source comment; the [SPI trace](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7ae573569522af9558cc04109ab2edd619592a55/nfc/src/spi.rs) now uses tracing and logs only the first 12 bytes, so it is not a full capture; the bench examples were not run.

7ae addresses the [read-only review of #475 on Oct 30](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/475#pullrequestreview-5709961230), whose reviewer explicitly had no hardware; the PR head is still c1b5d419 and was not updated or approved by this dev build. The 28 tests, beta ST25R100 / ST25TV and NTAG pairing, and 11–17 second durations reported by the old submitter remain historical external evidence and **cannot be relabeled as a final 7ae re-run or this project's real-hardware acceptance check**.

**With matching ST25R100 hardware this is a medium-priority reference; with no current NFC flow it is lower priority.** Before adopting, first check the reader / format, the A↔V round trip and mixed candidate selection. No HD-1910 servo registers / gain / current, camera, ToF, model management, training, weights or sound assets were added; the other dev tags, PRs, model metadata, the [official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) and this project's pinned production / test / release records are unchanged. This round only maintained documentation and did not run source / tests / bench examples, write cards, install, connect to the robot, or access / restart the existing web console.

### Nov 3: training PR #77 switches to Windows cu128; existing text-policy contract recorded after the fact

At 22:34 JST, [microduck_rl #77](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/pull/77) was updated to [`e8f8c29bf5`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/commit/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1), **still pending merge**. It replays 4 existing commits and then adds one Windows commit: [5 commits and 26 files relative to develop bae](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/compare/bae39f5900b318f7e87fc485c30a31213cfa14eb...e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1). The [comparison with the old head](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/compare/0ed85a56da1dd22f992059aefc2020a786505767...e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1) diverges into 10 incoming / 4 old-side commits and 34 files, including 5 training-bundle main-branch commits recorded long ago, so not all of it counts as new features. **A file-by-file comparison confirms that 18 files are identical to the old 0ed, including the VLA core, infer, the plan and all 6 test files; the text policy already existed, and this round records its contract after the fact rather than it being added today.** The truly new e8 changes only 4 files: the cu128 rules / lock, a cache startup script and ignore entries. The four default branches, official 0.18.4 and its 8 assets, and stress and other dev tags are all unchanged.

The [final dependency rules](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/pyproject.toml) pin torch on all win32 to the cu128 index, with the lock at 2.9.1+cu128; the PR description still says cu126, and the pinned source should be taken as authoritative. This is not automatic package selection by GPU generation. The CUDA device 0 probe already in the original PR switches the registered MicroDuck training / play configs to sparse Jacobian+CG when compute capability is below 7, and that probe is not limited to Windows. The msvcrt arrow keys, test UTF8 / launcher differences and PowerShell training notes are retained content with unchanged bytes and do not mean Win11 / GPU on this machine has been verified. The [cache script](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/scripts/fix_windows_profile_writes.py) writes sitecustomize.py to redirect cache / temp directories at Python startup; outside a venv it only warns and still writes, overwrites an existing file, and remove deletes that path. It is not a read-only check, was not run this round, and is not treated as a fix for the known WSL / web console issues.

**The "VLA" here is actually a text-conditioned velocity / head / body policy, with no visual input, speech recognition or TTS.** The [task config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/microduck_vla_env_cfg.py) and the [actor](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/vla/model.py) remove the numeric command from the actor, normalize only the 48-dim proprioceptive state, then concatenate the raw 384-dim frozen text vector; the critic and rewards still use the 13-dim numeric command for the same intent. The [table](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/vla/instructions.json) has 12 intents and 125 Chinese/English synonymous phrasings, resampled every 4–8 seconds; this is not arbitrary language understanding or proof of long-training quality. The default MiniLM encoder is an optional dependency; the hash backend is only for tests and is not a deployable semantic model.

The [release contract](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/publish/manifest.py) still marks model_api=1 but uses schema 3 / command.encoding=text plus policy.onnx and encoder.onnx: the policy maps obs[1,48] and text_emb[1,384] to actions[1,14], and the encoder maps input_ids / attention_mask to text_emb.
