The [publish CLI](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/publish/cli.py) checks input names / dimensions and records the encoder digest, but model can be written as unknown, and there is no fully pinned HF revision / tokenizer provenance; matching 384 dims is not enough to prove semantic compatibility. The [infer script](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/scripts/infer_policy.py) supports text / file / N-key changes and caches encodings only in MuJoCo rehearsal. **The official 0.18.4 [loader](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/2573246f44cb5dab426714a1b45ae34746d7f1a2/duck-control/src/policy.rs) and this project's pinned d870 native source still require 61-dim obs and only accept 1-in-1-out or LSTM 3-in-3-out; neither can load this 48-dim dual-input model directly.** Compatibility cannot be assumed from identical model_api or daemon API numbers.

Static review also found that the [pre-cache branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/mdp.py) returns the embedding array directly without setting the encoded state of [InstructionPool](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/src/mjlab_microduck/vla/instructions.py), so sample's precondition after construction will reject it; the existing config tests take the hash path and cannot be treated as verifying the cache path. This round did not attempt a reproduction. Some comments / plans still say 61 dims; the current 48-dim implementation and test definitions should be taken as authoritative. The [old 0ed commit message](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/commit/0ed85a56da1dd22f992059aefc2020a786505767), replayed as 3c440f this time, keeps its original report claiming 87 VLA / publish tests and 3 infer tests, a 64-env 5-iteration smoke run and encoder parity checks passed; the [plan document](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/e8f8c29bf56eec8b2f3998d2468c87324f100ee1/docs/vla-plan.md) dates the validation Oct 20 and explicitly says long training and the native runtime are not done. **This is the author's report on the old VLA commit and cannot be treated as a new test just because the replayed SHA changed; it is not evidence of a final e8 / cu128 re-run, nor an acceptance check for this project, ARM latency, model delivery or real hardware.**

**Medium-priority standalone training research: first fill in verification of the input / release contract, encoder provenance and the cache path, then consider runtime adaptation.** It still targets XL330 / 14 actions / 50 Hz, has no HD-1910 driver or joint / gain / current adaptation, and adds no camera, ToF or sound assets. Existing model metadata, blog posts, this project's standalone 18.2 production / test, released software and 27th image records are unchanged; this round only read the pinned source and test definitions, and did not install dependencies, download models / checkpoints, train, export, run tests, publish models, connect to the robot, or access / restart the existing web console.

### Nov 3: stress withdraws obstacle memory, looks behind before backing up instead

At 20:28 JST, the [stress-test dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test) was updated to 0.18.4-dev.1555, actually [`854adb810d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/854adb810d9d9fa2188ebace6d28543d888235a6), source still 0.18.4 / **trip API48**. The [2 commits and 4 files relative to 7d](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd...854adb810d9d9fa2188ebace6d28543d888235a6) first [withdraw the 6af obstacle approach](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/3e4f925a6365ea18f4d19e1299c2c3a7a62849ce) and then add look_back; relative to main branch d13 it is 28 commits ahead and 2 behind, 32 files. **The next section's 7d features (90-second grid memory, extra 35 cm fall room, re-entry, fall_skipped and boxed_in) have all been withdrawn and are kept only as history.** In the withdrawal notes the author says all falls were repeatedly skipped in a small enclosure; there is no reproduction by this project or pinned on-robot log that could serve as our acceptance check. The 30 cm wall and resetting home on hitting a wall are kept.

The [new driver](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/854adb810d9d9fa2188ebace6d28543d888235a6/robotctl/src/stress/mod.rs), before each backward walk, requests the head to face left and turns the body 90° to the left; after pausing 0.4 seconds it reads the nearer left-side ToF result over a 0.3-second window. [nearest_left](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/854adb810d9d9fa2188ebace6d28543d888235a6/robotctl/src/stress/guard.rs) rotates the Hit coordinates and filters them along the existing 15 cm half-width corridor. The required distance is abs(vx × that step's seconds) + wall: if a wall is near, it continues the 180° turn in the original direction and backs away from the wall; otherwise it turns back to the original heading. At the end it requests the head to face forward, logs look_back and releases the time anchoring, and the backward step still keeps its full duration; these extra body / head turns change the duration, current and motion conditions.

**This is the intended look geometry, not an accepted Feetech rear-coverage check.** The RobotLook result's accepted flag is not checked, the fixed 0.4 seconds is not confirmation that the head has arrived, and the previous gaze target is not restored. turn_to judges by a 0.08 rad odometry error, with a nominal 4 seconds per loop; a timeout or lost pose still returns success, and communication / reconnection can also stretch the time. A missing initial pose skips the look entirely, and missing valid depth / Hit can lead into the clear:nothing branch; the cache is limited to 500 ms, with no dedicated barrier waiting for a fresh frame after the head turns. There is no continuous rearward ranging protection during the actual backward walk, and the boxed_in limit for forward wall obstacles has also been withdrawn. **The clear log or the 6 scheduled fall steps still cannot be treated as proof of safety or as 6 measured falls.**

The version / protocol, the 10-second boot entry sharing a 120-second wait with the first frame, and the 5 Git blobs of the offline plotter and its tests are identical to 7d; the log concatenation / timeline limitations still apply, and there is no new official release. One new nearest_left test definition was added and two old grid-memory test definitions were removed; this round only read the source and did not run tests or any robot procedure. The 500 ms / both legs, per-trip config reading with failure fallback, getting up and the help sound are unchanged; official 0.18.4 / API47 and its 8 assets, the four default branches, PRs, model metadata and the [official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) are all unchanged.

**Still listed as a later controlled bench reference; first verify HD-1910 head turn angle / FK, ToF extrinsics, head control ownership and turning / stopping boundaries.** No servo driver, camera pipeline, model management, training, policy weights or sound assets were added; this project's standalone 18.2 and its committed tests, released software and 27th image records remain as they were. This round did not modify runtime code, run tests, install, play sounds, connect to the robot, or access / restart the existing web console.

### Nov 3: stress obstacle memory, fall room and offline log concatenation

At 19:58 JST, the [stress-test dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test) was updated to 0.18.4-dev.1554, actually [`7d195ab31b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd), source 0.18.4 / **trip API48**. The [3 commits and 8 files relative to 5c](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/5c0d1fb04181c722b3734168b51d911d1232702e...7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd) change obstacle judgment, offline charts and the boot wait respectively; relative to main branch d13 it is 26 commits ahead and 2 behind, 32 files, and still does not include the #519 editor row. **Official 0.18.4 / API47 and its 8 assets are unchanged; this is a standalone dev prototype, with no new official release or image for this project.**

[Obstacle memory](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd/robotctl/src/stress/guard.rs) places head ToF Hits within 1 m into a 5 cm grid according to the current odometry position / heading and keeps them for 90 seconds; the floor is not mapped, the map is cleared when an odometry jump is detected, and old obstacles are not cleared by free lines of sight. Forward walking takes the nearer of the live and remembered results, and backward walking only checks the forward ToF remembered before turning, **with no new rear sensor, SLAM, or guarantee of stopping when depth is missing**. It can still continue without ToF, and the memory also expires, so this cannot be treated as a Feetech collision-avoidance acceptance check.

The [actual motion driver](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd/robotctl/src/stress/mod.rs) changes the default wall from 20 to 30 cm and adds fall_room with a default of 35 cm; walks leading to a fall in the same direction and the fall check need 65 cm by default. When blocked it tries to turn and re-enter for up to 1 second, and if there is still no room it logs fall_skipped and skips, **so the 6 scheduled fall steps per lap do not equal 6 actual falls**. Encountering an obstacle on the way back to the box center can reset home; this is an approximate judgment, not proven physical drift. The turn limit for boxed_in is estimated from the commanded angular velocity and time, not a measured full rotation. The 500 ms / both legs defaults, per-trip config reading with failure fallback, and the existing getting-up / help sound are kept.

The [boot timer](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd/robotctl/systemd/robotctl-stress-boot.timer) moves from 30 seconds earlier to 10 seconds; on_boot still defaults to false. The entry first waits for the socket to appear, then for the first robot.state, with both stages sharing a 120-second deadline, rather than starting on the socket alone. **Receiving the first frame does not mean the servos, standing or ToF are ready**; this round did not do any actual systemd / robot boot test.

The [offline plotter](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/7d195ab31bf727016d2a90eca538955c5fdf2afd/scripts/endurance-plot.py) adds a repeatable --concatenate DIR, which writes the direct child run directories into two CSVs and meta.json under DIR/concatenated, keeping segments, marking session and continuing lap numbering; the three files are written sequentially. The main timeline prefers t_s, and when it drops by more than 1 second it continues by segment, excluding wall-clock adjustments and shutdown / charging gaps; **a concatenated curve cannot be used to claim one continuous real-hardware endurance run**. Known different lap versions are refused, and differing fall values in the last metadata only produce a warning; trip_ms / trip_legs or source identity are not checked, and stress-v1 is unchanged. Static review also found that non-empty old events missing t_s will index that column first, and samples / events detect resets separately; PDF event minutes still use wall time, so this cannot be described generally as full old-log compatibility or a unified timeline for all charts. session avoids pairing power-draw samples across concatenation boundaries, which does not mean the other unmerged #504 proposal has been integrated.

**Still listed only as a later controlled bench experiment; first check ToF loss / memory drift, skipped falls, stop boundaries and logged experiment conditions.** No HD-1910 driver, camera pipeline, model management, training, weights or sound assets were added; the motion and measurement conditions change, so old fall counts cannot be carried over as a new acceptance check. The two Rust memory tests and four Python chart tests were only read; the source was not run / imported and no charts were generated. The other official default branches, PRs, model metadata, the [official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) and this project's pinned production / test / release records are unchanged; this round did not run configuration, tests, stress / sound commands, install or connect to the robot, and the 15 version, the standalone 18.2 candidate, the 27th image and the existing web console were not changed.

### Nov 3: stress defaults to 500 ms and reads the config on each trip

At 18:36 JST, the [stress-test dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test) was updated to 0.18.4-dev.1553, actually [`5c0d1fb041`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/5c0d1fb04181c722b3734168b51d911d1232702e), source 0.18.4 / **trip API48**; this is not the same as official 0.18.4/API47 or the #477 gamepad API48. The [6 commits and 28 files relative to 25f](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/25f3a591cff1d3475ca661ab2ba469fc143a96e7...5c0d1fb04181c722b3734168b51d911d1232702e) include 4 already-recorded main-branch commits, 1 [7-file actual feature commit](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7228ae5568f2d3626366c121d69758bd7d1f1648) and one merge; it takes in the #517 dev signing and #518 version preparation, but not the #519 editor row. Relative to main branch d13 it is 23 commits ahead and 2 behind, 32 files. **The official tag and its 8 assets are unchanged; there is no new official release or image for this project.**

[StressParams](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c0d1fb04181c722b3734168b51d911d1232702e/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) changes the default leg-target hold time from 250 to 500 ms, with a default of both, and on_boot still false; new stress.trip_ms / trip_legs settings are added, and whole-Params validation requires 1–1000 ms and both / left / right. The editor still classifies on_boot as NextBoot, while the [trip parameters are classified as read on the next fall](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c0d1fb04181c722b3734168b51d911d1232702e/robotctl/src/configure.rs), with no restart needed; this is not per-second hot reloading.

The [actual driver](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c0d1fb04181c722b3734168b51d911d1232702e/robotctl/src/stress/mod.rs) reads the full config given by --config before every trip; the boot entry also passes the config path to the background experiment. --trip-ms / --trip-legs each override the corresponding item, so either can be fixed for this run. **If reading or validation fails, it logs config_unreadable, then takes the new built-in 500 ms / both legs and applies the command-line overrides on top; it does not stop the experiment or keep the last valid value.** The new CLI duration is an optional u32 with no front-end range check matching the file's, and the daemon ultimately still rejects requests outside 1–1000 ms. When config-driven, the trip entries in meta.args may be empty, so read the actual ms / legs from the fall_triggered events and the source; lap is still stress-v1, so the old 250 ms and new 500 ms measurements cannot be compared directly by the same name.

A source comment says 250 ms is often caught while 500 ms falls more easily; this is the author's statement, with no pinned real-hardware measurement by this project. **Keep it at controlled-bench-experiment priority; before adopting, first verify parameter changes / fallback and Feetech joints, gains, current, landing and stop boundaries.** The two new parameter validation / config-application plan tests were only read, not run. No HD-1910 driver, camera / ToF pipeline, model management, training, policy weights or sound assets were added; the existing help sound is unchanged. The four official default branches, PR status, official assets / manifests, model metadata, blog posts and this project's acceptance / release records remain as they were; this round did not run configuration, tests, fall or sound commands, install, restart or connect to the robot, and the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image and the web console were not changed.

### Nov 3: main branch adds a dev-switch editor row; stress gets a help prompt on failure

**The official 0.18.4 tag, its 8 assets and both manifests are unchanged.** At 17:36 JST, [#519](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/519) was merged into [`d13be2c1a2`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/d13be2c1a2f4b1e0b0e499b08b92f870518b1384), [2 commits and 6 files relative to official 257](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/2573246f44cb5dab426714a1b45ae34746d7f1a2...d13be2c1a2f4b1e0b0e499b08b92f870518b1384), source still 0.18.4/API47; the actual tag of dev-main1550 at 17:41 is d13. It is an unreleased main-branch change, not yet another official release.

The [config editor](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d13be2c1a2f4b1e0b0e499b08b92f870518b1384/robotctl/src/configure.rs) adds a dev board / allow_dev_daemon row at the top that can be toggled, reset to the default off, and searched; it is not a key in robotd.toml. [Switch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d13be2c1a2f4b1e0b0e499b08b92f870518b1384/robotctl/src/dev_daemon.rs) hides the row when reading / parsing the updater config fails or state_dir is missing, and its value only reflects whether the marker exists, **not the full effective permission state of "marker or old allow_dev_keys"**; when the old config is true, removing the marker still allows dev signing. Saving writes robotd.toml first and then the marker; if the former fails validation the marker is not written, but if the latter fails to write the already-saved TOML is not rolled back, so this cannot be treated as an atomic transaction across the two files. After saving it asks to confirm restarting updaterd; declining the restart keeps the on-disk edits and shows the command needed to apply them, so "saved" cannot be taken to mean the running key policy has switched. The 3 new editor / Switch test definitions were only read; the upstream coverage bot output is not this project's acceptance check.

**The stress standalone dev prototype** released 1551 at 18:03, actually [`25f3a591cf`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/25f3a591cff1d3475ca661ab2ba469fc143a96e7), source still 0.18.3 / trip API48, which differs from the gamepad #477's 48. The [single commit with 3 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1...25f3a591cff1d3475ca661ab2ba469fc143a96e7) only adds a [help prompt](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/25f3a591cff1d3475ca661ab2ba469fc143a96e7/robotctl/src/stress/mod.rs) after a failed get-up enters gave_up: two existing chirps, a wheee hold continued for about 1.2 seconds and then released, then a wait of call_every (default 15 seconds); 0 or negative stays silent and waits for stop. **It does not strictly start once every 15 seconds**; the sound sequence, RPCs and the old 5-second disconnect reconnection can all stretch the interval, and the sound RPC's accepted flag and actual audibility are not checked. The low-battery hands_off branch does not call this help prompt. The increment has no new audio files, automated test definitions, new get-up motion or policy weights; relative to the new main branch it is 21 commits ahead and 6 behind, 32 files.

**Medium-priority dev-workflow reference; stress is still listed only as a later controlled bench experiment.** Before adopting, first check effective signing permissions, ordered saving and the restart contract; the existing Feetech HD-1910 target holding / getting up / torque-release boundaries still need real-hardware verification. The help sound changes the acoustic / power measurement conditions at the gave_up tail, so comparisons should take into account meta's call_every, events and the source. Camera, ToF, model management, training, action mapping and audio assets have no new adaptation this time, and real volume / mouth effects are unverified. The other official default branches, model metadata, blog posts and this project's pinned acceptance / release records are unchanged; this round did not run tests, toggle config switches, restart, play sounds, install or connect to the robot, and the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image and the existing web console were not changed.

### Nov 3: 0.18.4 official release and check of its 8 assets

At 16:56 JST, the [official release page daemon-v0.18.4](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.18.4) and its tag were created, actually pinned at [`2573246f44`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/2573246f44cb5dab426714a1b45ae34746d7f1a2). [Source version 0.18.4](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/2573246f44cb5dab426714a1b45ae34746d7f1a2/Cargo.toml) / [API47](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/2573246f44cb5dab426714a1b45ae34746d7f1a2/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs); the [9 commits and 23 files relative to official 0.18.3](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...2573246f44cb5dab426714a1b45ae34746d7f1a2) bring the already-reviewed #513 simulation defaults / displacement report and the #517 persistent dev-signing switch into the official source. #518 only changes the package version in Cargo.toml / Cargo.lock, with no new dependencies or runtime behavior.

**This round spanned the upload of the assets from 0 to 8; they were complete by 17:05.** We checked the GitHub metadata of all 8 assets and the raw bytes / digests of the two small JSON files: manifest version 0.18.4, source_revision 257 in both, the daemon package 15,024,079 bytes with SHA256 `7888d861bf40a9c01d2ddab846d3b7bfd3b5155acdea15cd696aeed5b15ff560`, matching the package asset metadata; the relative / absolute URLs each point to the same package. min_hw_rev=1 is an official manifest field, not proof of compatibility with Feetech boards. **The daemon archive / bootstrap program was not downloaded, minisign was not verified, and nothing was installed on a device.** The historical record of 0.18.3's 8 assets is kept. The actual tag of dev-main1548 at 17:00 is also 257; the dev / official signing streams and acceptance checks remain separate, and the main branch and the official tag share the same source. SDK #271, the generic NFC card prototype and stress's trip/API48 are not in this official source, and this project's image and real-hardware acceptance checks are unchanged.

**We recommend keeping the standalone 18.2 delivery / test image route, then evaluating the 18.4 increment separately.** Simulation / model selection and the signed upgrade flow are the focus of this release; HD-1910 drivers, camera / ToF, policy weights, training, actions and sound have no new adaptation this time. This project's pinned production / test sources, released software, 27th image and web console remain as they were; below we keep the snapshot of the main branch / dev builds from a few minutes before the 0.18.4 release, and the official attribution follows the text above.

### Nov 3: main branch merges the dev-signing switch; stress prototype adds a boot entry and trip/API48

**Pre-release snapshot: the official release at that time was 0.18.3; #517 then went into the 0.18.4 tag above, and stress is still a standalone prototype.** At 16:52 JST, [#517](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/517) was merged into [`76c69647b7`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/76c69647b70583f8f6d9792fc98cf9269386f464); the [2 commits and 19 files relative to 997](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/997808c1d71056d31bd6f05fc5fde848c849d2bc...76c69647b70583f8f6d9792fc98cf9269386f464) add a persistent dev-signing switch. The source is still 0.18.3/API47, and the main branch is cumulatively 7 commits and 21 files ahead of the official tag; [dev-main](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-main) released 1546 at 16:56, with its actual tag also at 76c.

[updaterd](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/76c69647b70583f8f6d9792fc98cf9269386f464/updater/src/verify.rs) has the team.dev public key built in, always marked dev-only; it loads the normal trusted directory first, and an empty directory still fails. The [startup switch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/76c69647b70583f8f6d9792fc98cf9269386f464/updater/src/config.rs) is the old allow_dev_keys being true **or** allow-dev-builds existing under state_dir; the latter lives in /var, so the choice can persist across official A/B system updates. The release config is still false and ships no marker. The new [configure helper](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/76c69647b70583f8f6d9792fc98cf9269386f464/robotctl/src/dev_daemon.rs) writes / deletes the marker and only restarts updaterd when something changed; **off only deletes the marker, and if the old config is still true dev signing is still allowed**; this is not hot switching or skipping signature verification. The old updater does not recognize the marker. Medium-priority dev-workflow reference; before adopting, first check this project's keys, directory permissions, the dual switch and the restart / rollback contract. This round only read the 3 new test definitions and did not run them.

**The standalone stress dev build adds a motion experiment; it is not in the main branch.** [dev.1544](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test) was released at 16:00, actually [`a2e53304da`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1), [2 commits and 26 files relative to 0c14](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8...a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1), source 0.18.3/**trip/API48**; relative to the new main branch it is 20 commits ahead and 2 behind, 32 files, and does not include #517. Its 48 is not the same extension contract as #477's gamepad connect / status 48, let alone the local 18.2's own 47.

- [stress.on_boot](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) defaults to false; the [timer](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/robotctl/systemd/robotctl-stress-boot.timer) invokes the [oneshot](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/robotctl/systemd/robotctl-stress-boot.service) 30 seconds after boot, which waits up to 120 seconds for the robotd socket; a config error does not start it, and an existing experiment is not started twice. Changing the config is only recorded as NextBoot and does not start or stop the experiment immediately. New packaging / install paths are added; the oneshot has no Install section and the install code only enables the timer. These are source and packaging regression definitions, with no real systemd timing acceptance check by this project yet.
- [robot.trip](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs) holds the last-sent targets of the selected legs, defaulting to 250 ms / both, optionally left or right, allowing 1–1000 ms; the policy keeps running, and head and mouth targets still come from the policy. When the RPC has the policy on it accepts and queues the request, which does not mean it actually executes; the [control loop](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/robotd/src/main.rs) also checks for the Walk driver and a controller that is not busy / not sitting, otherwise it drops the request and logs it; a timeout or stopping the driver releases the hold. This is an active tripping tool and cannot be treated as an accepted HD-1910 fall protection.
- The [stress default fall](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/robotctl/src/stress/mod.rs) changes from turning the policy off to trip, with the old cut method still selectable; limp events / summaries are added during getting up. The lap identifier is still [stress-v1](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a2e53304da55f4b9a2b13b7e08498903162fb7b1/robotctl/src/stress/lap.rs); comparing measurements must take into account fall / trip_ms / trip_legs in meta and the pinned source, and the same lap string cannot be taken to mean the same experimental method.

**Keep it at controlled-bench-experiment priority; before adopting, check the signing and motion boundaries at high priority.** Feetech joint names / order, gains, current, target holding and landing must be accepted independently; there is no new camera / ToF implementation, the existing approximate guard and the fallback when ToF is missing are unchanged, and no new model weights, training or sound assets are added. A static review of the pinned local 785 found no DEV_BUILDS_MARKER, RobotTrip or StressParams, so the existing software / ARM acceptance scope is not expanded. Another PR, [#516](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/516), was closed without merging, with 3 merge commits and 0 files and a tree identical to 997, so it is not recorded as a new console feature. The other official default branches, the official tag / 8 assets, model metadata and blog posts are unchanged; this round did not run tests, enable dev signing / boot experiments, install software, restart services or connect to the robot, and did not change the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image or the existing web console.

### Nov 3: stress dev build syncs with the main branch: base and contract changes, the tool itself unchanged

At 15:18 JST, the [stress-test dev build](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test) was updated to 0.18.3-dev.1543, with the actual tag pinned at [`0c14a1145b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8), [source 0.18.3](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8/Cargo.toml#L82-L85) / [official telemetry API47](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8/duck-ipc-proto/src/lib.rs#L488-L494). **It is still a standalone dev prototype, not a new official release, image or integration into this project.** The [24 commits and 35 files relative to the previous 0c3](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/0c3e9c66a50144d92e02c9a330d226fad0bd90c6...0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8) are 23 already-recorded main-branch commits plus one merge; relative to the current main branch 997 it is 18 commits ahead and 0 behind, 13 files.

Comparing Git blobs one by one, the 10 tool source files of stress (getup / guard / lap / driver, runlog, endurance, monitor, CLI and plotting / tests) are identical to the old dev build. **There is no new stress algorithm, measured sampling or endurance acceptance check.** The new base inherits the already-recorded startup checks, gamepad reconnection, SSH cache, telemetry and simulation fixes; [on-board telemetry is on by default with an opt-out mechanism](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8/updater/src/telemetry.rs#L1-L6), while [duck-sim explicitly sets MICRODUCK_TELEMETRY=0 when launching updaterd](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/0c14a1145bb376b57ec2b3d27184bf363f8ffcc8/scripts/duck-sim). Before adopting a full dev build these inherited items must be reviewed, and the official telemetry 47 cannot be taken as the local 18.2's own 47 or the episode5 footstep 47 contract.

Meanwhile, the [endurance plotting #504](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/504) is still pending merge, with head [`20d4838399`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/20d48383999712401485c7923aaa36ac2ea14141). The [6 commits and 3 files this time](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/f28005581936cb794aaea089708474f8ca24dfab...20d48383999712401485c7923aaa36ac2ea14141) only sync the already-recorded main-branch simulation history, and the Git bytes of endurance-plot and its tests are unchanged; this does not count as a new analysis fix, and the historical 37 Windows results are not relabeled as a re-run on the current source. It and the stress branch's plotting prototype remain separately reviewed sources and were not automatically merged by the tag rebuild.

**Keep it at later-controlled-bench-reference priority.** The HD-1910 still needs checks of current units, joint order, backlash and fall / get-up boundaries; the existing forward ToF approximate guard and the continue-without-readings behavior are unchanged and are not a new safety acceptance check. Camera, ToF pipeline, model management, training / policy weights, action sequences and sound assets have no tool increment this time. The four default branches, official 0.18.3 / 8 assets, the four model metadata entries, the official blog and this project's pinned acceptance records are all unchanged; this round did not run source, tests, plotting or stress commands, and did not change the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image, the upgrade gate or the existing web console.

### Nov 3: SDK follow-up revisions, generic NFC pairing card and side-step / grasp prototypes

**The four official default branches and the official 0.18.3 release / its 8 assets are all unchanged.** The following are pending-merge PRs and two standalone dev releases; they are not a new official image and have not been integrated into this project. The earlier SDK sections below keep the status at the time; this section revises their judgment that "the suggestions are not yet implemented".

| Source and pinned version | Actual change | Impact on the Feetech replica and priority |
|---|---|---|
| **SDK pending merge** [#271](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/271), [`e47a27bf3e`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b); [4 commits and 13 files relative to 757](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/75748cc23dea863df2930c653a39d42bea4156a2...e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b) | Stand-up helper, video error contract, diagnostic entry point and daemon-tag release config. Relative to the current main branch it is 16 commits ahead / 0 behind, 34 files; no new daemon API. | **Medium-priority source reference; before integrating, check control ownership, posture / stop timing and video consumers at high priority.** The existing external 0.18.2 report has no pinned-SHA acceptance of this new source; this project's acceptance scope is not expanded. |
| **NFC dev release** [`nfc-pairing-tag`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-nfc-pairing-tag), [`5d6066b297`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/5d6066b297fda58af6189788202033a666b373b0); 01:29 JST on Nov 3, 0.18.3-dev.1540, source 0.18.3/API47 | [1 commit and 8 files relative to the main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/997808c1d71056d31bd6f05fc5fde848c849d2bc...5d6066b297fda58af6189788202033a666b373b0); the NFC card changes from a specified MAC to a generic pairing marker that discovers gamepads in pairing mode. | **Medium-priority feature reference; before adopting, first check old-card compatibility, the reader and multi-gamepad selection.** Does not include #477/API48 or the standalone pairing-cleanup fix; HD-1910, motors, camera, ToF, models and training have no increment here. |
| **Demo dev release** [`ep5-footsteps-side`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-ep5-footsteps-side), [`6844f912af`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/6844f912af1a2eceb893dce89c0c6e5d497d481e); 07:02 JST on Nov 3, 0.18.0-dev.1542, source still 0.18.0/API47 | [2 commits and 5 files relative to the already-recorded a9f](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/a9f70e5581feabd617cac50186023080d5ff8ed5...6844f912af1a2eceb893dce89c0c6e5d497d481e): sideways stick deflection selects footsteps, and the second grasp accepts target parameters; relative to the current main branch it is 28 commits ahead / 29 behind, 45 files. | **Low-priority experimental reference.** The old demo base inherits the personal Select / shutdown behavior; for Feetech, first check the joint, BAM, foot placement, target network and head command slot contracts; it cannot replace the whole package. |
| **Jump training pending merge** [microduck_rl #86](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/pull/86), [`0b5c5588be`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/commit/0b5c5588be7dfb654aa33072afb8238f62dd90db) | [1 commit and 2 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/compare/9ec2b8cc83991873acf02885a3853b621d117a13...0b5c5588be7dfb654aa33072afb8238f62dd90db) only add module notes and backlash tests: registration, observation bias / soft limits, original config invariance, and selectors such as the actual MJCF's 14 servos / ±1° backlash hinges. | **Medium-priority training reference.** No new reward implementation or weights; the author separately describes 64 envs × 5 iterations and a 61D→14D export without pinned SHAs for the run / model; this round did not run it, and the old 274 tests / 6000 iterations are not relabeled as a re-verification on the current source. |

The SDK's [`stand()`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/sdk/microduck/client.py#L184-L226) first enables, then checks sitting / homed; if still sitting after waiting 3 seconds it sends sit_toggle, retrying at 3-second intervals within a default 10-second loop budget. A missing sitting field only warns and returns, which does not prove the robot is standing; the budget is checked after the RPC returns, so it is not a strict end-to-end network / motion timeout guarantee either. **It does not change the last-writer arbitration under which the gamepad continuously writes twist.** The [README](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/sdk/README.md) adds notes on the gamepad and macOS FFmpeg; generic gaze holding is still left to a later PR, and look itself is still a single RPC.

[`frame()` / Camera](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/sdk/microduck/video.py#L62-L93) changes from returning None when there is no frame to raising VideoError, distinguishing first-frame timeout, ending before the first frame, stopping after frames were received, and closing; a new offer resets the image / counters, and late callbacks from an old connection no longer clear the new image. Consumers need to adapt to the exception; this is connection / track termination handling, **with no new image-freshness watchdog for frames frozen without events, and no fix for the ToF lane limitation.** Diagnostics move to [`python -m microduck [name]`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/sdk/microduck/__main__.py), which only reads policies / skills, and the old wire / rendezvous module entry points are removed.

The [SDK release config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/.github/workflows/sdk-pypi.yml) has switched from sdk-v to daemon-v tags, replacing the [local 0.0.0 placeholder](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/sdk/pyproject.toml) with the workspace Cargo.toml version at build time and checking the tag against the artifact file names; build and OIDC upload remain separate. The [setup document](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/e47a27bf3e5eb59a89088c99caa51398d8e0077b/docs/project/ci-setup.md) still says the pending publisher is not configured. **This is the config implemented within the PR; it does not mean the SDK has been released, nor will it automatically backfill a release for the existing 0.18.3.** The author's [follow-up reply](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/271#issuecomment-6583546501) at 02:19 JST explains the revision; this round did not install or run the SDK or the workflow.

The NFC card must contain a Text / URI record that, after trimming leading/trailing whitespace, equals [`microduck_gamepad_pairing`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5d6066b297fda58af6189788202033a666b373b0/nfc/src/ndef.rs#L173-L181) **exactly and case-sensitively**; old MAC cards no longer trigger. nfcd now sends default PadPairParams without a MAC, leaving the specific candidate to the existing discovery flow; [an already-connected gamepad blocking pairing, one attempt per tap, and the chirp / greet on success](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5d6066b297fda58af6189788202033a666b373b0/nfc/src/pairing.rs#L93-L113) are kept, with no new sound assets.

The side-step switch [`footstep_stick_side`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6844f912af1a2eceb893dce89c0c6e5d497d481e/robotd/src/control.rs#L573-L609) defaults to false, the overall footstep_control is still false, and footstep_stick is still true. Only when the overall footstep feature and model are available, stick is off and side is on, and the normalized sideways ratio is positive and not smaller than the forward / turn ratios, does it use footsteps; exiting still goes through the wait / foot-placement handoff, and the stick-interrupts-goal / pattern check still comes before this decision. The grasp parameter [`ground_grasp_2:dx,dy,c,s`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6844f912af1a2eceb893dce89c0c6e5d497d481e/robotd/src/main.rs#L5442-L5484) requires 4 finite numbers, dx / dy each within ±0.06 m and c / s each within ±1; the source does not enforce the unit circle, the values finally go into the second grasp's four head slots, and a plain call without the suffix uses zeros. **It does not automatically recognize targets from the camera / ToF, and the increment does not provide matching target-conditioned ONNX weights.** The two pure-function test definitions and one ORT regression ignored by default were only reviewed as source; this round did not run them.

The four tracked model metadata entries and the official blog are still unchanged. The local own 18.2/API47 cannot be treated as interchangeable with the official telemetry / NFC 47 or the episode5 footstep 47, and #477's gamepad extension is 48. HD-1910 registers / limits, physical camera / ToF, training model provenance and action / head control ownership must all be accepted independently; the only new sound impact this round is the timing of existing calls. The pinned local 0182 record, production code / test sources and ARM software results remain as they were and are not treated as acceptance of the SDK, NFC, side-step, grasp or jump items above; the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the 27th image, the online migration gate and the existing web console were not changed by this round.

### Nov 3: external real-hardware SDK feedback and release-method suggestions

At 00:54 JST, [a maintainer reported an external real-hardware test on #271](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/271#issuecomment-6581614872): a robot running 0.18.2, accessed from a laptop through the real rendezvous; the report says unit tests, wire queries, watch status, head control, walking once the intent stream stabilized, Ctrl-C stop and the `Duck(video=True)` live camera all worked. **This is someone else's test report, not this project's HD-1910 real-hardware acceptance check.** The comment does not give the specific SHAs of the SDK / firmware tested or the video ICE candidate path, and "through the real rendezvous" does not imply that TURN media relay has been accepted. The PR is still pending merge, the head is still `75748cc23dea863df2930c653a39d42bea4156a2`, and this round saw no source or release progress.

This feedback adds detail on control boundaries: while a gamepad is connected, padd continuously writes twist, including zero values when the stick is centered, so a script's move gets overwritten in the last-writer slot; after the gamepad left, the tested robot sat down, and `enable(True)` does not make a sitting robot stand up. The maintainer suggested adding a `stand()` that first checks `robot.policies.sitting` before acting, or at least documenting this. The [current SDK](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/sdk/microduck/client.py) still has no stand helper, and enable is just a single `robot.enable`; continuous gaze is still planned for a separate PR, implemented by re-sending `robot.head`, with the robot's idle keeping its default behavior. **These suggestions have not been implemented yet; a single external walking result cannot be taken to mean multi-source control ownership, explicit standing up and gaze timing are all solved.**

The maintainer also suggested releasing the SDK alongside the official `daemon-v*` tags, taking the version from the workspace Cargo.toml at build time, and dropping the separate version line. **The actual config is still [SDK 0.1.0](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/sdk/pyproject.toml) and the [`sdk-v*` workflow](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/.github/workflows/sdk-pypi.yml); the release contract did not switch automatically because of the comment.** The same comment also asked to distinguish "never received a frame" from "video has stopped", and mentioned FFmpeg warnings when av / OpenCV are loaded together on macOS and a wire-module pre-import warning; all are follow-up items, and this round did not reproduce or fix them in this project.

**High priority: before integrating the SDK in the future, check gamepad / script arbitration, sitting state, stop budget and camera gaze timing, then evaluate version binding.** The local own 18.2/API47 extension cannot be considered equivalent to this external 0.18.2 report based on version names alone. The camera evidence applies only to the tested device, and ToF being off by default and the cloud lane constraints are unchanged; model management, training, policy weights, action mapping and sound assets have nothing new. The four default branches, official 0.18.3 and its 8 assets, dev tags, model metadata, blog posts and this project's existing acceptance records are all unchanged. This round only read the comment and the pinned source, did not run tests, install the SDK, load models or connect to the robot, and did not change the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image or the online web console.

### Nov 2: Python SDK pending-merge revision: import name, CI and vision example

[#271](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/271), created back in September, is recorded here for the first time; it is still pending merge, with the pinned head now [`75748cc23d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/75748cc23dea863df2930c653a39d42bea4156a2), while the actual SDK revision is still pinned at `5c4360acec`. The [4 commits and 29 files relative to 558 as observed last round](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/55864186ef41887aaf46fb7bc315cda12c90b03f...5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b) are mostly package renaming and reference updates, plus CI / release workflows and a vision example; after the latest sync with the main branch, the whole PR is 12 commits ahead and 0 behind the current main branch, 33 files in total, so not all the existing SDK content counts as new this round. The subsequent 6 commits from 5c→757 only merge in already-recorded main-branch simulation history, and the raw Git bytes of the eight SDK / package / workflow files already read are unchanged.

The [actual package config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/sdk/pyproject.toml) declares the distribution name `microduck`, version 0.1.0, and the import is now `from microduck import Duck`; the SDK modules and vision-demo references were renamed accordingly. The new [CI config](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs the SDK tests on Python 3.10 / 3.15; the [release workflow](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/.github/workflows/sdk-pypi.yml) checks that the `sdk-v*` tag matches the SDK version, builds the sdist / wheel separately, then uploads via OIDC in the `pypi` environment. The [config notes](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/docs/project/ci-setup.md) still say the PyPI pending publisher needs to be set up, and this round saw no `sdk-v` tag on GitHub. **These are pending-merge package and workflow configs, not a released SDK or a passing test record for this project. The maintainer only said they were preparing to test on a robot; there are no real-hardware results for this yet.**

The [new `find_it.py`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/sdk/examples/find_it.py) takes the name of an object to find, has a host-side SmolVLM-256M-Instruct look at the left / right / front views, and calls the existing chirp when it answers yes. The example uses a thread that sleeps 0.5 seconds after each `look()` call, repeating the facing request during model inference; [the SDK's `look()` itself is still a single RPC](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/5c4360acec8b92d92fe9ed8be51f8963c7b3876b/sdk/microduck/client.py). The author left generic gaze holding / re-sending `robot.head` in the SDK as a [later suggestion](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/271#issuecomment-6579225798), so it cannot be recorded as a completed SDK capability. The example's model reference does not pin a revision; this round did not download or load the model or run the example.

It still depends on an HF account and the rendezvous link; the client `move`'s HOLD=1 s and RESEND=0.15 s re-sending and ToF being off by default are original behaviors of this existing proposal, with no new daemon timeout or depth-pipeline fix. **Medium-priority SDK reference; before adopting, first check the stop budget, head control ownership, image timing and the remote field contract.** HD-1910 joint mapping / limits, camera / ToF and control behavior while the model blocks must all be verified independently; the host-side vision example does not update training or robot policy weights, and sound still only calls the existing chirp, with no new audio assets. The pinned native `785254e` has no `sdk/` directory and project `0a61aa5` shows no such direct import; this round did not integrate this PR and does not expand the existing Linux / ORT / ARM software acceptance. The four default branches, official 0.18.3 and its 8 assets, dev tags, model metadata and blog posts are unchanged; the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image and the online web console were not changed.

### Nov 2: new review suggestion on gamepad reconnection scheduling

At 21:49 JST, [a new comment from pierre-rouanet on #477](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/477#issuecomment-6577559077) suggested keeping the manual reconnect entry point but having it wake the existing background reconnection loop and reset the backoff, then having the CLI wait for the status result, to avoid maintaining a second independent paging path. **This is a design suggestion that has not been implemented yet.** The PR is still pending merge, with the head / dev tag still at `d6f77afaa11ea5eb6e88004bbc7fcd0bd211d594`, 0.18.3/API48; there is no new source, dev release or official release.

The pinned source shows that the [background loop](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d6f77afaa11ea5eb6e88004bbc7fcd0bd211d594/configd/src/main.rs#L198-L228) starts with a 12-second sleep interval and doubles up to 120 seconds while the connection stays lost; there is currently no Notify wake-up as suggested. The [background filter](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d6f77afaa11ea5eb6e88004bbc7fcd0bd211d594/configd/src/bluez.rs#L933-L946) only considers paired, trusted, disconnected classic gamepads, skips when a gamepad is already connected, and does not actively page LE devices; [the sweep first tries to take the pairing mutex](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d6f77afaa11ea5eb6e88004bbc7fcd0bd211d594/configd/src/bluez.rs#L728-L764). The [manual path](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d6f77afaa11ea5eb6e88004bbc7fcd0bd211d594/configd/src/bluez.rs#L1093-L1166) holds the same lock, but once it finds the target MAC it hands off to the [flow that restores trust, connects and verifies the target within a shared 15-second budget](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/d6f77afaa11ea5eb6e88004bbc7fcd0bd211d594/configd/src/pad.rs#L629-L719), without the same check for whether another gamepad is already connected. **The existing lock serializes operations, so this cannot be written up as "the two paths will definitely page at the same time"; what needs review is back-to-back repeated paging, backoff bypass and control preemption boundaries.**

The maintainer's concern is that extra paging occupies the air time of the shared Wi-Fi / Bluetooth chip, and suggested considering making the MAC optional or removing it, and dropping `PadConnectPhase` / `connect_existing`; none of these interface changes have entered the current source. If implemented later, target selection, non-preemption, wake-up rate limiting, trust restoration, timeout / failure and the status result must be re-checked; the original exact-MAC contract cannot be carried over just because the API number is still 48. The earlier lack of evidence for positive proactive reconnection has not been filled in by this comment either; Home reconnection and the historical Page Timeout reports keep their original verification scope.

**High priority: decide the scheduling and contract before integrating manual reconnection.** The pinned local `785254e8105d42e8bab2085bdfe04d15635c0f7a` already has 12- to 120-second background reconnection, but neither the protocol nor configd has `PadConnect`, and no second path has been integrated. The HD-1910-related impact is the gamepad control ownership boundary; servos, registers and action mapping are unchanged. There is no new camera / ToF implementation; the shared wireless air-time impact is only a review concern, and this project has not measured throughput or latency; model management, weights, training and sound assets are unchanged. The four default branches, official 0.18.3 and its 8 assets, dev tags, model metadata and blog posts all match the previous baseline. This round only read the comment and the pinned source, and did not test, connect to the robot, or change the 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image or the online web console.

[`0.18.3-dev.1539.d6f77af`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-codex/gamepad-connection) at 20:57 is the dev build of this pending-merge line.

### Nov 2: simulation gait and backlash scene proposal (first record, before merge)

Community PR [#513](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/513) was created at 00:18 JST on Nov 2 and is still pending merge, with pinned head [`6eb8525135`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/6eb8525135abbd7cd9cdc2b468c74ef967e51793). The [two commits and three files relative to the official main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...6eb8525135abbd7cd9cdc2b468c74ef967e51793) only change `AGENTS.md`, the simulation design notes and `scripts/duck-sim`; there is no new daemon, API or model weight release.

The actual change sets the default simulation scene to `backlash` and makes the generated config use `walk=velstand.onnx`, `stand=none`; [policy directory selection](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6eb8525135abbd7cd9cdc2b468c74ef967e51793/scripts/duck-sim#L217-L228) now also checks for `velstand.onnx`. If the own `policies/` next to the repo only has the old alpha files, it is not selected; the script turns to the state directory and, if files are missing, tries the official seed. **A file existing does not mean the policy contract or Feetech compatibility has passed**; before adopting in the future, this project's models and download boundaries must be pinned; this round did not run the script or fetch weights.

The [new statistics for `drive`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/6eb8525135abbd7cd9cdc2b468c74ef967e51793/scripts/duck-sim#L739-L768) read the net XY displacement of the simulator trunk between start and end and accumulate the yaw change of the attitude quaternion; they do not use daemon odometry, but they are also not cumulative distance or signed forward distance. A hint is printed only when `abs(vx)>=0.05` and the displacement is less than a quarter of the requested amount; **it does not return a failing acceptance result and has no separate turning threshold**. "Standing steady" cannot be taken as "walked", and this hint cannot be taken as a complete motion-quality gate.

The author only reports shell checks, cold start and simulated motion on macOS: requesting 0.15 m/s gave about 48 cm in 8 seconds (0.061 m/s), the forward-speed shortfall is still unresolved, and Linux was not tested. The author lists actuator mismatch as the prime suspect, but it is not yet a proven root cause: the [existing body server](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/bae39f5900b318f7e87fc485c30a31213cfa14eb/src/mjlab_microduck/sim/body_server.py#L359-L388) still writes MJCF position control, and these three files do not add a fitted BAM backend. The VelStand in the [training registration](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/bae39f5900b318f7e87fc485c30a31213cfa14eb/src/mjlab_microduck/tasks/__init__.py#L239-L263) uses the allcollisions backlash robot, while the [new default scene](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/blob/bae39f5900b318f7e87fc485c30a31213cfa14eb/src/mjlab_microduck/robot/microduck/scene_backlash.xml#L1-L28) contains the groundcontact backlash robot; belonging to the same backlash model family does not mean the training model, collisions and actuators are identical. **These author measurements are not verification by this project; this tracking did not run simulation or tests.**

The `scripts/duck-sim` of this project's pinned native commit `785254e8105d42e8bab2085bdfe04d15635c0f7a` is still the empty-scene override, alpha_walking/alpha_stand and the old drive, and has not integrated this proposal; the existing Linux, ORT and ARM software acceptance scope is not expanded. **Medium-priority simulation reference; when preparing Linux simulation acceptance, first check the model, backlash, actuators and displacement metric.** The HD-1910 still needs its own dynamics / mass and geometry adaptation; camera and ToF have no new implementation, training code and weights are not updated, the action impact is only the simulation gait selection, and gamepad mapping and sound assets are unchanged. The four default branches, official 0.18.3 and its 8 assets, dev tags, merge status of existing PRs and blog posts are unchanged; the model metadata re-check has recovered, and all four entries match the stored baseline. The 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image and the online web console were not changed by this round.

### Nov 1: gamepad status contract review: NFC pairing may be blocked by a phone connection

[A new review comment on #477](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/477#discussion_r4434936078) points out that if a phone is already paired and keeps a Bluetooth connection, the NFC gamepad tag may be treated as "a gamepad is already connected" and simply ignored. The PR is still pending merge, with the pinned head still `789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2`; **this is a new consumer contract issue, with no new commit, dev build or official release.** The four default branches, the official 0.18.3 release and its 8 assets, tracked dev tags and PR merge status, and blog posts are all unchanged.

The pinned source supports this conditional reasoning: the [configd status list](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2/configd/src/bluez.rs#L936-L944) includes all `paired` devices on the selected adapter; [nfcd takes `status.pads` as is](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2/nfc/src/main.rs#L286-L290) and then [returns as soon as it meets any `connected` entry](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2/nfc/src/pairing.rs#L87-L105). Therefore a paired, connected non-gamepad phone on the same adapter can also block tag pairing and skip the pre-pairing chirp and the greet on success. This is a static review conclusion; **there is no real-hardware reproduction by this project or in this round**, and it cannot be generalized to every phone connection causing a block.

The reviewer suggested excluding `hid_recognized=false` devices; no accepted fix has been seen yet. [The field also has a `None` unknown state](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2/configd/src/pad_hid.rs#L4-L34), depending on whether sysfs can be read fully; matching HID identity also does not mean padd has opened the device. **High priority: before adopting #477, check the status producer and the NFC consumer together**, filling in regression boundaries for "phone connection does not block, a real gamepad is not preempted, sysfs unknown state, explicit-MAC pairing of non-heuristic gamepads", then decide the filter rule. This tracking did not run these tests or modify the implementation.

**This project's pinned native commit `785254e8105d42e8bab2085bdfe04d15635c0f7a` still uses the `paired && is_gamepad()` filter at lines 961–973 of `configd/src/bluez.rs`, matching [the filter in the official stable main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0/configd/src/bluez.rs#L961-L973), and has not integrated #477's "all paired devices" extension.** The local NFC consumer has the same `connected` gate, so if the status contract is migrated later they must be reviewed together; this time we only read the committed source, and the existing software acceptance records were not re-run or expanded. HD-1910 servos / registers, camera, ToF, model management, training and action mapping have no new implementation; the sound impact is only the early return in the NFC flow above and does not involve audio assets. The additional model metadata check still hit the previously reported HTTP 429 rate limit, so the last known baseline is kept and this round does not claim the weights are unchanged on that basis. The 15 version, the standalone candidate, the image and the online web console were not changed by this round.

### Nov 1: pairing fixes and the episode5 footstep demo (checked at 13:16 JST)

**The four official default branches and the official 0.18.3 / eight assets are all unchanged, no new merge of recorded PRs was found; the blog still has 3 posts with the latest on Oct 23, and the four model metadata entries are also unchanged.** The new content this round comes from dev branches and cannot be installed as a new official release.

**Gamepad pairing: [#477](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/477) is still pending merge; the dev tag actually advanced to [`789ec47e00`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2).** The [3 commits and 4 files relative to bcd](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/bcd2f70d3ad56a213bbaff88b433ac1bf1c012b1...789ec47e00d743f0909ba5e71e09b9731f38cac2) only change pairing / discovery logic, the CLI hardware gate, docs and regressions; the [dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-codex/gamepad-connection) published `0.17.9-dev.1534.789ec47` at 06:54 JST on Nov 1, with actual source 0.17.9/API46, 8 commits ahead and 50 behind the official main branch.

- `StartDiscovery` retries only on BlueZ `NotReady` every 100 ms, and the whole discovery-start call takes at most 5 seconds; other errors return immediately, and hung calls are also bounded. This does not change the whole pairing process to 5 seconds.
- After a pairing timeout, both transport paths explicitly request `CancelPairing` and wait up to 2 seconds; failure or timeout is logged, saved associations are not deleted, and the radio fault is not declared recovered on that basis.
- The old pause-btd / power-reset workaround now requires both the `weird-ble` marker and the device tree identifying a Zero3; beta or unknown hardware does not trigger it even if the marker is left behind, and it cannot be turned on via the editable `board.version`. Evaluating Radxa pairing for the Feetech replica is higher priority, but the actual device tree and wireless chip must still be checked; the marker cannot be treated as hardware proof.

3 new test function definitions cover transient / permanent NotReady, hung calls, and the marker and hardware matrix. The author reports configd 66 passing, robotctl 234 passing / 5 ignored, and ARM clippy separately; the author's beta still runs 1533, manually removing the old marker does not mean the new 789 hardware gate has been accepted on a board, and the new Sync pairing still awaits real operation. **These are the author's reports; this tracking did not run tests or operate Bluetooth.** A static check of this project's pinned new candidate `785254e` still lacks these new helpers, and the CLI still only checks the marker; this round did not integrate it. Camera, ToF, models, training, actions and sound implementations have no such increment.

**New [episode5 dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-ep5-footsteps), pinned at [`a9f70e5581`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a9f70e5581feabd617cac50186023080d5ff8ed5).** `0.18.0-dev.1536.a9f70e5` was released at 09:27 JST, with source **0.18.0/API47**. [Relative to the official main branch it has 45 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...a9f70e5581feabd617cac50186023080d5ff8ed5), 26 commits ahead and 24 behind; it combines the existing footsteps with ground-grasp / phase skills, mouth sound accompaniment, fine movement and other experiments into the demo line, so the merged-in historical changes cannot all be reported as new features.

[a9f's new 4-file increment](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a9f70e5581feabd617cac50186023080d5ff8ed5) adds `policy.footstep_stick`, defaulting to true, while the overall switch `footstep_control` still defaults to false. With the stick switch off, the stick continues normal gait when there is no goal / pattern, and goals and patterns can still use footsteps; the existing stick-interrupts-goal / pattern logic comes before this check, **so this does not mean stick preemption is prohibited**. 1 new regression definition, ignored by default and requiring ORT, was added and not run this round; no new model weights were added and this project's models were not switched.

The demo line also inherits the already-recorded **personal Select mapping: short press-and-release restarts the servos, long press for 3 seconds shuts down**; being bundled into the demo package does not make it a recommended default mapping. Its API47 is for the footstep / skill extensions, and it cannot be interchanged by number with official 0.18.3's telemetry 47 or this project's own 18.2 47. **Medium-priority prototype reference; keep the high-priority contract / stop-boundary review before adopting.** Camera tag re-observation, ToF, foot-placement geometry, HD-1910 motion and sound must be verified independently; the dev build, models, training and real hardware were neither run nor integrated this round.
