The records are on a standalone local branch and have not been pushed, so no inaccessible GitHub commit links are fabricated here.

The gamepad learning/shared mapping, in-place camera zoom, SSH guide, CAD32/34 fixes and companion update transaction from published software 2026.10.3101 have been integrated into a new 0182 candidate, while keeping layered config/native validation, model identity, IMU freshness and the final motion gate. This candidate still pins official 0182/e1b49; it does not include the new pairing/episode5 proposals in this section, nor has it merged the telemetry from official 0183.

| Committed verification scope | Result and pinned source |
|---|---|
| Full Linux app suite on canonical Git bytes | Studio 1558 run/1484 passed/74 skipped; voice 181, updater 59, image assembly software 128 passed, source 0680ebb |
| Full canonical native workspace | Rust 1.94.1 serial 2390 passed/0 failed/71 ignored by default, source 785254e; of these, 61 ORT tests passed when run explicitly, 10 manual probes still ignored |
| Real consumers and browser fixtures | Native/Python/Node consumers and mapping contract: 10 passed; new Edge SSH/mapping/camera: 3 passed, upgrade page another 1 test/24 checks passed; the existing web pages were not accessed |
| New ARM build and isolated execution | Canonical d870 source built three programs: robotd, padd, updaterd; real QEMU check, 3 ORT inferences and 26 signed software-only install/restore transactions passed; not a complete native release bundle |

The canonical source archive was compared file by file against Git blobs. Earlier failed attempts (Windows auto line-ending export, 10 ms virtual serial timeout under concurrent load, QEMU host CPU view, etc.) are kept separate from the later correct scope, and historical tests were not relabeled as new reruns. ARM inference used an explicit private ARM CPU info fixture, and the transactions used temporary keys and private service capture; these do not prove dependency compatibility with the original card from the 27th or real robot behavior. **This tracking only reads commits; it does not rerun these checks or repair/restart the environment.**

**Next, prioritize completing 0182-specific native release acceptance, old-card dependency/config/service migration vectors, and a hardware acceptance check on a separate test card.** The native/companion production directory and the 15→18 online migration remain disabled; the 20261027 full-card image, the public 2026.10.3101 software and the online web pages were not changed by this round. Hardware acceptance checks for the Feetech HD-1910, camera/ToF, IMU, model actions and voice/music are still pending, and training and model weights have not been replaced. This round only publishes this tracking summary and JSON; it does not merge, copy or push the standalone migration branch.

### Nov 1: experimental line torque-release sound stop and personal Select mapping (checked 00:28 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.3/API47; the four default branches, official assets and recorded PR states are unchanged.** The [`ground-grasp-x` dev tag](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-ground-grasp-x) actually moved to [`abc903c7dc`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/abc903c7dc0610718756f2e05d421a340fee18c9), publishing `0.17.8-dev.1531.abc903c` at 00:18 JST on Nov 1. The [2 commits / 4 files relative to a5912](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/a5912e80502cd3a02ee37f592931612ad63afc1e...abc903c7dc0610718756f2e05d421a340fee18c9) contain two changes of different nature; [compared with the official main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...abc903c7dc0610718756f2e05d421a340fee18c9) it is still diverged, 11 commits ahead and 64 behind, and the source is still 0.17.8/API44, so it cannot be treated as a 0.18.3 upgrade package.

**The skill sound-stop patch can be referenced on its own.** [`8f9d60d453`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/8f9d60d45356fa5e9921c81063f82a3057817684), after relax successfully releases torque and after the servo reboot handling, first checks whether the controller is busy and whether voice exists, then calls `Sound.stop()`, and then clears the skill state. If relax fails to release torque, that branch is not entered; the reboot path continues to clear state and request a sound stop even if it reports a partial failure. It still only stops the native one-shot audio subprocess, the ride/theremin/chorale exceptions are unchanged, and it cannot be interpreted as stopping all sound or this project's Studio player.

**The Select change is the author's personal setting; the commit is explicitly marked "not for main, not for the PR".** The mapper no longer uses "short tap does nothing, release between 2 and 4 seconds → rest, 4 seconds → shutdown". Instead, releasing before the 3-second threshold sends `robot.rebootMotors` with empty IDs, requesting a torque release and reboot of all servos without sitting down first; holding until the sample reaches 3 seconds sends `robot.shutdown`, going through the sit down/rest/shutdown flow, and the subsequent release does not repeat the request. **The short-press action happens on release, not at the moment of pressing.** It also sets the mapper state to not standing; these are request and source semantics, not yet a hardware guarantee on the HD-1910.

This round only read the complete 4-file patch plus hold, control loop, Sound and pinned local source context. One new mapper regression definition covers short-press release, no action at 2.9 seconds, shutdown at 3 seconds and silence on release after a long press; no regression definitions were added for the relax/reboot sound stop, and no tests, audio or servo operations were run. The previous missed persistence write of `fall_safety` is still in the same editor blob; this change did not fix it or rerun any record.

**Recommend prioritizing the sound-stop patch as a reference; the personal Select mapping should not be part of a default upgrade for now.** The corresponding relax/reboot branches in the pinned local 18.2 native commit `3a696c711b78d63e4e8dcb65420e192bbe588c2e` do not have these two sound-stop calls, and the mapper still keeps 2-to-4-second rest / 4-second shutdown; this round did not integrate it. For the Feetech HD-1910, torque release, bus reboot, gain recovery and accidental button-press boundaries need separate verification. Camera, ToF, model management, training, and model/WAV/CSV assets have no additions; blog and model metadata are unchanged. The published 15-series software, image, standalone migration branch and online web pages remain as they were.

### Oct 31: proposal to block daemon switching during OS upgrades (checked 23:24 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.3/API47; the four default branches, dev tags and assets are unchanged.** New pending-merge [#512](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/512), pinned at [`ae08189e17`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/ae08189e1789dd24da4e80f2ae9060b7e011ea67), [1 commit / 4 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...ae08189e1789dd24da4e80f2ae9060b7e011ea67); version and interface remain 0.18.3/API47, with no new release.

After `os.apply` starts an OS upgrade it releases the engine lock, and the OS flow may reboot the machine later. The proposal makes apply/rollback/select/reset and unattended install query `microduck-osupdate-apply.service` after acquiring the engine lock: active, activating and deactivating are all treated as in progress. **Manual calls return BUSY and ask to retry later; they are not queued until the OS finishes.** The automatic scheduler first records a deferral and re-evaluates at the next check, and the internal install path also rechecks while holding the lock.

This check closes the "check, then start os.apply" window within the same updaterd API path, but it is **not two-way mutual exclusion across all entry points**: the author explicitly leaves a race where the 8090 page does not take the engine lock and can still start an OS upgrade during a daemon switch. A failed status query is treated as not running, so this cannot be summarized as "any unknown state blocks updates". Before adopting it, the OS upgrade unit and system layout must be confirmed; the new check does not mean this project's image already has system OTA capability.

The source adds 2 Unix regression tests that use the presence or absence of a file to make a fake systemctl return active/inactive, covering a manual install being rejected and then recovering, and a forced-version automatic schedule being deferred and then installing; this is not a real OS write or reboot test. The author reports the cases fail when the check is removed. This round only read the complete patch and the lock and status-query context; no tests or systemctl were run, and no software was installed.

**Higher priority if an official OS upgrade unit is present.** A future standalone migration needs to compare entry points, BUSY handling, query failures and the 8090 concurrency boundary. The pinned local 18.2 native commit `3a696c711b78d63e4e8dcb65420e192bbe588c2e` still has the original engine lock but does not yet have this OS-active check in IPC; this round did not integrate it or change its verification records. The proposal adds no HD-1910/BAM, camera, ToF, model management, training, action or sound implementation; model metadata and blog are unchanged, and the 15-series software, image and online web pages remain as they were.

### Oct 31: community policy switch layered-read proposal (checked 22:27 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.3/API47; the four default branches, dev tags and assets are unchanged.** New pending-merge [#510](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/510), pinned at [`7414d4d045`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7414d4d045e82558ed8513d4785741a563b4aa62), [1 commit / 1 file](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...7414d4d045e82558ed8513d4785741a563b4aa62). It only changes the parameter library's reading and regression tests; version and interface remain 0.18.3/API47, with no new weights or release package.

On the official main branch, `community_allowed` previously read only the base file and could miss the user choice written by `robotctl configure`. The proposal reads `/etc/robot/robotd.toml` and `/var/lib/robot/robotd.toml`, with a valid user layer overriding the base; updaterd rereads before every fetch, so a new on/off choice takes effect at the next download decision. For a non-default path, the read function still reads only that file. This is a config consistency fix for the community policy download permission; it does not automatically switch an already running model, nor does it mean the policy has been adapted to the HD-1910.

**The fallback for a bad layer needs separate comparison.** The proposal reuses `layered_text`: when the user-layer TOML is corrupt, that layer is ignored and the base is used, so if the base allows downloads it can still return true. It only takes the merged boolean key and does not perform full Params load validation, so it cannot be summarized as "all bad configs are rejected". One new regression function covers 4 cases: user layer on, user layer off overriding base on, user layer missing, and corrupt-layer fallback. The author reports the new case fails on the old main-branch reader. This round only read the patch, merge logic and download call site; no tests were run and no policy was downloaded.

**This project's standalone 18.2 already has a different implementation; this is not a new integration.** Pinned native commit `3a696c711b78d63e4e8dcb65420e192bbe588c2e` reads the effective layers via `Params::load_layered` and requires `user_layer_rejected` to be empty before allowing community downloads; even if a bad user layer makes startup fall back to a base that allows downloads, the download gate still returns false. The source already has regression definitions for layered selection, invalid `control.hz=0`, and a corrupt user layer withdrawing permission; this round only read them and adds no pass records. **Community policy / user config adaptation is higher priority, but this proposal must not directly override the local semantics of rejecting a bad user layer**; a future merge should first compare paths, overrides and failure behavior.

Model management is only affected through the permission read; training, model weights, HD-1910/BAM, camera, ToF, actions and sound implementations are unchanged, and model metadata and blog are also unchanged. The local migration checkpoints, the published 15-series software and the online web pages remain as they were.

### Oct 31: proposal to fix response queuing in the cloud relay (checked 21:23 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.3/API47; the four default branches and dev tags are unchanged.** New pending-merge [#508](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/508), pinned at [`4a8a6b2909`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/4a8a6b290987f12446b26b91e4e776312d8f078a), [1 commit / 2 files relative to the b2b main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...4a8a6b290987f12446b26b91e4e776312d8f078a); source version and interface remain 0.18.3/API47, with no new release.

The fix targets the JSON-RPC control lane of the official account rendezvous cloud relay: the old code POSTed messages one by one in arrival order, so state/ToF subscriptions could be queued ahead of call responses. The new Outbox collects queued messages before each send; responses with a non-null id take priority and are sent in the order the responses arrived; `robot.state`, `tof.frame` and `head_imu.frame` each keep only the latest pending snapshot, replacing their original queue position. `pad.report`, `update.progress` and rejections without an id are not snapshot-merged, but are still subject to the existing budget of 400 notifications per 60 seconds, so "preserves event order" must not be written as "all events are guaranteed to be delivered".

Limits remain: an in-flight POST cannot be preempted by a response; the original 256-line session channel is only drained between two POSTs, so a long POST can still fill it, and the new Outbox itself has no independent capacity limit. The proposal adds no ToF stream rate parameter and does not change sensor sampling, camera encoding, or LAN gamepad/HID reading.

The author reports that in a fixture with a 50 ms fake POST / 2 ms state stream, the old response took about 4.3 seconds while the new test requires under 1 second; in a state/ToF experiment with real rendezvous plus duck-sim, about 0.5–1.7 seconds, versus a 25-second call timeout on the old main branch. These are the author's simulation and network experiments, not latency on this project's real hardware. The code adds 1 ordering unit test and 1 async relay regression. This round only read the full patch and the Budget/send loop; no tests were run, no relay was connected, and no online web pages were accessed.

**Higher priority when using the official cloud relay and subscribing to state/ToF; lower when using only the LAN.** Before a future adoption, first check slow POSTs, the event budget, queues and disconnect behavior. A static check of this project's standalone 18.2 pinned native commit `3a696c711b78d63e4e8dcb65420e192bbe588c2e` shows it still uses the old arrival-order loop without an Outbox and has not integrated this fix. HD-1910/BAM, model management, training, action and sound implementations are unchanged, and model metadata and blog are also unchanged; migration verification, version 15 and the image remain as they were.

### Oct 31: skill interruption and accompanying-sound experiment (checked 20:26 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.3/API47; the four default branches and PRs are unchanged.** The [ground-grasp-x dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-ground-grasp-x) moved at 19:57 to pinned [`a5912e8050`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a5912e80502cd3a02ee37f592931612ad63afc1e), version `0.17.8-dev.1530`, API44. The [2 commits / 7 files relative to fe9](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/fe9ad02a408cc4578c4bc300ce635bbd9fc5d151...a5912e80502cd3a02ee37f592931612ad63afc1e) add skill interruption behavior; it is still [a fork 9 commits ahead and 64 behind the current main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...a5912e80502cd3a02ee37f592931612ad63afc1e), so this dev build must not be installed as if it were the latest official release.

Skill entries gain an optional `fall_safety`: when unset, it defaults to on for phase skills and stays off for other skills, and an explicit true/false overrides it; the existing roll and kick defaults are unchanged. When the existing fall/pick-up monitoring meets its conditions (running, IMU, model, etc.) and triggers, it ends the protected skill and requests that native one-shot accompanying sound stop. A protected skill must also satisfy `gravity_z > -0.643` (interpreted in code as about 50° of tilt) before samples are fed into the fall predictor; **this is not a hard rule that "beyond 50° it always stops"**. The threshold rationale comes from the author's dance simulations and is not yet an acceptance conclusion for this project's Feetech servos, IMU mounting orientation or real pick-up actions.

Another commit makes the Start/policy stop path call `Sound::stop()` when an action is in progress, fixing the problem of the action stopping while the one-shot accompanying sound keeps playing. That stop function keeps the existing ride/theremin/chorale exceptions, and it is not an interface for stopping this project's standalone web music player. `fall_safety` is a local skill config field: new entries created by the CLI use the default, and the RPC tries to carry over the existing value when constructing the object; no new remote setting field was added. API44 does not mean the config contract is the same as other dev builds with the same number.

**A config write-back check is still needed before adoption.** Static reading found that the [writer](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5912e80502cd3a02ee37f592931612ad63afc1e/robotd-params/src/edit.rs#L396) reconstructs and replaces the skill table of the same name without writing `fall_safety`; the [RPC save path](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a5912e80502cd3a02ee37f592931612ad63afc1e/robotd/src/main.rs#L4915) also calls this writer. So, inferring from the source, re-saving an entry via CLI/RPC may lose a manual override, and after reload it reverts to the phase/non-phase default. This is a source finding that has not been reproduced by running it, so "preserved when constructing the object" must not be written up as "persistence passed".

**Higher-priority reference: interruption and accompanying-sound cleanup for long actions.** The increment includes 2 test functions, of which the controller regression is ignored by default and requires ORT ≥ 1.23. This round only read the full patch and the control/sound channel context; nothing was built, no tests were run, and no actions were tried. Before adoption, first check the monitoring conditions, action exceptions, IMU orientation and consumer contract in the standalone migration line. There are no new weights, CSV, WAV, HD-1910/BAM, camera, ToF or training changes; this project's pinned 18.2 source has not integrated it yet, verification status is unchanged, and the existing version 15 and online web pages were not touched.

### Oct 31: draft fix for the microphone device name (checked 08:16 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.3; the four default branches and dev tags are unchanged.** New draft pending-merge [#506](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/506), actual head [`cbe0a9fd7d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/cbe0a9fd7de96ef53dcd84b90322f84515756a8c), [1 commit / 2 files relative to the b2b main branch](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b2b0d213a402caeac492dd662ea891faa17f67c0...cbe0a9fd7de96ef53dcd84b90322f84515756a8c). The source version is still 0.18.3/API47; there is no new official release or dev release from the same source.

**10:14 status update: #506 is out of draft and ready for review, still not merged.** The pinned source cbe, the author's test notes and the adaptation recommendation are unchanged; there are currently no review comments, discussion or check-run results. This is only a PR stage change, not a feature, release or verification update for this project.

The fix is for the recording device name of the official mic worker: with an explicit `audio.device = "default"`, the old code built `default,0`; the author reports that ALSA rejects that device and the worker restarts repeatedly, and in simulation with `pet_detect` explicitly enabled it also could not hear input for this reason. The new rule appends `,0` only to `hw:`/`plughw:` configs that contain no comma, and uses all other PCM names as-is, covering default, pulse and custom PCMs. The default `plughw:aic3104` still resolves to `plughw:aic3104,0`; the existing default fallback logic when the sound card is missing is unchanged. `pet_detect` is still off by default; this change adds no speech recognition, AI conversation or new sound assets.

**Medium-priority conditional reference: evaluate it if recording fails when using simulation or a custom PCM; lower priority for the default HAT config.** The author reports that on Linux, using a real pet worker and injecting hand claps via PulseAudio, there were no events before the change and Noise events after it, with no events in silence, and reports that fmt/clippy/workspace tests pass; this is the author's software audio experiment, not verification on this project's real hardware or of Chinese voice commands. One new regression function covers 5 PCM names, and an existing test gains a `hw:1` case. This round only read the pinned patch, the parameter parsing context and the tests; nothing was run.

A static check of this project's standalone 18.2 pinned native commit `3a696c711b78d63e4e8dcb65420e192bbe588c2e` shows it still has the old "append `,0` if there is no comma" implementation and **has not integrated this fix**; existing verification status is unchanged. There are no implementation changes to HD-1910/BAM, camera, ToF, model management, training or motion actions; model metadata and blog are also unchanged. The existing version 15, the standalone migration branch and the online web pages were not touched.

### Oct 31: supplementary note on this project's software release (checked 04:02 JST)

**The four official default branches, the 0.18.3 official release, dev tags, model metadata and blog have no new substantive changes.** This section records this project's separately completed [2026.10.31 software release](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/tag/microduck-software-v2026.10.31), published at 03:37 JST, with the tag pinned to [`26150eb614`](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/commit/26150eb61407d3498dc2e2c5d1f9dc3ea130acde). Gamepad mapping / stick and trigger calibration, in-place zoom of the camera card, and the SSH connection guide have been delivered as software; the web console, actual gamepad control and voice buttons share the mapping. The camera change only affects how the image is displayed; it does not change the lens field of view, calibration or capture driver.

**This is not completion of the 18.2 migration, nor a new system image.** The companion robotd recorded in the [release provenance JSON](https://github.com/fanhao375/microduck-replica/releases/download/microduck-software-v2026.10.31/runtime-provenance.json) is still the 0.15.1 series, build suffix `.47`, **API42**; suffix 47 is not API47. padd has its own mapping protocol 1. The historical export commit of the native source tree is `27d7921dbcb280bd7d6ba3e9937624f1e2e61d23`; for build input equivalence see the [committed acceptance record](superpowers/verification/2026-10-31-controller-linux.md). The Cargo version checked at that time was 0.15.1, and it was not rebuilt. The standalone 18.2/API47 record and native commit are still `3b8646835bd56a3c4af01aff6191f88c5ff5425b` and `3a696c711b78d63e4e8dcb65420e192bbe588c2e` respectively, and have not been moved, merged or pushed.

This round checked the metadata of 11 GitHub attachments, and read and verified the size and SHA256 of the small provenance file and the version manifest; the outer software tar is 9,287,680 bytes, SHA256 `5f6a597abe27d9c4af8e137841c9dcab4b6c4b378f44c8f88f4c7b4f6ae09204`. The manifest describes the compressed payload inside the package, so its byte count cannot be compared directly with the outer tar. This round did not download the software/bootstrap archives, verify signatures, install or execute anything; the earlier signature and original 27 updater acceptance conclusions come from the [committed verification record](superpowers/verification/2026-10-31-controller-linux.md).

These records cover 165 Linux control/mapping tests, 13 voice mapping tests, 322 cross-language socket state checks, and real systemd permissions and old-version rollback protection; the service body, hardware input and native file recovery still use corresponding fixtures. **They are not the final Linux regression for standalone 18.2, nor an acceptance check of a physical gamepad, servo e-stop or a complete ARM driver upgrade transaction.** The image from the 27th remains as it was; cards with an existing entry point should follow the [instructions](功能软件升级-20261030.md) to update the software first and then explicitly confirm the companion drivers; actual device installation and readback need separate verification.

Priority: existing 0.15 cards should go through acceptance in stages following the version 31 instructions; 18.2 should continue to complete its own Linux, consumer, ORT, ARM delivery and hardware checklists, and this software release cannot substitute for that. ### Oct 30: limits tool and endurance log analysis (checked 22:15 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.2; the four default branches are unchanged.** New pending-merge [#503](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/503), actual head [`7a4d917362`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7a4d917362a99cd0ee7c718e77a4b5c303dee6b0), [2 commits / 5 files relative to the official release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/e1b49bf20c5efcbe187553e7b2210ec3df2cd788...7a4d917362a99cd0ee7c718e77a4b5c303dee6b0). The same-source [robotctl-set-limits dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-robotctl-set-limits) was published at 21:56, version 0.18.2-dev.1525, interface still 46, and has not entered the official release.

#503 adds a PWM/current limit CLI that writes XL330 EEPROM directly using XL330Controller, DXL2 and 1 Mbaud; **it is not a Feetech HD-1910 implementation**. The code requires explicit confirmation, first sends torque release to the selected joints, then reads the original value, writes and reads back joint by joint, and finally reboots and pings; PWM percent maps to 0–885, and the current code counts 0–1750 mA. Per-joint failures are reported, and successful EEPROM writes are not rolled back. When socket.lock already exists it holds the lock for protection; if the lock file does not exist it continues without creating the lock, so this cannot be summarized as guaranteeing exclusive serial access in all startup scenarios. The author reports fmt/clippy/robotctl tests pass and explicitly says it has not been run on a robot yet; it includes 4 conversion/lock behavior test functions. This round only read the source; these commands and tests were not run. It can be a medium-priority reference as an XL330 bench tool; before adopting for Feetech, the protocol, registers, units and bus ownership must be checked separately and not copied directly.

Another pending-merge [#504](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/504), actual head [`33d0b101d3`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/33d0b101d3dfb2aa647bd2d54ef5d2892d0a55bc), [3 commits / 2 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/e1b49bf20c5efcbe187553e7b2210ec3df2cd788...33d0b101d3dfb2aa647bd2d54ef5d2892d0a55bc), only changes offline endurance plotting and tests. Sessions are identified by t_s/uptime_s going backwards; the metadata start time only assists when supported by sampling evidence such as gaps; wall-clock downtime gaps are kept, and lines/shading are broken across sessions, non-contiguous original rows, backward time and obvious sampling gaps. Active duration is computed from valid adjacent timestamps, missing battery readings are not bridged, zero/negative discharge rates are kept and the runtime estimate is recorded as N/A; throttling is classified as 0, 1 or unknown. Distance resets per session, the fence uses matching metadata, and 0.45 m is only a reference circle; truncation of the 8 curves is also removed.

**The endurance result processing can be a higher-priority reference, but it is still not a new endurance test.** The source validates all inputs before writing plots, but the output itself is not an atomic multi-file transaction; reboot detection still depends on counters and metadata, so missing information may cause missed detection. The battery percentage still comes from a linear voltage mapping, not a charge capacity measurement. It includes 36 unittest functions and still requires NumPy/pandas/Matplotlib, with the minimum Matplotlib raised to 3.9; the author's PR body only lists a py_compile command and does not give full results for the 36 tests. This round read all of the 1042-line plotter and 539-line tests, without compiling, importing, installing dependencies or running anything; there are no changes to the sampler, motion thresholds, camera, ToF, training, model or action/sound implementations.

### Oct 30: usage statistics proposal and skill mouth trajectory (checked 21:08 JST)

**The official release is still 0.18.2; the four default branches are unchanged.** New pending-merge [#502](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/502) and the same-source [telemetry dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-telemetry), actual head [`1f60acc8f2`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/1f60acc8f2ee825f0ec3c256372e178f111345b7), published at 20:21; the [pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/e1b49bf20c5efcbe187553e7b2210ec3df2cd788...1f60acc8f2ee825f0ec3c256372e178f111345b7) is 5 commits, 13 files. It is still based on 0.18.0, 5 commits ahead and 8 behind the current main branch, and **has not entered the official release or our image**.

The proposal makes updaterd send usage statistics to PostHog EU by default: a resettable random ID, daemon/OS version and BUILD_ID, board type/architecture, plus startup, version changes, daily heartbeat, and policy snapshot/switch events. Hub policies are identified by repo namespace, revision and file name, and other manually placed paths are recorded only as local; so the actual fields include a persistent random ID and public repo identifiers, and cannot be summarized as non-identifying pure counts. The source payload does not include logs, Wi-Fi name, robot name, account token, hardware serial number or images/audio, and it sets Geo-IP and person profiles to disabled; server-side retention policy was not verified in this round.

**Before adoption, checking the default choice, fields and switch permissions is higher priority.** A missing telemetry.json defaults to on, a corrupt/unreadable file defaults to off; turning off via `telemetry.set` clears the queue and deletes the ID, turning it back on generates a new ID, and the environment variables `MICRODUCK_TELEMETRY=0` or `DO_NOT_TRACK=1` force it off. The new **API47 telemetry.status/set** are allowed through BLE/WebRTC; the source explicitly allows a reachable LAN client to modify them, without authentication as a mutating operation. This 47 is a different contract from branches with the same number such as phase skills and footstep placement. The queue holds at most 500 items in memory only and attempts sending every minute; active only means the switch and environment allow it, not a delivery confirmation. The static source also shows that turning it off does not cancel HTTP requests already in flight; this round did not reproduce this by running it.

Another [ground-grasp-x dev line](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-ground-grasp-x) moved at 20:28 to [`fe9ad02a40`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/fe9ad02a408cc4578c4bc300ce635bbd9fc5d151); [1 commit / 5 files](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/ade23e7c16abef90f18d06f01f15a0be502b709f...fe9ad02a408cc4578c4bc300ce635bbd9fc5d151) adds `--mouth CSV` / a skill mouth file key: it reads a t,mouth trajectory in seconds/radians, linearly interpolates, holds at both ends, and clamps to the official mouth range of −5° to 30°; while a skill is running and driving, it overrides the original mouth request, hands control back when it ends or stops, and holds the last value during the exit phase. A file failure only warns, and the dance continues without a mouth trajectory; RPC only keeps the existing file key and adds no field for setting the trajectory. **Medium-priority reference for action/sound coordination**; it is still the old 0.17.8/API44, delivers no CSV, WAV or new weights, and has no hardware-tested mouth/music sync or Feetech limit acceptance.

Verification scope: for #502 the author reports workspace software checks, and temporarily replaced updaterd on their own zero3 board to send some events before restoring 0.18.2; the board test happened before the success field was finally removed, and the end-to-end RPC switch, daily heartbeat and policy change events were explicitly not accepted on the board. The mouth change includes parsing/config tests and the source of one ORT test that is ignored by default. Everything in this round was read-only; the downloaded implementation/tests were not run, the statistics endpoint was not called, no image was installed and no device connected; the existing migration code and online web pages were not touched. Other PRs, model metadata, training/HAT/emotion libraries and blog have no substantive changes.

[#474](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/474) has been merged; [relative to 0.18.1](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/7d866d0f864c9b6f19ec6b7762ec2341520db3cc...e1b49bf20c5efcbe187553e7b2210ec3df2cd788) there are 5 commits and 6 files in total, including version preparation. The blobs of the four gamepad feature files are identical to the 14e source read in the previous round; the 17:10 record saying "old branch pending merge" is updated by this section, and the historical source is kept.

The official release includes, after Classic Bluetooth pairing, a 2-second wait each time and up to 3 redials (8-second connection timeout each), plus background reconnection of paired/trusted gamepads with a 12–120-second global backoff; LE does not use this active redial, and it does not grab the connection when a gamepad is online or the pairing window is busy. **Evaluating it for the standalone migration is higher priority, but it is still not a general fix for padd/HID button reading, nor a hardware test by this project.** `ensure_link` can add about 30 seconds of waiting after pairing; the maintainer notes that robotctl has no overall deadline, and other clients such as the web console/BLE still need their wait budgets checked. No quantitative measurements were provided for the radio occupancy/video benefits of the new backoff, and boundaries such as two-way paging and coexistence with phone BLE were not accepted on this basis either. The [maintainer review](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/474#pullrequestreview-5709459991) and the [follow-up note](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/474#issuecomment-6518090938) are each kept for reference.

The program package/bootstrap binary was not downloaded, signatures were not verified, and nothing was installed; the OS image was not certified. The new [PR474 dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-pr-474-pad-reconnect) was published at 17:24, but it is actually still the old [`14e096948d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/14e096948d631356318c779c0e4a3c74b150f162) / 0.17.9 / API44; it is recorded as the pre-merge source and must not be equated with the 0.18.2 bundle.

### Oct 30: Classic Bluetooth gamepad reconnection backoff (checked 17:10 JST)

Pending-merge [#474](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/474) was updated to pinned [`14e096948d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/14e096948d631356318c779c0e4a3c74b150f162); the [increment relative to the old b8f8571](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b8f8571eeebf8152355af18e55ad0269a3c392d1...14e096948d631356318c779c0e4a3c74b150f162) is 1 commit, 4 files, not yet merged or released. **The official release is still 0.18.1; this proposal is still based on the old 0.17.9/API44**, diverging from the current main branch, 2 commits ahead and 41 behind, so it cannot be used as a replacement bundle for the new version.

The background task no longer sleeps a fixed 12 seconds per round: if in a round there are still candidate proxy creation failures, refused connections or timeouts, the next sleep is extended in turn to 24, 48, 96 and at most 120 seconds, to reduce useless paging of powered-off gamepads and occupancy of the shared Bluetooth/Wi-Fi radio. **This is a global scan interval, not a per-gamepad timer or a promise that "it will always reconnect within two minutes"**; the time spent in connection calls is extra. It returns to 12 seconds when there are no candidates, a connection call succeeds, the pairing lock is busy or the outer device query errors; no immediate wake-up that interrupts the sleep was added.

The original filtering boundaries are kept: only paired, trusted, offline Classic Bluetooth gamepads; it does not grab the connection when a paired gamepad is online, skips while the pairing window is busy, and LE does not use this active redial. It also fixes continuing to process other candidates when a single BlueZ proxy fails, adds the MAC to pairing logs, and makes the internal backend methods require explicit implementation. **A successful connection call still does not prove that padd has opened the HID device or read button presses.**

**Medium priority: a reference for gamepad reconnection and radio resource occupancy; the existing pairing issues still need verification on this robot.** The 63 tests and the beta board/8BitDo reports in the PR come from the old commit, and the PR body was not updated with this change; this increment adds no new test functions and provides no board-side, duty cycle or video improvement data for the new backoff strategy. This round only read the pinned source; nothing was rerun, no gamepad paired, no service restarted and no robot connected.

Other PRs, the four default branches, official release/dev tags and assets, model metadata and blog are all unchanged; there are no new camera, ToF, HD-1910/BAM, training, action or sound implementations. The tracking only updated docs; the existing image, migration code and online web pages were not touched.

| **Pending merge [#500](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/500)** · [`34fceb53f5`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/34fceb53f5caa980b78840162a5c5afa38888704), 1 commit 1 file | Only changes `scripts/duck-sim`: switches to the existing `velstand.onnx` without a separate stand, simulation action_scale set to 1.0; when the scene variable is unset it defaults to backlash, and an explicit empty string still uses the original rigid scene. The author reports it fixes the simulation "standing but not walking", with about 0.69 m over 3 drives of 8 seconds; this is their headless macOS simulation experiment, not a test by this project or on Feetech hardware. **Medium priority: reference for launching the official simulation locally.** |
| **Pending merge [#495](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/495)** · [`1757b67008`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/1757b6700840973e7c7c0f0819da6fb712dfcc48), 1 commit 2 files | Adds to the alpha kinematics XML assets 3 approximate head/mouth collision bodies, a lower-jaw body, a 0–0.7 rad jaw hinge and a tip site, plus 2 XML/FK tests. **Medium priority: reference for later head contact modeling**; it does not change the training repo's simulation scene, hook up mouth actuation or deliver a grasping policy. |
| **Pending merge [#496](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/496)** · [`7fa9ffbe0c`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7fa9ffbe0cf7d3f338145948945663a5171c6c0f), 1 commit 1 doc | Adds an ordered entry point for contributors to the docs index: contribution guide, testing, simulation, architecture, development deployment, conventions and troubleshooting; link targets were checked. **Low-priority documentation reference**; no runtime mechanism or build process implementation changes. |

#500 uses the existing official v8 model. The [pinned manifest](https://huggingface.co/pollen-robotics/microduck-policies/resolve/665ae4eb20bfe13bca1ef4ea6d4bb6b4a096e448/manifest.json) confirms that `velstand` occupies the walk slot, with 61-dim observations / 14-dim actions, XL330 and 50 Hz, and the training task is Backlash; the corresponding scene path also exists in the RL default branch. **No new weights or Feetech BAM results were delivered, and the simulation scale=1.0 must not be taken as a setting verified on Feetech hardware.** The script now also decides whether to use a self-provided policy based on whether the local directory has velstand, and otherwise may try to fetch the official set; custom Feetech model names need to be checked separately.

#495 also changes the model contract: by static parsing of the XML, joints go from 14 to 15, with jaw inserted before the right leg; the original camera/ToF site definitions are unchanged, but the right leg joint indices shift. The existing FK parser ignores collision bodies and inertia, and the old MuJoCo fixture fills angles by name and leaves zero for the new jaw, so the old site tests passing does not mean the new mouth contact forces or grasping have been verified. Before adoption, the mapping between model joints, the 14-dim policy actions and the 15 physical servos must be clearly separated, and the Feetech geometry and the newly added 8 g inertia must be checked; this round only parsed text data and did not run a physics simulation.

Training/HAT/emotion libraries, model metadata, earlier PR states and blog are all unchanged; there are no new camera calibration, ToF capture, hardware action or sound implementations. The existing 0.18.1 install and rollback limits remain; the existing image, migration code and online web pages were not touched.

### Oct 30: 0.18.1 official release and current logging (checked 02:00 JST)

**The official [`daemon-v0.18.1`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.18.1) was released at 01:49**; the actual tag is the same as the main branch, [`7d866d0f86`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7d866d0f864c9b6f19ec6b7762ec2341520db3cc), and **API46 is unchanged**. [Relative to 0.18.0](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/a360b3843bb49fd48a635b39eabcb835d9fffecb...7d866d0f864c9b6f19ec6b7762ec2341520db3cc) there are 3 commits and 14 files in total, mainly the boot recovery check from the merged [#494](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/494) and version preparation; [relative to this project's 0.17.9 upstream base](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/96b89d8613f06722c1d64ce75ab0d7124fed9d8f...7d866d0f864c9b6f19ec6b7762ec2341520db3cc) there are 41 commits and 57 files in total. 02:02 addendum: the metadata of 8 GitHub assets and the two small JSON manifests were checked; all point to this commit, and the listed package of 14,842,331 bytes and its SHA256 match the asset metadata. The software package/bootstrap binary was not downloaded, signatures were not verified, and nothing was installed.

| Source and status | Actual change and impact on the Feetech build |
|---|---|
| **Merged in 0.18.1: boot recovery decision** | The timer changes from a single decision 180 seconds after boot to starting after about 1 second and observing every 2 seconds; if the four member services stay active continuously for at least 10 seconds it can pass early, and if any is failed or restarted at least 3 times in the current boot it is immediately handed to the rescue program. `update_gate.boot_deadline_s` defaults to 60 seconds; the script reads both the base and user layers and clamps it to 20–300 seconds. At the deadline it still decides based on failure/restart evidence, and services that were manually disabled or not started will not trigger a rollback merely for not being stable. The config editor shows "takes effect at next boot" and does not restart robotd for it; dry-run now exits with code 3 when a rescue would be due. **Medium priority: reference for the upgrade recovery flow.** |
| **Merged: [Yocto #75](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/pull/75)** · [pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/compare/61c87f158e3db20692d04791bc711e7b29e851d0...f90ba276025257dcf17d94641775c99eae5419e7) | Makes the OS partition health check reuse the dry-run conclusion above, and waits for the expected Wi-Fi/Bluetooth until 60 seconds after boot; it depends on the new exit code contract. 02:06 recheck: the PR was merged into the main branch at 02:01, and the 8 files of the actual [merge commit a7d1468](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/commit/a7d1468736f883ba636b81d342b2923d4dde9cbf) match the pinned patch already read. **Releasing only the daemon does not mean the existing 8090 page and RAUC partition decision have become faster**; the actual OS image containing it must still be verified. The current Armbian build should be evaluated according to its own boot/update mechanism. |
| **Standalone development: stress-test** · [`0c3e9c66a5`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/0c3e9c66a50144d92e02c9a330d226fad0bd90c6), published 01:07 | First imports the already-recorded 0.18.0 main branch, then in 1 commit / 3 files adds per-sample average/peak current for the 15 joints and the peak of simultaneous total current, switching from a 20 Hz subscription to receiving every published control frame; appending to an old CSV keeps the original header, and new columns are not forced into old tables. Plotting adds currents.png, a PDF current page and a summary table. **Medium-priority logging reference; the active fall bench test remains deferred.** It has still not entered 0.18.1, and there are no new endurance tests, gaits or models. |

**Verification scopes need to be kept separate.** The #494 author reports that 25 rescue tests (7 new) and parameter/editor tests pass, and did one read-only dry-run on a beta board that had been running for a long time with the uptime protection threshold raised; the wait loop is not yet covered, and this is not an overall acceptance check of the timer on a fresh boot, fault restarts or rescue rollback. About 25 seconds is the expected decision point on the author's healthy beta board, not our boot time; a service being active may also just mean it is waiting for hardware, so this cannot certify the gamepad, camera or ToF. The script and unit must be copied by the install hook and only work with the new logic at the next boot; if copying the script fails, it only warns and keeps the old copy. If the deadline is set too short, it may still miss an updaterd restart loop that takes about 45 seconds to accumulate enough restarts.

Although the [diff between tags](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/8668d77396218d8009ef8596a6c68edc52145c81...0c3e9c66a50144d92e02c9a330d226fad0bd90c6) of stress-test is 48 commits and 56 files, it mainly imports the earlier main branch; relative to 0.18.1 it still diverges, 17 ahead and 3 behind. The new peaks come from discrete samples of the existing state stream, and the total peak is the maximum of the per-frame sum of absolute currents, not the sum of per-joint peaks or a continuous electrical transient peak. For Feetech, the current units, joint order and missing values need to be checked; this round only read the source and did not run scripts, generate reports or capture hardware data.

The training default branch, HAT, emotion library, model metadata and blog are unchanged, and there are no new camera, ToF, Feetech BAM, action or sound implementations; the base-layer read discrepancy of the old community download switch is still present in the pinned 0.18.1 source. **This project's recorded migration checkpoint is still 0.17.9; this round only maintained docs and did not change the image, migration code or online web pages.**

### Oct 29: label serial number and default pairing PIN proposal (checked 23:08 JST)

New pending-merge [#493](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/493), which actually shares the same source [`52b965eb27`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/52b965eb27ff03e119d70df49bbf09e6ba67cd09) as the [configd-label-pin dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-configd-label-pin); the dev build was published at 22:48 JST. The [pinned diff relative to 0.18.0](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/a360b3843bb49fd48a635b39eabcb835d9fffecb...52b965eb27ff03e119d70df49bbf09e6ba67cd09) has only 1 commit and 3 configd files; **API46 is unchanged, and it has not entered the main branch or the 0.18.0 official release**.

The scheme reads the label serial number from address 0 of the FT24C32A ID EEPROM on the official beta board: the first 18 bytes must be ASCII digits and not all zeros, and the last six digits are then used as the default PIN for pairing the robot with clients such as phones, keeping leading zeros; a PIN already set by the user takes precedence. A missing EEPROM, read failure, blank, all zeros, short data or binary/BCD encoding all fall back to the public default `000000`, with a notice at startup. **A Radxa Zero 3W without this programmed ID EEPROM still uses the original default; this is not a fix for gamepads not reading button presses or for Bluetooth reconnection.**

**Low priority for the current Feetech replica build; check again if using the official beta board.** The PR author is still waiting for the manufacturer to confirm the encoding and whether the weekly numbering resets; if the numbering resets weekly, robots with the same number 10 weeks apart may have duplicate six-digit PINs. In the source, `is_default` actually checks whether the PIN equals `000000`, so a label-derived PIN cannot be categorically described as non-default. The author reports that 61 configd tests pass and clippy is clean, but explicitly states there has been no test on a programmed board yet; this round only read the pinned source and test text, and did not rerun anything or connect a device.

The four default branches, the 0.18.0 official tag and its 8 asset metadata entries, earlier PR states and source, model metadata and blog are all unchanged; the blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23. There are no new camera, ToF, Feetech HD-1910/BAM, model management, training, action or sound implementations. This round only updated the tracking material; no runtime code, existing image, migration branch or online web page was changed.

**Migration priority: evaluate config and model download first, then the camera and the upgrade entry point.** The 0.18.0 differences in old config moves/old program rollback, the Yocto script and hook dependencies, and the camera lens applicability are still as checked in the previous section; the community switch in the [official tag source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/a360b3843bb49fd48a635b39eabcb835d9fffecb/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) also still reads the base file directly, so the discrepancy with the editor writing the user layer remains; this is only a static finding, not reproduced on hardware. **This project's recorded standalone migration checkpoint is still 0.17.9; the official release must not be taken to mean our image has been updated or that it has passed Feetech acceptance.**

`footsteps` is still a standalone dev line: actual head [`1b64c8bf41`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/1b64c8bf412b5b4aed126f513bc03d990ff52276), published at 21:06. It imports the main branch/config editor work and changes `robot.footsteps / robot.footstepGoal` to **API47**, avoiding the main branch OS interface's API46; it diverges from the official tag, 13 commits ahead and 6 behind. **It has not entered the 0.18.0 official release**, nor is it the same as the other API47 of the raw motor diagnostics branch. This change does not modify the footstep planner or tag walking source and delivers no new model; limits such as no visual recorrection in the final segment remain, and there are no new placement accuracy or HD-1910 hardware test results.

The later CLI change only fixes spacing in a prompt. **Higher priority for the Yocto build, low for the current Armbian build**; it adds no dual partitions/update service, and does not mean the phone app or remote web page can already upgrade the whole system. |

**Two points need extra checking before migration.** First, the final config adoption writes the user layer first, and only removes the matching keys after the base file independently validates; an incomplete move can be finished later. When rolling back to an old program that does not recognize the user layer, it will read template values, so the original config cannot be promised to stay the same. If the OS is upgraded first, the daemon can adopt the old config from `robotd.toml.from-slot`, but this depends on the Yocto bundle hook actually leaving the file behind; this round does not certify that hook or that the image has been released. The submitter only reported Radxa Zero3W tests for the earlier 90fcc86 bind-mount version, which cannot cover the latest 7497 or this project's Feetech acceptance check.

Second, `PolicyParams::community_allowed` in the [pinned main branch source](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/52a8e2377aa2ef1ce9d6ae3fdc062d1cc3ab7b36/robotd-params/src/lib.rs) still reads the base path directly, while the new editor writes the user layer. **Static source inference: turning the switch on only in the user layer may still result in downloads being refused**; this round did not reproduce it by running or fix the code. API46 here is the OS extension, and it must not be assumed from the number that it includes the same-numbered dev contracts for Bluetooth, footstep placement, etc.

New pending-merge [#489](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/489) and the same-SHA [config editor dev version](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-configure-tolerates-unknown-keys): during the check it was updated to pinned commit [`6e6111f1ca`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/6e6111f1ca25870ca8d6de1051c5a47be70a4ca0), catching up with the 0.18.0 main branch; 3 files tolerate and keep unknown keys from other branches, and also show a persistent notice for unknown keys in the base file, so the editor does not fail to open and gamepad bindings do not wrongly show defaults; type/syntax and save validation are still kept. It has not been tried on hardware yet and is a **medium-priority reference for branch switching**. [#491](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/491) only proposes upgrading the policy-playground web page's Vite dev dependency to 7.3.7; low priority, not merged. The new head 9ddb47f of training [#51](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/pull/51) only syncs the already-recorded develop checkpoint work; the 26 functional patches compared with the old base are substantively the same, not new visualization/dynamics results; it is still not merged and has no new Feetech weights.

The four corresponding feature dev tags and release pages (camera calibration, community switch, config persistence, OS entry point) have each been independently confirmed 404, and their last pinned sources are moved into history; the features have been merged into the main branch, so **this is not a rollback or an ongoing check failure**. The training default branch, HAT, emotion library, model metadata and blog are unchanged; there are no new ToF, HD-1910/BAM, action or sound implementations. This round only maintained docs; it did not run downloaded implementations/tests, install dev versions, replace models, build images or access the robot or online web pages; the existing 0.15 image and the standalone 17 migration line keep their original acceptance status.

| Pinned source and status | Actual change, impact and priority |
|---|---|

A local WAV can be triggered when a skill starts successfully, and the CLI adds `--phase/--sound/--action-scale`; file edits save the new keys, `robot.setSkill` only keeps existing keys, and these two were not added as writable RPC fields. **Medium-priority reference for action/sound coordination**: it still needs a model trained on phase, Feetech action scaling and sound timing to match; these 5 files include no new model or WAV, and there is no hardware test of audio-motion sync. |
