It includes the source of two regression tests; this round did not run them or generate the PDF. |

#485 reuses the update engine lock and the robot-stationary/remote-session checks when starting write operations; `interrupt_sessions` only skips the session item. `apply` checks the clock, `switch` does not. BLE allows all four interfaces, while the unauthenticated `mediad` channel only allows queries. A missing update script returns `NOT_SUPPORTED(15)`; what is returned is the start/phase status, and when a slow source check exceeds 30 seconds it may also return checking first, so **this is not install success**; after reboot, `last_result` must still be read. The source docs keep the known limitation that "the channel manifest is unsigned and can be pointed at an older but validly signed bundle". The submitter reports stub/component checks and explicitly states that **a complete on-board BLE upgrade and partition switch has not been done yet**; this round did not rerun anything.

API46 here refers to the four-method `os.*` extension and does not mean it includes the same-numbered dev contracts for Bluetooth or `footsteps`.

The two moved tags are still **0.17.8 dev lines** diverged from the main branch, not new official runtime versions; `precision-pad` is still at 614acd6 and is no longer fully identical to the new `ground-grasp-x`. Feetech models, training/BAM, camera and ToF capture were not updated with these increments; the official blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23. This round only read the pinned source and test text and updated docs; it did not run downloaded implementations, install updates, replace models, connect to the robot or change migration code or online web pages.

### Oct 29: ground tag walking revision (checked 01:33 JST)

**In this round only the `footsteps` dev tag has substantive changes; the official release is still 0.17.9, and the merge states of the four default branches and the tracked PRs are unchanged.** The [dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-footsteps) pointed to [`f0034aa996`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/f0034aa99632ac4b8a9a955fd584e8f303c4085d) at 01:03 JST; the [pinned diff from b11a4ad](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/b11a4ad49f43b1eedb21f131c44b2b6088a57fd2...f0034aa99632ac4b8a9a955fd584e8f303c4085d) is 4 commits, 3 source files, with no new weights. This version revises the single-detection flow and the world-coordinate entry limit in the 00:45 record below, and has still not been merged into the main branch/official release.

| Actual change | Impact and verification scope |
|---|---|
| **Detect, approach, re-detect**: `goto tag` makes at most 3 observation rounds, first approaching a staging pose 22 cm behind the footprint, with each approach leg at most 0.8 m; when the staging pose error is under 5 cm and 0.21 rad (about 12°), it sends the footprint target based on the latest observation. When rounds run out it still sends the final target, avoiding the old behavior of stopping behind the footprint while reporting arrival. | This is a limited number of stop-and-look rounds; **the final leg has no visual recorrection or final error measurement**. When rounds run out, the final leg may exceed 22 cm and is not subject to the 0.8 m approach-leg limit above. It is not continuous visual servoing, ToF obstacle avoidance or SLAM navigation. |
| **World-coordinate check fix**: the 1.5 m coordinate norm limit now only checks the target relative to the robot; `odom:true` no longer rejects targets based on distance from the world origin at boot; corresponding regression source was added. | Fixes the admission bug where "after walking far, a tag right next to the robot was also considered too far"; **it does not mean long-distance navigation has passed acceptance**. The stance is now computed from measured joints, IMU and odometry, but the client still hard-codes `Model::alpha()` and a 7 mm forward offset of the foot sole center, so the Feetech mechanical geometry needs to be checked separately. |
| **Observation diagnostics and output contract**: adds `--look-only`, which only reports the tag/footprint position relative to the current foot-sole stance and sends no leg walking target; the head still scans. Fixes arguments being swallowed and keeps negative coordinate parsing. | The normal JSON becomes `tag / miss_m / miss_rad`, where miss is **the error of the last observation relative to the staging pose**, not the footprint placement accuracy after walking. Waiting for the policy to hand back and the success message are also not evidence of physical arrival; the API is still 46, and the client must match the pinned source contract. |

The commit notes record issues on the author's beta robot, such as the old version being off by 10–15 cm after a single observation, losing sight of the tag when approaching the footprint, and staging pose heading readings fluctuating by 3–10°, which explain these revisions; these cannot be carried over as hardware tests for this project or on Feetech, and no quantitative endpoint acceptance for this latest version was found. The source adds regressions for coordinate transforms, argument parsing and target admission; this round only read the diff and test text and did not run the downloaded implementation or tests.

**Adoption priority: medium, kept as a reference for camera and footstep placement research.** For Feetech, first check the actual lens calibration, alpha geometry differences, the companion footstep policy and control ownership, then test visual estimation and actual placement separately. Existing model metadata is unchanged; ToF, training/BAM, sound and action packages were not updated with these 3 files. The official blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23; this project's image, migration branch and online web pages were not touched.

### Oct 29: community models, config persistence and visual target development (checked 00:45 JST)

**The official release is still 0.17.9; the four default branches are unchanged.** The new #482/#483/#484 are all **pending-merge PRs**, and the table below also lists the dev releases. The `footsteps` branch merged #484's code on its own, which does not mean #484 has entered the official main branch. This round did not run downloaded implementations, tests, installers, models or bench commands, nor did it connect to a robot or change the image/migration code or online web pages.

| Pinned source and status | Actual change, impact and adoption priority |
|---|---|
| **Pending merge #482** · [`b60349fee0`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/b60349fee07468be8203ff11a627e1e00d7b9119), [PR](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/482) / [dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-policy-community-opt-in) (00:01) | Defaults to `policy.allow_community=false`, rejecting non-`pollen-robotics` repos in `updaterd`'s `policy.fetch` before the request is sent; the switch is read every time, and changes do not need a restart. **It only governs downloads**: already installed community models can still run, and library path loading and search are not disabled by this switch. After a model actually switches successfully, a vetbook event records the community repo/revision; failed loads are not recorded as having run; once the shared book is public these names are also visible. **Higher priority to check before adopting for model management**; it is not a certification of Feetech model quality/dynamics, and the API number is unchanged. |
| **Pending merge #483** · [`a809897bc2`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/a809897bc2465c88a2dd2298efb55cd663c6b8d8), [PR](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/483) / [dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-config-survives-os-update) (00:27) | The default config is split into the `/etc/robot/robotd.toml` base and the `/var/lib/robot/robotd.toml` user override; tables merge key by key, arrays are replaced as a whole, the editor only writes the user layer, and the gamepad watches for changes in both layers. An invalid user layer is ignored, running on the base and reporting degraded; a bad base still fails. HF credentials move to `/var/lib/robot/hf-token`, with a rollback symlink left at the old path. **Higher-priority reference for config/upgrade and rollback**; it must not be taken to mean our Armbian image has completed the same migration. |
| **Pending merge #484** · [`0ab5d193c1`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/0ab5d193c18cd6ef646882e0e494c727903e87dc), [PR](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/484) / [dev release](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-beta-camera-intrinsics) | Adds family intrinsics for the GC2093 on the official Seeed beta board and its companion lens: at 1280×720, `fx≈695.6, fy≈696.0, cx≈627.5, cy≈363.6`, about 85.2° horizontal and about 55° vertical; the alpha family is about 62°. The author captured 80 views on sky-gtc, RMS 1.28 px, still above their guide's 1 px target; it is not an error-free calibration. The user's own `media.intrinsics` take precedence, and 1080p scales by 1.5×. **Higher priority before adopting for camera geometry/tag targets**: these angles belong to the corresponding lens and readout crop, and cannot be used as general purchasing parameters for any GC2093 module. |

#483 has explicit migration limits: an A/B system must update the daemon first, complete the copy while the old `/etc` still exists, and then update the OS; old edited values that are identical to historical template values cannot be recognized as personal changes, `board.version` is not copied, and logouts after rolling back to the old program are not synced forward. `allow_dev_keys` / `auto_apply` in `updater.toml` are not migrated along with it. The submitter reports 1761 tests passing on macOS with Linux-only NFC excluded, **with no hardware or aarch64 lint acceptance**; the component tests and checks for #482/#484 are also only the submitter's records, and this round did not rerun them.

**The `footsteps` dev version continues to expand**: the actual SHA is now [`b11a4ad49f`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/b11a4ad49f43b1eedb21f131c44b2b6088a57fd2); the [pinned diff from 9195d88](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/9195d88fa8a232daa67d33dde9abeb344276f57d...b11a4ad49f43b1eedb21f131c44b2b6088a57fd2) is 7 commits, 24 files, published at 00:35 JST. This segment **updates the usage conditions in the earlier historical record**:

- The new switch `policy.footstep_control` defaults to off; the editor tries to fetch the two companion models from `apirrone/microduck-footsteps`, and if files are missing it logs this and falls back to the normal gait. `footsteps` / `footstep_planner` can still override paths, and the old `footstep_horizon_s` has been removed. The joystick now **maps deflection to stride length proportionally per axis**, no longer planning with a look-ahead target; the release idle threshold changes from 0.75 seconds to **handing back at the next touchdown after 0.4 seconds**, not an immediate stop.
- Also within the unreleased **API46**, `robot.footstepGoal` is added: it can take a foot position/stance target in the starting stance frame or odometry world coordinates, replanning at each touchdown and handing back after the feet come together. The CLI uses degrees for angles, RPC uses radians. The entry source checks that the target coordinate norm does not exceed 1.5 m, and gives up after more than 70 touchdowns; this cannot be restated as an accepted 1.5 m navigation capability. Both `btd` / `mediad` reject these two bench interfaces. **Being API46 as well does not mean it can be assumed fully identical to the previous version's pattern interface or the Bluetooth API46 contract.**
- Adds a `goto tag ID` client prototype: the head scans the ground, reads raw UYVY camera frames, and computes the target from tag36h11 AprilTag corners, intrinsics/distortion/rotation, camera extrinsics, IMU and odometry, then sends a world-coordinate target. It uses the actual family calibration and full-resolution detection; the printing convention is a 100 mm tag, target stance center 130 mm from the tag, and footprint center 84 mm. **This detects a ground tag first and then walks toward the target; it is not a continuous visual closed loop, ToF obstacle avoidance or general SLAM navigation.**
- The companion [model repo pinned notes](https://huggingface.co/apirrone/microduck-footsteps/blob/19c38fae6afabddf9c0821009028d0a37963f05f/README.md) now list `footsteps.onnx` (61→14) and `footstepnet.onnx` (8→3), plus pattern/tag materials. The tracking policy run is `m09310ur`, iteration 3750, which the author says was tried on the robot; the card's 5–7 mm and 1–2° are **simulation errors**. This round only read metadata and text/JSON; it did not download weights or PDFs, run detection/rendering/training, and there are no Feetech end-to-end tag walking results.

**Source inference before a combined migration**: #482's switch currently reads the base file directly, while #483 writes edits to the user layer, so cross-layer reading must be checked when adopting both; the new footstep switch also fetches `apirrone/*` through `policy.fetch`, so with #482 on top, the community model enable conditions must be made explicit. These branches were not merged or tested in combination in this round. For Feetech, first keep the existing config/model/rollback paths intact, then evaluate calibration, model semantics, stride normalization and action cancellation separately. ToF capture, BAM parameters, sound assets and existing action packages were not updated as a result; the official blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23.

### Oct 28: gamepad precision nudges and paper footstep test (checked JST 23:38)

**The release is still 0.17.9, and the four default branches are unchanged.** A new `precision-pad` dev release appeared, and the `ground-grasp-x` and `footsteps` tags also moved; none of this is an official feature or a Feetech hardware acceptance check. This round we only read the pinned diffs and test sources; we did not install or run any tools (including bench commands), swap models, or connect any devices.

| Dev source | Actual change and adoption conditions |
|---|---|
| **Gamepad precision nudges**: [`precision-pad`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-precision-pad), released JST 22:55, actually [`614acd6eac`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/614acd6eac926931f5bd4ea22ca5910246c89355) | While already in Drive mode, pressing D-pad left enters Precision, and pressing left again returns; other D-pad directions or a long press on Start also exit. The left stick's main axis triggers forward/back/left/right `nudge_*`, and the right stick's horizontal axis triggers a turning nudge; the next deflection above 0.6 is accepted only after the stick returns below 0.3, holding the stick does not repeat, and a stick already deflected on entry does not trigger immediately. A zero-velocity heartbeat is still sent every frame, and what gets called is a separately trained skill; when the model is missing or the skill is busy, the request is rejected per the existing skill rules, and requests made while busy are dropped, not queued. Entering/leaving the mode uses the existing Chirp/Coo cues. **Six separate nudge models are required**; the 1.5cm/7.5° in the source comments is a target description and cannot be taken as this project's precision. |
| **Existing grasp dev tag synced**: [`8df04b3…` → `614acd6…` pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/8df04b35a7ea2a2f7548f3e990eb96f25912a2b0...614acd6eac926931f5bd4ea22ca5910246c89355), released JST 22:54 | `ground-grasp-x` and `precision-pad` **currently point to the same source SHA under different dev release names**. This time only two files, `pad-map/src/mapper.rs` and `padd/src/main.rs`, were added; there is no new grasp model, BAM parameter, or controller grasp logic. It is still the old 0.17.8 dev base, forked from the current main branch, 5 commits ahead / 16 behind, and cannot be taken wholesale as a replacement for the latest main branch. |
| **Paper footstep bench interface**: [`footsteps`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-footsteps) updated to [`2b9ed7dbbc`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/2b9ed7dbbcf1d2cae06e50a05aceda44978ac5d2), released JST 23:03; [this 12-file diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/bd7a3677b887b96cf140b603efee36b449f09172...2b9ed7dbbcf1d2cae06e50a05aceda44978ac5d2) | Adds `robot.footsteps` / `robotctl robot footsteps pattern.json`, which executes 1–64 footstep markers, given in the starting stance frame, one by one, for comparison against the A4 printed pattern described upstream. The entry point checks that the policy is enabled, HOME is complete, and the models are configured; acceptance means the request was queued, **not that the action is complete**. At each touchdown it takes the marker for the next swing foot, stepping in place to re-phase if needed, and hands control back after the last marker touches down (the last step of the upstream-generated pattern takes care of closing the stance); moving the stick cancels the pattern and switches to stick following, and a new pattern can replace the old one. Footstep errors are logged, but the source is still contact odometry and cannot be taken as measurement against an external scale. `btd` and `mediad` explicitly reject this interface; it is meant as a local on-board bench call. |

During a follow-up check, `footsteps` published [`9195d88fa8`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/9195d88fa8a232daa67d33dde9abeb344276f57d) (JST 23:33), a [one-file follow-up diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/2b9ed7dbbcf1d2cae06e50a05aceda44978ac5d2...9195d88fa8a232daa67d33dde9abeb344276f57d): the author reports that the old version still took three extra steps after the physical robot had arrived; the normal flow now **hands control back as soon as the last marker touches down**, the two tail steps are kept only as a fallback when the last marker has not touched down, and the ideal walker's expectations were updated. The old behavior was observed on the author's machine; this round did not verify the physical effect of the new fix, and there are no Feetech footstep accuracy results. The pinned SHA is the reference for the current tag.

**The interface versions need to be looked at separately**: the new `footsteps` changes API45 to **API46 (`robot.footsteps`)**, which is not the same extension as **API46 (status / reconnect to a specific MAC)** in Bluetooth PR #477 under review, and it does not include raw motor diagnostics. The source adds tests for the ideal walker's footstep order / initial stance foot, pose frame tests, and configuration admission tests; this round did not run them, and there are no records of physical paper footstep errors.

**For Feetech: medium priority, kept on a separate research track.** Precision nudges require training for the HD-1910 and checking named skills and gamepad mode arbitration; paper footsteps first require a matching tracking policy and checks of foot sole / IMU frames, odometry, skill preemption, and cancel conditions before a controlled comparison. Neither increment includes new model bytes, camera/ToF, sound assets, or BAM adaptation. The #477 description only further explains the existing software review; the head is unchanged, a positive reconnect to a disconnected gamepad that is confirmed awake and connectable is still unverified, and there is no new deployment. The official blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23; the existing image, migration checkpoint, and online web page were not touched.

### Oct 28: footstep planning dev prototype (checked JST 22:32)

**The release is still 0.17.9, and the four default branches are unchanged.** A new [`daemon-dev-footsteps`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-footsteps) was released at JST 22:29, actual commit [`bd7a3677b8`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/bd7a3677b887b96cf140b603efee36b449f09172). The [pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/6cdfdaad21bed25e01b450c0dd4e0aee0ae19fde...bd7a3677b887b96cf140b603efee36b449f09172) against main `6cdfdaad21…` is 1 commit and 23 files; it has not been merged into main or a release, and it has not entered this project's migration track.

| Content | Actual behavior and limits |
|---|---|
| **Stick-driven footsteps** | Adds a `duck-footstep-control` planning/tracking layer, which first computes a target ahead along the stick direction and then gives a dedicated gait network target foot positions `(dx, dy, dyaw)`; the original twist slot then represents the footstep pose in the stance-foot frame and can no longer be interpreted as a normal velocity command. By default it looks ahead 1 second, plans at most 8 steps per touchdown, and executes the first step. |
| **Two matching models required** | Walk mode only, off by default; the dedicated `policy.footsteps` tracking policy and the `policy.footstep_planner` planner must both be configured, and loading is refused if either is missing. The planner's [pinned model card](https://huggingface.co/apirrone/microduck-footstepnet/blob/1132e672d261cb8520c296acaa06fac955424b14/README.md) gives 8-dim input → 3-dim output; geometry/limits come from ONNX metadata, and it is warmed up on load. The ONNX added in the source diff is only a **planner test fixture**, not delivered Feetech gait weights. |
| **Relies on odometry; exit has timing** | Adds poses for both foot sole centers, with the frame origin 7mm in front of the foot site; a stance-foot anchor switch counts as touchdown, replanning happens only on touchdown, and the target is held during swing. Without odometry it uses the normal gait. After the stick is released, it first closes the stance in place, and after 0.75 seconds idle it hands back to the normal gait at the next touchdown, **which is not the same as stopping immediately on stick release**; on a planning failure it hands back to the normal gait immediately, and body posture and skills take priority. |
| **No sensor-based obstacle avoidance wired in** | The planner library supports one circular obstacle, but the robot's stick path actually passes `None`. No camera or ToF obstacles are connected in this change, and it certainly cannot be described as completed autonomous navigation / real-hardware obstacle avoidance. |

**Verification scope**: the source provides 12 Python reference replay fixtures with 221 steps, with a footstep pose tolerance of `1e-6` for the pure logic; the real ORT planner and controller test cases are ignored by default and need ORT ≥ 1.23 to be enabled separately, with a planner ORT tolerance of `1e-4`. These are upstream source and fixture contents; this round did not run any downloaded code, tests, or models, and we saw no acceptance check that could prove footstep performance on real Feetech hardware. The planning-environment reach rate in the model card also cannot be taken as a physical walking success rate.

**Feetech migration recommendation: medium-priority research reference.** First check the 95mm foot spacing, 54×41mm sole, 7mm frame offset, joint/IMU directions, and contact detection, then prepare a tracking policy adapted to HD-1910 dynamics; separately evaluate control loop timing, action preemption, stick-release/fault handover, and the new model slots. Camera, ToF capture, BAM parameters, sound, and the existing action packs did not get new support with this dev build. The release image, local migration checkpoint, online web page, and robot were not touched; the official blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23.

### Oct 28: weak-network video patch merged into main (checked JST 18:23)

**The release is still 0.17.9.** [#431](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/431) was merged at JST 17:28, and main is now [`6cdfdaad21`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/6cdfdaad21bed25e01b450c0dd4e0aee0ae19fde); [`daemon-dev-main`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-main) also actually points to that commit, released at 17:37. The [pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/5def2f3e66501f0f365d4fb1183020b8ee875443...6cdfdaad21bed25e01b450c0dd4e0aee0ae19fde) against the previous baseline touches only one file, `mediad/src/pipeline.rs`, with 6 added lines (including comments).

- **Actual behavior**: sets `enable-mitigation-modes=downsampled` on `webrtcsink`, so that on a weak network it drops frames and lowers the frame rate while keeping the original resolution, avoiding the problematic path where software scaling produces system-memory frames that then go into `mpph264enc`. Here `downsampled` refers to frame-rate mitigation, not lowering resolution; it does not change the camera field of view, module, or intrinsics.
- **Prerequisites**: the PR's linked [Yocto #59](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/pull/59) was merged on Oct 26, pinned source [`7f5eff804d`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_yocto/commit/7f5eff804d83770bc93d9d65b632a3254504163c). That patch reverts the hard-coded `identity` conversion chain and restores the default `videoconvert ! videoscale`, passing camera buffers through when compatible and keeping encoder bitrate control. Before adopting it, check the local GStreamer plugins and encoder stack; you cannot copy just one parameter and claim the whole-system problem is solved. This is also not a newly released Yocto image this round.
- **Verification limits and priority**: the submitter reports that with Zero3W, the matching system packages, and this patch on 0.17.8, under a 600kbit/s limit it still ran at 1280×720 and about 15fps, with no negotiation errors or kernel anomalies observed, and reports 117 mediad tests passing. **This is verification on their old base, not our own test of the new main branch or a Feetech image.** It is a higher-priority check before real camera and weak-network acceptance checks; this round only read the source and records, and did not run tests, download dev builds, or connect devices.

The dev tags and release pages for `vetbook-gallery-cards` and `mediad-no-downscale` have both been independently re-checked as 404 and moved into the JSON historical sources, keeping the pinned commits [`0f72879447`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/0f728794479a3a2238fa1ee489d8c40a7230b579) / [`b0d9466eb5`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/b0d9466eb5f75b8ee9a9e143592e8d324525b9b6). The corresponding features are still in main; **this does not mean they were reverted, and retired tags are no longer treated as a new failure every round**. Also noted: #471's new head `3e5993082f…` only syncs with existing main, the hashes of its three feature files are identical, and it is still not merged; there is no new stick fix.

The pipeline source at this project's pinned migration checkpoint `c615e500a58b…` does not yet have the above mitigation parameter, and it was not ported this round. The other three default branches, the release, the grasp model / missing v9 status, and the official blog are unchanged. The Feetech bus, ToF, training, model management, actions, and sound were not updated by this one-file change; published content, the separate migration track, and the online web page were not touched.

| **Existing `ground-grasp-x` dev tag moved**: [`01a9074…` → `8df04b3…` pinned diff](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/01a90748823e92295de4220feb4f61ccd0089464...8df04b35a7ea2a2f7548f3e990eb96f25912a2b0), released JST 04:01 | One commit, 7 files, adding an optional `ground_grasp_2`: another network path with its own mouth opening amplitude / closing phase, walk mode only, unavailable without a configured model. It adds a fourth built-in request slot, so the configurable skill mask start moves from 3 to 4 and the upper limit from 29 to 28, which requires the whole interface to match. It is still the **0.17.8 dev line** forked from main (4 ahead, 14 behind since the common base), not the default low-grasp patch above, and not a new release. |

The model author reports a roughly 50-second [hardware demo](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MpWuZ9_dxuk) on one robot, **with no statistics on real-hardware success rate**. The model card's Lego/block/dice/chess piece 3/3 each, battery 1/3, and coin 0/3 are all simulation results; limitations such as target joint angles exceeding the joint range by 0.25rad and leg errors affecting ground clearance are also kept. The training source points to [`666dab6c59`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/commit/666dab6c59c8e388ba2d1fd299b72080f2bc7e68) on a separate `static-grasp` branch, checkpoint 1249, which does not mean the training default branch or the Feetech policy has been updated.

**Recommendation for Feetech**: first wait for the model set to be published and re-check the default files, then evaluate HD-1910 dynamics, mouth angle / zero position, joint ranges, control ownership, and interrupt recovery on the separate migration track; the XL330 weights cannot simply be swapped in. Action/model management is affected; camera, ToF, sound, HAT, and the already-merged Feetech training configuration were not updated by these patches. The official blog still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23. This round only read pinned source, test text, and model metadata; we did not download weights, install dev builds, run tests, connect the robot, or access the existing online web page.

### Oct 28: diagnostics and Bluetooth split, stress test prototype (checked JST 02:02)

**The release is still 0.17.9, and the four default branches have not changed.** #473 and #477 have moved from draft to regular PRs under review, **neither has been merged**, and they now cover motor diagnostics and Bluetooth connection respectively.

| Pinned source and status | Actual diff this round and impact on the Feetech plan |
|---|---|
| **Pending merge #473**: [`9187d5e3d8`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/9187d5e3d8eff92b85c13ba7a82fdd3406e37be0), [PR](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/473) | Raw maintenance is independently aligned with the current main branch and assigned **API47**, without Bluetooth reconnect. New admission checks: if the XL330 Drive Mode is unknown or has `Torque On by Goal Update` (bit3) enabled, entering maintenance is refused, and it is checked again before torque is enabled on the selected servo, to avoid "torque is off but writing a target implicitly enables torque"; EEPROM is not changed. Adds regression tests for refusal / zero writes across all 15 selections and fresh re-checks. **Compare against this first before adopting raw maintenance**; Feetech register semantics need their own adaptation and cannot be copied directly. |
| **Pending merge #477**: [`bcd2f70d3a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/bcd2f70d3ad56a213bbaff88b433ac1bf1c012b1), [PR](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/477) | Reworked into **Bluetooth-only** precise status / reconnect to a specified MAC, API46; the diagnostics implementation, internal session records, and temporary branch CI settings were removed, while connection auth coverage is kept. The docs state that Home is the normal way for an existing gamepad to reconnect, and `pad.connect` does not guarantee remote wake. After confirming disconnection, the submitter got an HCI Page Timeout on one outbound connect, without establishing the success condition "gamepad awake and connectable"; explicit positive reconnect is still unverified. **Higher-priority diagnostic reference; it does not mean the padd button issue is fixed.** |
| **Historical combined dev reference**: [`codex/bluetooth-diagnostics-integration`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-codex/bluetooth-diagnostics-integration), actually `f72c19bf7e…`, released JST 01:17 | Keeps the old Bluetooth + diagnostics API46 combination for reproduction; it is not the latest standalone Bluetooth, and it lacks the new admission checks of diagnostics `9187d5e`. **API47 does not mean it includes the API46 Bluetooth features**: the two branches under review each add their own set of contracts, and the full set of methods, routes, and implementations must be checked. |
| **Dev prototype**: [`stress-test`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-dev-stress-test), actually [`7a72b45038`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/7a72b450386a270f3986518060fa4d33e8de41a3), released JST 01:25 | The version name is **0.17.8-dev**, forked from the current main branch, not a complete increment on the 0.17.9 release. Adds fixed rounds for `robotctl stress`: each round has six deliberate forward/backward falls, walking and turning, and three sit/stand cycles, recording get-up time, failures, battery level, temperature, current, and odometry, and plotting them. Falls are induced by disabling the policy for 0.5 seconds while walking and then re-enabling it; if getting up fails within 7 seconds, the policy is disabled for 2 seconds and another 7 seconds are given, and only if it still fails is torque released and driving stopped, with recording continuing. **Listed only as a reference for a later controlled bench endurance tool, not a new stable gait or a routine startup item.** |

The stress test's odometry box and forward ToF are only limited constraints, and it continues without ToF; falls make the odometry drift, so it cannot be treated as navigation or a reliable physical fence. Before connecting Feetech, check the HOME/enable thresholds, action models, gamepad control ownership, and exit behavior. This round only read the spec, round/get-up/guard code, and test sources, and **did not run the tool (including dry-run), download dev builds, connect the robot, or trigger actions**.

#473's submitter only reports native readback of 15 servos, write-back of original RAM values, torque release, and session checks on earlier diagnostics/combined versions; **the new admission checks and the integration with the current main branch have not been deployed yet, and torque-enable/position response are still not physically verified**. The #477 submitter says their robot is still on the old combined `61f4ad5`, and this round's standalone branch review has no new deployment. Earlier evidence is kept against the corresponding versions and is not converted into acceptance checks for this project.

Camera/ToF capture, model management, training weights, action skills, and sound were not updated with these changes; the stress test only uses existing ToF data and adds no new sensor capability. The [official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23. This time only the tracking doc and JSON were pushed; the existing 0.15 image, the 0.17 migration track, and online services were not changed.

### Oct 27: NFC pairing tag addition (checked JST 15:47)

New runtime **pending-merge PR [#472](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/472)**, created JST 15:18, pinned commit [`98777d776a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/98777d776addf6351e1b0918401c58d3f469b7e3). The release is still 0.17.9; the four default branches, the actual SHAs of tracked dev tags, and earlier PR statuses are all unchanged.

The actual diff only touches `nfc/src/ndef.rs`: NDEF text records choose UTF-8 or UTF-16 based on the status bit; UTF-16 recognizes little/big-endian BOMs, defaults to big-endian without a BOM, and reports an error on an incomplete two-byte unit. Previously everything was decoded as UTF-8, which could prevent a valid Bluetooth pairing address in a tag from being extracted. Adds two pure parsing regressions covering three endianness/BOM combinations, non-BMP characters, and truncated data.

**Relevant as needed: check this first when using NFC stickers to trigger Bluetooth pairing.** This is a fix for parsing NFC tag text into an address; it does not involve Studio's regular Bluetooth pairing, connection recovery, or gamepad button reading, and it adds no new NFC hardware support. The Feetech bus, IMU, camera/ToF, model management and training, action weights, and sound did not change with this patch.

The submitter reports testing the original pure parsing module directly with `rustc` on Windows, with 7 tests passing, among which the pairing regression failed before the fix; the full workspace and physical NFC/Bluetooth pairing were not tested. **This round only read the patch; we did not run the downloaded code, re-run tests, or connect devices.** It was not automatically integrated into the release, this project's image, or the migration runtime; the JSON keeps the source and UTC times. The doc update is based on the latest published content in the main repo and keeps other user changes; the [official blog](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/) still has 3 posts, the latest from Oct 23.

### Oct 26: release 0.17.9 (released JST 20:19)

"Official" here refers to the Microduck project by the original author, **Pollen Robotics**. The release tag [`daemon-v0.17.9`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/releases/tag/daemon-v0.17.9) and `main` at that time are both [`96b89d8613`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/96b89d8613f06722c1d64ce75ab0d7124fed9d8f). We read the [pinned commit diff for 0.17.8 → 0.17.9](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/942e58e3aa6dbb28e7037203fff11866122c7567...96b89d8613f06722c1d64ce75ab0d7124fed9d8f); the scope of this release is as follows:

| Merged item | Change in the release | This project's status and priority |
|---|---|---|
| [#467](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/467) · merged JST 19:59 | Idle head gradually returns to center while looking around; GC2093 exposure is handed to rkaiq, while IMX219 keeps the mediad exposure loop; the official console reconnects when decoding freezes for 1 second after the first frame. | **Medium: migration candidate for camera and head interaction.** Moved from "pending merge" to "included in the official release"; Studio client adaptation and the Feetech hardware acceptance check are still not done, and it was not ported this round. |
| [#468](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/468) · merged JST 20:17 | Adds `robotctl endurance` and a plotting script: randomly schedules walking, standing, and sitting, and by default records battery level/voltage, temperature, CPU frequency/throttling, servo data, and odometry every 10 seconds; writes persistent CSV/metadata, and can query status, stop, or sample only without driving. | **Medium: reference for battery life and heat measurement tooling.** The activity area depends on odometry and parameters and is not treated as a real physical fence; Feetech battery estimation, stop conditions, and control arbitration need separate verification. This round did not start the tool or read from the robot. |
| [#469](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/469) · merged JST 20:19 | Package versions in the workspace and lock file go from 0.17.8 to 0.17.9; the upstream API stays at v44. | A release version change, not a new servo protocol, training weights, or Feetech adaptation. This project's separate migration interface is not automatically downgraded or upgraded because of the version number. |

By default the endurance tool stops active driving and keeps recording when the battery is at or below 2%, following the original `robotd` empty-battery handling; this is a handover condition of the test tool, not a new Studio rule of "no walking on low battery". The code's sample-only mode does not create an action driver; normal mode can run in the background, and arbitration with other control sources such as the gamepad needs to be checked per the adaptation plan. The accompanying script generates timelines, per-activity power/temperature, and multi-run comparison plots from the logs; this round only read the source and did not install dependencies or run the script.

**The batch of input, model, IPC, and upgrade robustness PRs #455–#466 is still unmerged and continues to be tracked separately.** HAT #8, the training PRs, and the earlier emotion / phase-skill dev lines did not automatically enter the release with this publication either. ToF, model import, trained models, sound weights, and HD-1910 support do not appear in this version diff.

Also recording the actual SHAs of the dev tags: `daemon-dev-main` is `96b89d8613f06722c1d64ce75ab0d7124fed9d8f`, and `daemon-dev-prepare-release-0.17.9` is `9897704dfdc998e1908646695264abae5f9d3d30`; these are dev releases, separate from the release tag. This project keeps the published image, online web page, and separate migration branch as they are; neither the upstream release nor the submitters' test records replace this project's real-hardware acceptance check.

The limit is 1 MiB for updaterd and 64 KiB for configd, robotd, and tofd. ### Oct 26: early-morning addition (JST)

The four default branches, the release, and the five tracked dev tags are still unchanged; the substantive new reference this time is the training project's **draft PR [#82](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/pull/82)**, not yet merged. Its pinned commit is [`930dc02c51`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/commit/930dc02c5154f5e0c6a819f99ab6630ea45ac18b), and the PR was created JST Oct 26 01:44.

The actual patch deep-copies the template separately in the four rough-terrain factories for Velocity, SitStand, StandUp, and GroundPick, with VelStand inheriting from Velocity; this prevents registering the demo config from also changing the training config and the shared template to 5×5 random terrain. It also enables curriculum mode for the Velocity/VelStand rough training, while the demo keeps using 5×5 random terrain, and the Backlash variants are also within the regression scope. The template still declares 10×20; the submitter reports that, under the mjlab 1.3.0 curriculum generation rules, walking training actually generates 10×4, while the other three action families keep random generation and return to 10×20.

**Medium priority: check before starting long rough-terrain training.** This changes the training terrain distribution and geometric scale and is relevant to the task configuration of the Feetech training platform; it cannot be taken directly as a verified new gait or moved into the existing image. The submitter provided config regressions and CPU terrain compilation records, and states explicitly that no CUDA training, learning quality evaluation, or real-hardware testing was done; this project only reviewed the patch this round and did not run these tests. Camera, ToF, the model import interface, the action runtime, and the sound pipeline did not change with this PR.

Also recorded: the runtime docs-only PR [#453](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/pull/453), pinned commit [`3d67e43f1b`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/commit/3d67e43f1b04fb6d1b08f8d012999c2ef5986f3c), still not merged: it only limits WebRTC's "authentication and encryption" wording to the remote rendezvous path, and clarifies that the existing LAN path has no such authentication. **Low priority, a documentation correction**; it adds no new authentication mechanism and is not a fix to camera, remote control, or Bluetooth runtime logic. Its single-file change was reviewed in the previous round but not reported as a version update; this round it is filed together with the training change.

| [Official RPI Robot HAT](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/elec_RPI_Robot_HAT) | `main` · [`6de8628329`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/elec_RPI_Robot_HAT/commit/6de86283297e4c49a9da8312cd3395ab83c437e3) | Older production files added on 2026-10-21; this commit is not a new circuit revision |
| [Official emotion prototypes microduck_emotions](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_emotions) | `main` · [`431c73df8a`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_emotions/commit/431c73df8a9d3cb6b6067d09ced6c169fdfa59fd) | The default branch commit is from 2026-10-07 and contains 14 early emotion prototypes; what was added on Oct 24 is the integration in a runtime dev branch |

Dates are shown in JST; the JSON keeps the upstream UTC times. Dev release tags may move, so the actual commit SHAs are recorded as well.

## What the official code currently includes

The following are the main components of the runtime, as a feature map; it does not mean all of it is new this week. See the [official README](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/942e58e3aa6dbb28e7037203fff11866122c7567/README.md) for the entry point.

| Part | Main content | Relation to this project |
|---|---|---|
| `robotd` and policies | Servo bus, control loop, ONNX policies, standing/walking, and action skills | The Feetech bus, IMU, and config interfaces need to keep this project's adaptations |
| `padd` / `netpadd` / `duckctl` | Physical gamepad, LAN UDP gamepad, and computer-side control | Compare against the existing gamepad and web control implementations, protocol item by protocol item |
| `mediad` / `tofd` | Camera WebRTC video, frame metadata, and depth sensor service | Connect to the Studio camera and ToF pages, verifying each on real hardware |
| `configd` / `btd` / `updaterd` | Network setup and identity, BLE communication, signed upgrades, and rollback | Different from the replica image's boot, network, and release mechanisms; evaluate separately |
| `microduck_rl` / simulator / emotion assets | MuJoCo, reinforcement learning training, and ONNX export; simulation environment; action keyframes and sounds | Official policies depend on the corresponding hardware and training config; an action with the same name cannot be assumed to suit the HD-1910 |

## New official showcase: 8×8 ToF mapping

The official Oct 23 [SLAM hardware test post](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/microduck-slam/) describes: mapping with the head's 8×8 ToF and leg odometry, matching the environment when stopped, relocalizing after being picked up and carried away or moved while sitting, and saving the map. The head camera does not take part in this mapping; an external camera and floor markers are used to measure the true position. The post also publishes the measured dataset.

This is a reference for future ToF features. The status recorded this time is "official showcase and data published"; this project still needs to check where the code is released, the hardware model, sensor mounting pose, time delay, and Feetech gait odometry, and this image cannot be labeled as already having SLAM on this basis.

The same day's [foot and IMU odometry post](https://pollen-robotics.com/microduck/blog/microduck-odometry/) is recorded this round: it estimates relative motion from joint positions, IMU attitude, and foot sole contact points; it does not integrate acceleration into position, and on its own it cannot correct the localization jump after being picked up and carried away. Using it in the Feetech setup also requires checking leg geometry, foot sole shape, joint readings, and slippage. The post's publication date is still **Oct 23**, not a new publication this round; the official measured accuracy does not count as this project's accuracy.

## Camera selection notes

For the **Radxa Zero 3W + IMX219 route**, the official [`mediad/src/camera.rs`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/942e58e3aa6dbb28e7037203fff11866122c7567/mediad/src/camera.rs) and [`sensor.rs`](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/blob/942e58e3aa6dbb28e7037203fff11866122c7567/mediad/src/sensor.rs) record: an approximately 3.05 mm M12 lens with an **approximately 62° horizontal field of view**. The mounting rotation angle in the code and the simulated camera's vertical field of view are not this lens selection angle.

When buying, confirm the sensor, horizontal field of view, lens height, module outline, and ribbon cable. After switching to a wide-angle lens, recalibrate the camera intrinsics before using it for visual ranging or localization. The official beta board also has a GC2093 route; do not mix in the IMX219 calibration data.

## How far behind this project's published version is (2026-10-25)

Compared against this project's **20261023c image and the published code in the main repo**. Unpublished experiments in other working trees are not counted as delivered, and unmerged official PRs are not counted as release features.

**Conclusion: the full-robot Feetech motion replica already works end to end; the main gaps are in real perception, interaction, results from self-trained policies, and bridging to the new interfaces.** Without unified acceptance items, tests under the same conditions, and weightings, no credible "percent complete" figure can be given.

| Item | Latest official status | This project's published status | What still needs to be done |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standing, walking, roll-over get-up | Corresponding policies and real-hardware demos exist | Feetech HD-1910 has real footage of walking and getting up after a forward roll; the first-time setup path works end to end | Keep accumulating stability, turning, terrain, and long-duration tests; no same-condition performance comparison between the two yet |
| Network setup, assembly calibration, and web console | Official install, configuration, and control tools exist | Provides the image, hotspot network setup, servo and IMU calibration, gamepad remote control, diagnostics, and a model management page | This is content this project already delivers; new hardware combinations continue to get separate acceptance checks |
| Camera | Real video pipeline, camera intrinsics, and browser video exist; plus recent dev patches | The Studio interface, image, and resource release flow have software tests; the image defaults to a test pattern | Full-robot acceptance check of the real module, frame rate, temperature, weak network, and encoding pipeline |
| ToF and localization | Depth service is implemented; SLAM is an officially published hardware test showcase, release status to be checked separately | The ToF page and service read interface are implemented; real ranging still awaits hardware acceptance | Real ranging, timing and mounting pose correction, odometry, mapping, and relocalization integration |
| Sound and action interaction | Basic sounds exist; skill integration for the 14 emotion prototypes is in a dev branch | The web page has a basic quack entry; the full microphone and emotion sync pipeline has not had a hardware acceptance check in this setup | Keyframe / WAV synchronized playback, control arbitration, and microphone features; Studio continuous remote-control recording and playback is not yet released, and the existing [debug console](../tools/servo-web/README.md) already has pose saving and sequence playback |
| Model management | The release has policy and skill interfaces; phase skills and import dry-run are still in PRs | Supports importing, naming, switching, key binding, deleting, and restoring defaults for fixed action slots | Compare the policy list, observation/action contracts, and API, and adapt to the new skill types |
| Training platform and training results | Public material exists on training, evaluation, export, and real-hardware policies | Our own web training platform has run real GPU short trial runs, evaluation, and ONNX export end to end; the current hardware demo uses a community-trained model | Long training and real-hardware verification of self-trained policies; later upstream tasks and export/release changes need item-by-item evaluation |
| Upgrades and maintenance | Signed releases, health checks, rollback, and computer-side tools exist | Image releases, web settings, diagnostics, and SSH maintenance exist | Compatibility acceptance check of the official new upgrade/rollback and remote protocols with the Feetech setup; the official full package cannot simply overwrite it |

### Source baseline span

| Part | Comparison start → upstream checked version | GitHub comparison result |
|---|---|---|
| Runtime | Official 0.15.1 baseline `1fa84386f0` from the image notes → official `942e58e3aa` (0.17.8) | [239 commits, 207 files changed](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck/compare/1fa84386f07884e27866411bc1ba166977bced95...942e58e3aa6dbb28e7037203fff11866122c7567) |
| Training project | Official baseline `d424a0c899` from the local import record → official `develop` at `b02fdea490` | [75 commits, 159 files changed](https://github.com/pollen-robotics/microduck_rl/compare/d424a0c899f6b33cbd3daeb279913134349c0b63...b02fdea4902c2b93b7d34a83a6f05da4ececb7d3) |

These numbers are **the cumulative upstream changes since the borrowed baseline**, not the number of features this project is missing, nor a line-by-line gap between the published Feetech fork and the official code. This project has layered on the kissqy adaptation and its own patches, and has selectively ported features such as the UDP gamepad; overlaps and differences need to be confirmed item by item. The pinned source of robotd in the current image is `0791fcd44f71c705dc8bb0fbcde405593b52d19b`, see the [image source notes](../software/image/README.md); for the training import and licensing, see [UPSTREAM.md](../software/training/UPSTREAM.md).

Evidence for this project's existing capabilities: [hardware test records](历史更新.md), [Studio features and test scope](../software/studio/README.md), [GPU short trial run and export verification](../software/training-studio/docs/verification.md). These verifications do not replace real-hardware acceptance checks of new features.

## Recommended follow-up order for this project

1. **Real camera pipeline**: image output, frame rate, temperature, weak-network behavior, and resource release; check the video patches above.
2. **Model import and action playback**: compare the import dry-run and phase skill contracts, keeping the web operations and existing Feetech adaptations.
3. **Emotion actions and sound**: first wire up synchronized keyframe and WAV playback, then evaluate the next layer of features such as microphone triggers and imitation.
4. **ToF localization**: first verify real ranging, then study odometry, mapping, and relocalization.

## How to record from now on

- Check for changes against the filed SHAs, releases, dev releases, and PR merge status; read the actual changes, not just commit titles.
- When there is a substantive change, update this doc and the JSON baseline, keeping the date, source, upstream status, impact on this project, and integration status.
- When nothing has changed, do not create an empty commit; a change in the repo's `pushed_at` alone is not taken as a release feature update either.
- Real-hardware verification conclusions follow the [existing records](历史更新.md); official code updates do not automatically change our verification scope.
